# Implementation Status

## Purpose

This file is the durable operational implementation checkpoint for developers and coding agents. It records progress only; approved Requirements, Specifications, ADRs, the relational model, and migrations remain authoritative for product and technical behavior.

Slices 1–16 are closed. Slice 16 — Standard Appointment Duration & Guardian Requestable Times is the latest closed checkpoint. Its original migration, `20260920120000_standard_appointment_duration_and_candidate_times.sql`, delivers Clinic-level standard Appointment duration (`clinics.standard_appointment_duration_minutes`, bounded 10–240 minutes, SPEC-APT-013 BR-57/BR-61/BR-62), the Guardian-facing `get_requestable_appointment_start_times` candidate read (SPEC-APT-014), and forward-only breaking start-only signature changes to `request_appointment` and `reschedule_requested_appointment` that derive the Appointment end from the Clinic's current standard duration (SPEC-APT-001 BR-29–34, SPEC-APT-004 BR-22–24); candidate generation reuses the domain's existing Clinic-timezone/DST-safe civil-time resolution with no new Availability rule and introduces no Veterinarian or Clinic-capacity semantics. A corrective forward-only migration, `20260921093000_candidate_availability_lookup_performance.sql`, was subsequently required and deployed: the approved 30-day mobile candidate window measured ~9.2s locally (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`), caused entirely by a redundant per-candidate Clinic-timezone re-validation against `pg_catalog.pg_timezone_names`; extracting the existing Availability-coverage logic into an internal helper parameterized by the caller's already-validated timezone reduced the same call to ~55ms with byte-identical candidate output and no semantics change. Both migrations, the corresponding mobile candidate-selection UI, and an offline/degraded-network state-handling correction on the Request Appointment screen have been validated on a physical device; see the dedicated Slice 16 section below for full detail.

Slice 15 — Discovery query support closed immediately before Slice 16. Its migration `20260905090000_discovery_query_support.sql` is deployed successfully and adds exactly three new public read functions — `get_published_clinic_location(uuid)`, `discover_nearby_clinics(double precision,double precision,double precision,integer,double precision,uuid)`, and `discover_clinics_in_viewport(double precision,double precision,double precision,double precision,integer,uuid)` — with no new table, view, index, or PostgreSQL extension, and no change to any Slice 1–14 migration. Deterministic reconstruction from all migrations through Slice 15 passed; the rollback-only `discovery_slice15.sql` suite passes cleanly with zero retained rows, including an oversized 101-row fixture proving the server-side 100-row page cap, `has_more`, and exact-cursor continuation with no duplicate or gap for both Nearby and viewport Discovery; and the Slice 13 and Slice 14 regression suites both pass unchanged against the Slice-15-extended schema. A distance-cursor precision defect surfaced during validation — an unrounded Haversine distance could lose enough precision on a JSON round trip to reintroduce the previous page's boundary Clinic — and was corrected by canonical millimeter rounding at the single point where `distance_meters` is computed, before it is used for filtering, ordering, pagination, or serialization. Remote deployment verification confirmed the three functions exist with the expected signatures and `SECURITY DEFINER` / `STABLE` / empty-`search_path` attributes, hold the expected `EXECUTE` grants for `anon` and `authenticated`, and that direct `SELECT` on `clinics`, `clinic_locations`, and `clinic_public_profiles` remains denied to both roles. Discovery is a pure database read/query slice; no live-provider validation applies.

Slice 14 remains deployed, with its `pg_cron` dispatch job provisioned and active and its retained live-provider Communications evidence intentional and immutable under the documented retention rule. Slice 13 remains deployed with zero retained synthetic database rows, zero retained synthetic Storage objects, and no confirmed production defect after its corrective hotfix.

### Slice 5 addendum — Update Pet Profile completion

Not a new slice; Slices 1–15 remained closed and no new slice was opened by this addendum.

- Migration `20260906090000_pet_profile_update_completion.sql` completes SPEC-PET-005, which was approved alongside SPEC-PET-001/004 when Slice 5 closed but was never given a backend implementation there.
- Adds `public.pets.profile_revision bigint not null default 0` (with a `>= 0` check) and the `update_pet_profile(p_pet_id, p_expected_profile_revision, p_name, p_species, p_breed, p_sex, p_date_of_birth, p_approximate_date_of_birth, p_microchip_identifier, p_profile_image_reference, p_behavioral_safety_information) returns bigint` function.
- `get_my_pets()` was dropped and recreated (required because `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` cannot change a `RETURNS TABLE` column set) with one addition, `profile_revision`, and is otherwise unchanged: same columns, same `STABLE`/`SECURITY DEFINER`/empty-`search_path`, same current-Guardianship scoping, same grants.
- Authorization mirrors the existing Pet/Guardianship lock order and non-disclosure precedent: the Pet row is locked `FOR UPDATE` before Guardianship (matching `create_guardianship_invitation`/`accept_guardianship_invitation`), current Guardianship (`effective_start <= clock_timestamp() and effective_end is null`) is re-verified at execution time under lock rather than trusted from request construction, and a nonexistent Pet and an existing Pet the caller does not currently guard raise the identical `42501` / "Current Guardianship of the Pet is required" (mirroring `grant_pet_care_authorization`).
- Optimistic concurrency mirrors `update_draft_clinical_record_entry`'s `p_expected_draft_revision` idiom: a revision mismatch raises `P0001` / "Stale Pet Profile revision" (see the cross-slice corrective addendum below for the SQLSTATE correction history); a material change increments `profile_revision` by exactly one; a semantic no-op (compared with `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` after the same normalization `register_pet` already applies) performs no write and returns the unchanged current revision.
- Update semantics are full-replacement, identical to `register_pet`'s normalization and exact/approximate date-of-birth mutual exclusivity; no email-verification precondition is checked, matching SPEC-PET-005 and the existing ongoing-resource mutation precedents (`grant_pet_care_authorization`, `revoke_pet_care_authorization`, `update_draft_clinical_record_entry`).
- `EXECUTE` on both `update_pet_profile` and the recreated `get_my_pets()` is revoked from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated` and re-granted only to `authenticated`; direct table `UPDATE` on `public.pets` remains denied to `authenticated` (Row Level Security plus no table-level grant), matching Slice 5's original design.
- Deterministic reconstruction from all migrations through this addendum passed locally (`supabase db reset`).
- The rollback-only `supabase/tests/pets_profile_update.sql` suite passes cleanly with zero retained rows, covering: current-Guardian update; a second current Guardian observing the update through `get_my_pets()`; a current Guardian who did not originally register the Pet updating it; a former Guardian rejected; an authenticated call with no resolvable subject rejected (`28000`); an authenticated non-Guardian rejected; a nonexistent Pet and a non-Guardian's existing Pet producing an identical SQLSTATE and message; direct table `UPDATE` denied and non-mutating; blank required `name`/`species` rejected; nullable-field add/change/remove via `null`; exact/approximate date-of-birth mutual exclusivity; a stale revision rejected; the rejected stale attempt leaving prior values untouched; a successful update incrementing the revision by exactly one; a semantic no-op (including whitespace-only differences) leaving the revision unchanged; full-replacement preservation of every resubmitted-but-unchanged field; unrelated Guardianship rows for the Pet remaining byte-identical; and the exact `EXECUTE` ACL matrix for `authenticated`/`anon`/`public`.
- The pre-existing `clinical_attachments_slice13_corrections.sql`, `communications_slice14.sql`, and `discovery_slice15.sql` suites were re-run unchanged against the addendum-extended schema and continue to pass with zero errors.
- This migration is deployed to the linked development Supabase project, in addition to the local reconstruction and regression-suite validation described above. Its remote behavior has been physically validated through the mobile Milestone D flow — register Pet → list Pet → open detail → edit Pet → reload confirmed server state — and optimistic-concurrency handling was validated through a deliberately stale `profile_revision`.
- No Specification, ADR, or mobile application file was modified in this pass.

### Cross-slice corrective addendum — PostgREST retry-safe application errors

Not a new slice; Slices 1–15 remained closed at the time of this addendum, and it did not open a new slice or change roadmap numbering. It is documented separately from the Slice 5 addendum above, rather than attached to it in the slice table, because it also corrects Slice 12 (Clinical Care) functions, not only Pet Profile.

- A subsequent forward-only corrective migration, `20260917120000_postgrest_conflict_status_correction.sql`, is deployed to the linked development Supabase project.
- It corrects application-level manually-raised SQLSTATE `40001` values that are unsafe under PostgREST 14, which automatically retries a `40001` response on the assumption it always denotes a genuine, transient SERIALIZABLE-isolation conflict. This project never runs any transaction at SERIALIZABLE isolation, so every `40001` this system could produce was one of the manually-raised ones below, and none of them is safe to retry.
- Final deployed corrections, with every human-readable message preserved unchanged:
  - `update_pet_profile` stale Pet Profile revision: old custom SQLSTATE `40001` → ordinary `RAISE EXCEPTION` → `P0001` ("Stale Pet Profile revision").
  - `update_draft_clinical_record_entry` stale Draft revision: `40001` → `P0001`.
  - `finalize_clinical_record_entry` stale Draft revision: `40001` → `P0001`.
  - `revoke_care_authorization_after_guardianship_lock` defensive impossible-state assertion: `40001` → `55000`, matching this project's existing "should never happen" assertion convention.
  - `slice12_view_clinical_record_entry` client-reachable revalidation failure: `40001` → `P0001`.
- No authorization, locking order, revision logic, grants, or product semantics changed anywhere in this migration; only the SQLSTATE each function raises changed.
- A database-wide source inspection after the corrective migration confirmed zero manually-raised/custom `40001` occurrences remain in any application function. (This does not state that PostgreSQL can never generate a genuine `40001`; it is restricted to the verified fact that no application function in the current database manually raises `40001` anymore.)
- Runtime validation: the corrective migration was deployed to the linked development project, which was then restarted to clear already-running PostgREST retry-loop transactions. The stale Pet Profile mobile test was repeated afterward: the RPC returned normally instead of retrying indefinitely, the mobile Save spinner terminated, the localized stale-edit state appeared, and Reload current data successfully refreshed the Pet and current revision. The Milestone D end-to-end Pet workflow passed.
- The pre-existing `pets_profile_update.sql`, `clinical_attachments_slice13_corrections.sql`, `communications_slice14.sql`, and `discovery_slice15.sql` regression suites, and mobile typecheck/lint/format/tests where applicable, were re-run against the corrective-migration-extended schema and continue to pass.

### Slice 13 addendum — Guardian Medical History Provenance Completion

Not a new slice; Slices 1–16 remained closed and no new slice was opened by this addendum.

- Migration `20260921160000_guardian_medical_history_provenance_names.sql` closes a gap found while designing the Guardian-facing mobile clinical-history screen against SPEC-MED-005 BR-5/BR-7: the only previously Guardian-reachable Clinic-name surface (`published_clinic_public_profiles`) is gated on `clinics.is_published`, which contradicted BR-29 ("Unpublishing a Clinic must not remove its historical clinical contributions... Public Clinic Discovery and historical clinical provenance are separate concerns"), and no Guardian-reachable Veterinary Organization-name surface existed at all.
- Adds exactly two fields, `clinic_display_name` and `organization_display_name`, to each Entry already returned by `view_guardian_pet_medical_history`. The Slice 12 base function and the Slice 13 attachment-decoration wrapper are unchanged, only renamed one layer deeper (`slice13_view_guardian_pet_medical_history`, itself wrapping the untouched `slice12_view_guardian_pet_medical_history`); a new private `decorate_guardian_medical_history_provenance_names` function composes that unchanged result with the two provenance names, resolved from `clinic_public_profiles.display_name` (not the `is_published`-gated Discovery view) and `veterinary_organizations.display_name`, both reachable internally from the `clinic_id`/`organization_id` the payload already carried.
- No new public lookup surface, view, or RPC was created: the two names are embedded only inside the already-Guardianship-authorized payload, gated by the exact same current-Guardianship check as before. Direct `SELECT` on `clinic_public_profiles` and `veterinary_organizations` remains denied to `anon`/`authenticated`, verified live against the reset local schema. No Veterinarian personal name or `public.user_profiles` data was added; that remains explicitly out of scope.
- Authorization, DENIED/audit behavior, pagination/cursor semantics, finalized-only filtering, Amendments, and Attachment decoration are unchanged — none of that code path was modified, only wrapped one layer further out.
- Deterministic reconstruction from all migrations through this addendum passed locally (`supabase db reset`).
- The rollback-only `supabase/tests/guardian_medical_history_provenance_names.sql` suite passes cleanly with zero retained rows, covering: permitted Clinic display name; permitted Organization display name; Clinic display name surviving Clinic unpublication; a DENIED caller receiving only `{"status":"DENIED"}` with no provenance leak; pagination/cursor unchanged across two Entries; Amendment content unchanged; and Attachment decoration unchanged.
- The pre-existing `clinical_attachments_slice13_corrections.sql`, `communications_slice14.sql`, `discovery_slice15.sql`, `pets_profile_update.sql`, and `standard_appointment_duration_and_candidate_times_slice16.sql` suites were re-run unchanged against the addendum-extended schema and continue to pass.
- This migration is deployed to the linked development Supabase project. A development-only clinical fixture (a Clinical Encounter, finalized Entries, a Clinical Amendment, and one real PDF Attachment) was created manually against that project through the approved trusted RPC / `clinical-attachments` Edge Function workflow to support physical validation; it is fixture data only and was not turned into a migration.
- Mobile: the Guardian-facing clinical-history experience this payload supports is implemented and physically validated. Pet Detail has a "Historial clínico" / "Clinical history" entry point to `pets/[id]/medical-history`, a single paginated screen (no independent per-Entry route, no deep link by Clinical Record Entry id) with expandable finalized-Entry cards showing localized category (all 10 `clinical_record_entries` categories plus a neutral no-category fallback), clinical/event date, and the Clinic/Organization provenance names this addendum adds — Veterinarian personal names are never shown. Expanding a card reveals full clinical content, Amendments (the section is omitted entirely when none exist), and Attachment metadata with a Guardian "Open" action that requests a fresh signed download URL on every tap (never cached) and opens it via the already-installed `expo-web-browser`. The typed `DENIED` domain state, an empty-history state, and keyset "Load more" pagination (the server's own cursor only, no offset) are each implemented as distinct, non-error states. Physical-device validation on Android covered the development fixture end-to-end, including a real professional-initiated PDF Attachment (Storage upload → `phase1-pdf-v1` validation → finalization) appearing in the Guardian's history and opening successfully via its signed URL, `DENIED` verified at the backend/domain level against another Guardian's Pet, an empty-history Pet, and both Spanish and English locales. No new native dependency was added and no new EAS development build was required.

### Slice 14 addendum — Guardian Mobile Push Notifications and Push Payload `pet_id` Correction

Not a new slice; Slices 1–16 remained closed and no new slice was opened by this addendum.

- Migration `20260921170000_communications_push_navigation_pet_id.sql` is a forward-only `create or replace function` of `get_communication_delivery_context(uuid, uuid)`; `20260901120000_communications.sql` and every other Slice 14 migration are unchanged. The only change is one added key, `pet_id`, in the function's existing `APPOINTMENT_*` branch, sourced from the `pets` row that branch already joins (`join public.pets as pet on pet.id = appointment.pet_id`) to resolve `pet_name` — no new join, no new query, no new backend lookup.
- `supabase/functions/_shared/communications/templates.ts`'s `DeliveryContext` and `renderPush` were updated to require and forward `pet_id` in the push `data` payload (`{ route: 'appointment', appointment_id, pet_id }`), alongside the `communications` Edge Function's Deno test suite, which now asserts `pet_id` is present and that a missing `pet_id` is rejected.
- Rationale: the Guardian appointment-detail mobile route is Pet-scoped (`get_pet_appointments(p_pet_id)`); there is no Guardian-scoped "get one Appointment by id" read. Before this correction, the mobile push handler had to fetch every currently-guarded Pet's Appointments to find which one owned a tapped notification's `appointment_id`. `pet_id` removes that client-side fan-out entirely; the mobile fan-out resolver was deleted rather than kept as unused code.
- Privacy: `pet_id` is a plain domain identifier, not a name or medical content, and is not covered by SPEC-COM-002 BR-26/BR-27 or the Communications Operational Policy's push-content-privacy exclusion list (Appointment reason, diagnosis, medical history, Clinical Attachments, Care Authorization internals, recipient email, provider token) — the same category as the `appointment_id` this payload already carried. SPEC-COM-002 BR-28 continues to hold unchanged: the navigation reference does not itself grant authorization; the mobile Appointment-detail screen's existing fresh authoritative read (`get_pet_appointments`, re-verifying current Guardianship server-side) is unchanged and remains the actual authorization boundary. The exact push `data` payload shape is implementation detail, not Spec-fixed (SPEC-COM-003 §15 explicitly places "notification template implementation" out of scope).
- Deterministic reconstruction from all migrations through this addendum passed locally (`supabase db reset`). The existing rollback-only `communications_slice14.sql` suite — extended with one added assertion that the already-exercised happy-path Push delivery context now also exposes the expected `pet_id` — and the other five existing rollback-only suites (`clinical_attachments_slice13_corrections.sql`, `discovery_slice15.sql`, `guardian_medical_history_provenance_names.sql`, `pets_profile_update.sql`, `standard_appointment_duration_and_candidate_times_slice16.sql`) all continue to pass unchanged against the addendum-extended schema. Deno `fmt`, `lint`, and `check` for the `communications` and `communications-dispatch` Edge Functions and the shared `templates.ts` module, and the Communications Deno test suite (10/10), all pass.
- This migration and the corresponding `communications-dispatch` Edge Function update (the production dispatcher that imports the updated shared `templates.ts`) are deployed to the linked development Supabase project.
- Mobile: production Push Registration is implemented and wired into the authenticated session lifecycle (`src/lib/session-provider.tsx`, `src/hooks/use-push-notifications.ts`) rather than remaining a manual test harness. Scope, matching REQ-COM-002/REQ-COM-003 and SPEC-COM-002/SPEC-COM-003:
  - a per-installation UUID (`src/lib/installation-id.ts`) generated once and persisted via `expo-secure-store`, using `expo-modules-core`'s native `uuid.v4()` (already a transitive native dependency of every installed Expo module, now declared explicitly in `package.json`) rather than a non-cryptographic fallback or a new native dependency;
  - permission request gated on a real authenticated session (never before authentication, never re-prompting after denial) and registration through the already-deployed `communications` Edge Function (`register-push`/`disable-push`, `src/data/push.ts`);
  - Expo push token rotation handling, an Android default notification channel, and foreground notification display;
  - notification tap-to-navigate for the live/background path and for cold start (a dedicated pure state machine, `src/lib/push-response-consumer.ts`, holds a validated navigation target pending until session bootstrap completes, so a cold-start tap is never lost to the session-restoration race — see below);
  - direct navigation to the Guardian Appointment-detail route using the payload's `appointment_id` and `pet_id` (`src/lib/push-notification-payload.ts` strictly validates both as UUIDs and safely ignores anything malformed or unrecognized), with no client-side fan-out and no change to the destination screen's existing fresh authoritative `get_pet_appointments` read;
  - best-effort Push Registration disable before Supabase sign-out (`disableCurrentPushRegistrations`), and single-flight/generation-guarded registration concurrency (`src/lib/push-registration.ts`, `src/lib/push-registration-state.ts`) so overlapping registration attempts (the permission effect and Expo's own token-rotation listener, which its SDK documents can itself be triggered by fetching a token) cannot leave a later registration silently un-disabled at sign-out.
  - Two defects were found and fixed during physical validation before closure, both covered by unit tests: a cold-start notification tap could be consumed (and the native response cleared) before session bootstrap finished, discarding the navigation target; and an in-flight registration attempt racing sign-out could complete after the tracked "current" registration was already disabled, leaving an undisabled orphaned row. Both are detailed in the corresponding fix reports and covered by `push-response-consumer.test.ts` and `push-registration-lifecycle.test.ts` respectively.
  - Mobile typecheck, lint, format, and Jest (24 suites, 296 tests) pass. No new native dependency was added for any implemented behavior (`expo-notifications` and its config plugin were already present from an earlier pass; `expo-modules-core` was already present transitively and is now only declared explicitly) and no new EAS development build is required.
- Physical validation (Android, real device, against the deployed linked development project — including the deployed `20260921170000_communications_push_navigation_pet_id.sql` and `communications-dispatch` update): permission-granted path; an active Push Registration confirmed in the backend; a real, remotely-generated Appointment cancellation producing both email and push delivery; a background notification tap navigating to the correct Appointment using the deployed `appointment_id + pet_id` payload, with a fresh authoritative read reflecting current state; a cold-start notification tap after full app termination, also using the deployed `appointment_id + pet_id` payload (initially defective, fixed, retested and passing); sign-out Push Registration cleanup (initially defective under a registration/logout race, fixed, retested and passing); and a same-installation User A → User B handoff confirming User A's registration was invalidated, User B's registration was active, the same installation id was correctly reused, and no simultaneous active registration existed for both Users. iOS push provider provisioning and physical validation, airplane-mode physical resilience, and UI-DENIED scenarios unrelated to push were not performed and are not claimed; airplane-mode/offline handling is covered only at the unit-test level (silent, non-blocking failure).
- Known non-blocking follow-ups, none of which reopen this Slice or any Specification: iOS push provider provisioning and physical validation remain future work; the push body remains Spanish-only in the existing backend template (unchanged by this addendum — SPEC-COM-002 BR-30 permits, but does not require, localization); Communication preferences remain REQ-COM-004, P2/Later.

### Slice 15 — Discovery query support

CLOSED.

- Migration `20260905090000_discovery_query_support.sql` deployed successfully. No prior Slice 1–14 migration was modified; no new table, view, index, or PostgreSQL extension was introduced.
- Adds exactly three new public read functions, each `SECURITY DEFINER`, `STABLE`, with empty `search_path`, executable by both `anon` and `authenticated`, reusing existing Clinic/Location/public-profile data with no new persisted Discovery entity:
  - `get_published_clinic_location(uuid)` — single-Clinic public location lookup; a nonexistent and an unpublished Clinic both return zero rows, with no distinguishing behavior.
  - `discover_nearby_clinics(...)` — bounded public Nearby Clinic Discovery; the search radius is a required caller input with only a technical great-circle upper bound (`pi()` times the mean Earth radius), not a product-defined default or maximum; results are ordered nearest-first with deterministic keyset pagination.
  - `discover_clinics_in_viewport(...)` — bounded public map/viewport Clinic Discovery with inclusive bounds and deterministic keyset pagination; an antimeridian-crossing viewport (`min_longitude` greater than `max_longitude`) is rejected as a documented Phase 1 API limitation, not implemented as wrap-around behavior.
- Publication eligibility for every Discovery read remains controlled solely by `clinics.is_published`; no second discoverability flag was introduced.
- Appointment/Availability-time Discovery, service/specialty filtering, and any Subscription/Entitlement gate remain explicitly out of scope, consistent with SPEC-DIS-001–003 and SPEC-SUB-003 BR-42.
- Base tables (`clinics`, `clinic_locations`, `clinic_public_profiles`) remain private: direct `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` stays denied to `anon` and `authenticated`, and Row Level Security stays enabled, unchanged from Slices 4 and 6.
- Deterministic runtime validation: local reconstruction from all migrations through Slice 15 passed; `discovery_slice15.sql` passed cleanly and rollback-only, covering published/unpublished/nonexistent/no-location lookup behavior, anon/authenticated callable parity, radius boundary inclusion and outside-radius exclusion using the function's own reported distance, near-antipodal Haversine numerical safety, the great-circle radius bound, deterministic equal-distance tie ordering, real cursor round-trips (not fabricated) for both Nearby and viewport pagination, invalid/null/non-finite input rejection, antimeridian rejection, and the ACL/RLS regression matrix.
- A distance-cursor precision defect found during validation — an unrounded Haversine distance could lose enough precision on a JSON round trip to reintroduce the prior page's boundary Clinic — was corrected by rounding `distance_meters` to millimeter precision at its single computation point, before filtering, ordering, pagination, or serialization use it.
- An oversized 101-Clinic fixture (built with `generate_series`) proved the server-side 100-row page cap, `has_more`, and exact-cursor continuation with no duplicate or gap, for both Nearby and viewport Discovery.
- The Slice 13 and Slice 14 regression suites both passed unchanged against the Slice-15-extended schema.
- Remote deployment verification passed: all three functions exist with the expected signatures and attributes; all expected `EXECUTE` grants are present; direct `SELECT` on the three base tables remains denied to `anon` and `authenticated`; a nonexistent published-location lookup returns zero rows without error; and both Nearby and viewport Discovery return a valid bounded JSON envelope.
- No live-provider validation applies; Discovery is a pure database read/query slice.
- Retained synthetic state: 0.

### Slice 16 — Standard Appointment Duration & Guardian Requestable Times

CLOSED.

- Migration `20260920120000_standard_appointment_duration_and_candidate_times.sql` deployed successfully: adds `clinics.standard_appointment_duration_minutes` (bounded 10–240 minutes, SPEC-APT-013 BR-57/BR-61/BR-62) and its `set_clinic_standard_appointment_duration` management RPC; adds the Guardian-facing `get_requestable_appointment_start_times` candidate read (SPEC-APT-014) and its `clinic_day_covered_civil_ranges` / `resolve_clinic_civil_candidate` generation helpers (SPEC-APT-013 BR-58); gives `request_appointment` and `reschedule_requested_appointment` forward-only breaking start-only signatures, deriving the Appointment end from the Clinic's current standard duration (SPEC-APT-001 BR-29–34, SPEC-APT-004 BR-22–24).
- No Veterinarian identity, assignment, or availability, and no Clinic-level numerical scheduling capacity model, was introduced: `REQUESTED`/`CONFIRMED` Appointments do not filter candidates (SPEC-APT-013 BR-66/BR-67, SPEC-APT-014 BR-8/BR-9). Candidate generation reuses the existing Clinic-timezone/DST-safe civil-time resolution with no new Availability rule (nonexistent/ambiguous civil starts omitted; the exact resolved UTC offset is returned with every candidate).
- Corrective migration `20260921093000_candidate_availability_lookup_performance.sql` deployed successfully, fixing a `57014` statement-timeout observed from the mobile client for the approved 30-day candidate window. Root cause, measured locally (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` against a Valverde-shaped fixture — Europe/Madrid, 30-minute duration, 09:00–14:00 and 16:00–20:00 Mon–Fri, 395 candidates): `clinic_appointment_interval_is_available` re-fetched and re-validated the Clinic's already-known-valid scheduling timezone against `pg_catalog.pg_timezone_names` once per generated candidate (~23ms/call), for ~9.2s total, although `get_requestable_appointment_start_times` had already resolved and validated the same timezone once at its eligibility step. Fix: the existing Availability-coverage logic was extracted into a new internal helper, `clinic_interval_is_covered_by_timezone(...)`, taking an already-validated timezone name; `clinic_appointment_interval_is_available(...)` keeps its exact signature and behavior (still self-validates) and delegates to it, so `request_appointment`, `reschedule_requested_appointment`, and the Slice 16 test suite are unaffected; only the candidate read skips the now-redundant re-validation. Post-fix measurement: ~55ms for the identical 30-day call (~167× faster), with identical candidate-count output before and after. No Availability, DST, capacity, or disclosure/eligibility-ordering semantics changed; no new persisted or materialized state was introduced.
- Deterministic reconstruction from all migrations through this corrective migration passed locally (`supabase db reset`).
- The rollback-only `supabase/tests/standard_appointment_duration_and_candidate_times_slice16.sql` suite passes cleanly with zero retained rows, covering: duration bounds and authorization; structural/uniform-empty-eligibility/Clinic-relative-horizon validation ordering (BR-19); normal/split-window/CLOSED-exception candidate generation; coverage-helper/authoritative-predicate equivalence (touching periods, disjoint gaps, day-boundary sentinel); `REQUESTED`/`CONFIRMED` non-filtering; DST `NONEXISTENT`/`AMBIGUOUS`/`UNIQUE` resolution across Europe/Madrid, Australia/Lord_Howe, Pacific/Auckland, and Pacific/Apia; and `request_appointment`/`reschedule_requested_appointment`'s start-only contract, current-duration derivation, and no-duration rejection. Re-run unchanged after the corrective performance migration.
- Mobile: the Request Appointment and Reschedule Appointment screens read the fixed 30-Clinic-local-day candidate window once, offer only dates with ≥1 candidate (`CandidateDateList`), and filter the already-loaded candidates in memory per date (`CandidateTimeList`) with no second RPC call; the selected candidate is submitted verbatim with no client-side Availability or duration computation.
- An offline/degraded-network state-handling defect on the Request Appointment screen, found during physical-device validation, was corrected: the screen no longer depends on the unused Clinic Location/Service-Offerings reads it previously inherited from the shared `getPublishedClinicDetail` composition, and a Pet-list-fetch failure no longer forces the fatal "could not load clinic information" state when Clinic identity/timezone already loaded successfully (new pure, unit-tested helper, `lib/request-appointment-load.ts`).
- Mobile typecheck, lint, format, and Jest (190 tests across 15 suites) pass. No new native dependency; no new EAS development build required.
- Both migrations and the mobile client (Metro, against the existing development build) have been validated on a physical device.
- Retained synthetic state: 0.

### Professional Context stream — SPEC-ORG-007 View My Professional Context

Development only. Not yet applied to Production.

- Specification: SPEC-ORG-007 — View My Professional Context is APPROVED (REQ-ORG-008, REQ-ID-005, REQ-ORG-005, REQ-SUB-003), governed by ADR-010 — Single Mobile Application with Derived Professional Context.
- Migration `20261001120000_professional_context_read.sql` is deployed to the linked DEVELOPMENT Supabase project. It:
  - adds `professional_capability_catalog()`, the single authoritative Phase 1 capability catalog (SPEC-ORG-005 §5);
  - redefines `user_has_organization_capability` to consult that catalog instead of an inline list, with unchanged signature, attributes, and observable behavior;
  - splits the body of `user_has_clinic_capability`, without behavior change, into `user_has_organization_scoped_clinic_capability` and `user_has_clinic_scoped_clinic_capability`, keeping `user_has_clinic_capability` as exactly their disjunction so every existing protected operation keeps its authorization;
  - adds the self-scoped, advisory `get_my_professional_context()` read (`SECURITY DEFINER`, `STABLE`, empty `search_path`, `EXECUTE` granted only to `authenticated`, so `anon`/`public` are rejected with `42501`), which raises `28000` for an `authenticated` context with no subject or a subject with no coherent domain User;
  - changes no table, column, index, constraint, table grant, or existing data, and no Entitlement, Subscription, Commercial Onboarding, or Organization-establishment behavior.
- The rollback-only regression suite `supabase/tests/professional_context_spec_org_007.sql` is source-controlled alongside the migration and passed LOCALLY. It has not been recorded as run against the DEVELOPMENT project.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`): the Professional Context foundation is implemented:
  - a typed, validated `get_my_professional_context()` data wrapper (`src/data/professional-context.ts`); SQLSTATE `28000` maps to the shared session-expired message; malformed payloads fail safely; Responsibilities, scope/Clinic shape, and the four Entitlement identifiers are strict, while unknown capability identifiers are kept only as non-rendered `unknownCapabilities` metadata so a newer backend catalog does not break an older app;
  - the authenticated Guardian Home loads professional context in the background on focus (latest request wins; failures never affect Guardian functionality) and shows a single "MenizVet Profesional / MenizVet Professional" entry only when one or more current Organizations are returned;
  - a `/professional` landing route showing the Organization (with a local-only selector when there are several), human-readable Responsibilities, and current assigned Clinics (with an unnamed-Clinic fallback), plus loading, retryable error, and no-access states; raw identifiers, capabilities, and Entitlements are never rendered, and nothing is used as client-side authorization.
- Physical mobile validation in development covered a Guardian-only User, a Clinic-scoped Administrator, a Receptionist, and a Veterinarian; the observed behavior matches the expected professional-context model. An Owner and an Organization-scoped Administrator have not been physically validated.
- Production: NOT updated. Neither the migration nor the mobile Professional Context foundation has been applied to or released against Production.

### Professional Context stream — SPEC-ORG-008 View My Professional Clinics

Backend in DEVELOPMENT only. Mobile integration implemented and physically validated in DEVELOPMENT. Not yet applied to Production.

- Specification: SPEC-ORG-008 — View My Professional Clinics is APPROVED (REQ-ORG-008, REQ-ORG-005, REQ-APT-008).

It adds only the self-scoped, advisory `get_my_professional_clinics()` read (`SECURITY DEFINER`, `STABLE`, empty `search_path`, no parameters, `EXECUTE` granted only to `authenticated`): a Clinic is returned exactly when `user_has_clinic_capability` grants the acting User at least one Clinic-context catalog capability for it, with that complete capability set, so no second responsibility-to-capability mapping exists. It changes no table, grant, existing function, or existing data.
- The rollback-only regression suite `supabase/tests/professional_clinics_spec_org_008.sql` is source-controlled and passed LOCALLY after a full `supabase db reset`, including the per-User × Clinic × capability parity check against `user_has_clinic_capability` and the SPEC-ORG-008 BR-18 consistency check against `get_my_professional_context()`. Every other committed SQL regression suite also passed LOCALLY against the extended schema, with zero retained synthetic rows. It has not been recorded as run against the DEVELOPMENT project.

  - an authenticated `/professional/clinics` route listing each Clinic once in backend order, grouped by Organization when several are present (since replaced: the list now shows only the Organization selected on `/professional`; see "Post-Phase-1 Clinic self-service UX/context polish"), with an unnamed-Clinic fallback, focus refresh with latest-request-wins protection, a retryable first-load error, and a calm empty state; raw identifiers, capabilities, and Entitlements are never rendered. When first delivered it offered no Clinic action; each Clinic card now opens the professional Clinic screen (see the Professional Appointment Schedule mobile section below).
