- Professional Clinics has been physically validated on mobile against the DEVELOPMENT backend.
- Production: NOT updated with SPEC-ORG-008.
- The follow-up gap previously recorded here — `get_clinic_appointment_schedule(clinic_id)` identifying each Appointment's Pet only by `pet_id` — is addressed by the SPEC-APT-012 Pet current-name change below, which is deployed to DEVELOPMENT.

### Professional Context stream — SPEC-APT-012 Pet current name in the Clinic Appointment Schedule

Implemented, validated LOCALLY, and deployed to DEVELOPMENT. Not applied to Production.

- Specification: SPEC-APT-012 — View Clinic Appointment Schedule remains APPROVED. It now defines `pet_id` as the Appointment's Pet identity and `pet_name` as the Pet's current name, resolved at read time (BR-41); no other Pet Profile, Guardianship, or Guardian information is exposed by this change (BR-42). No Pet-name snapshot or name history exists or is introduced.
- Forward-only migration `20261001170000_clinic_appointment_schedule_pet_name.sql` is deployed to the linked DEVELOPMENT Supabase project. It adds only the `pet_name` column (immediately after `pet_id`) to `get_clinic_appointment_schedule(uuid)`, through an inner join to `pets` inside the already-authorized read. Because a `RETURNS TABLE` column set cannot change through `CREATE OR REPLACE`, the function is dropped and recreated (no database object depends on it) with its attributes, authorization, SQLSTATEs, and ACL re-applied unchanged.
- It does NOT add species or any other Pet field, add a Pet lookup RPC, grant any Pet table access, change schedule authorization, change Appointment lifecycle behavior, or change schedule ordering. It changes no table, column, index, constraint, table grant, or existing data.
- The rollback-only regression suite `supabase/tests/clinic_appointment_schedule_pet_name_spec_apt_012.sql` passed LOCALLY: a full `supabase db reset`; the new suite with `ON_ERROR_STOP`; every previously committed SQL regression suite, unchanged; zero retained synthetic rows; and `git diff --check`. It has not been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- `pet_name` is now available in the DEVELOPMENT `get_clinic_appointment_schedule(uuid)` contract.
- Production: NOT updated with this change.

### Professional Context stream — Relevant scheduled interval correction (SPEC-APT-011, SPEC-APT-012)

Implemented, validated LOCALLY, and deployed to DEVELOPMENT. Not applied to Production.

- Specifications: SPEC-APT-011 — View Guardian Appointments and SPEC-APT-012 — View Clinic Appointment Schedule remain APPROVED. Both now explicitly define the relevant scheduled interval of an Appointment as its latest effective scheduling context: the latest REQUESTED Schedule Revision if the Appointment was never confirmed, otherwise the latest CONFIRMED Schedule Revision, including accepted reschedules (SPEC-APT-011 §4 and VN 19; SPEC-APT-012 §4 and VN 27).
- Forward-only migration `20261002090000_appointment_relevant_scheduled_interval.sql` is deployed to the linked DEVELOPMENT Supabase project. It changes only `get_pet_appointments(uuid)` and `get_clinic_appointment_schedule(uuid)`, through `CREATE OR REPLACE` with unchanged return signatures, attributes, authorization, and ACLs: both reads now select the Schedule Revision with the highest `revision_sequence` as the relevant interval, replacing a lifecycle-context selection that predated `CANCELLED`.
- No Schedule Revision, Lifecycle Event, Proposal, or other history row is rewritten, and no Appointment lifecycle semantics change.
- The visible behavior change affects only Appointments that were confirmed, then accepted one or more confirmed reschedules, then cancelled: both reads now show the final confirmed interval instead of an earlier requested interval. For every other lifecycle path the selected interval is unchanged.
- The rollback-only regression suite `supabase/tests/appointment_relevant_scheduled_interval.sql` verifies that the Guardian and Clinic reads return the same relevant interval for the same Appointment. It passed LOCALLY: a red/green regression check (the suite fails against the uncorrected reads and passes after the migration); a full `supabase db reset`; the new suite with `ON_ERROR_STOP`; every other SQL regression suite; zero retained synthetic rows; and `git diff --check`. It has not been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- DEVELOPMENT now uses the corrected relevant scheduled interval in both `get_pet_appointments(uuid)` and `get_clinic_appointment_schedule(uuid)`. The Guardian mobile Appointment screens, which read `get_pet_appointments`, need no code change to receive it.
- Production: NOT updated with this change.

### Professional Context stream — Professional Appointment Schedule mobile (read-only)

Implemented and physically validated against DEVELOPMENT. Not applied to Production.

- Consumes the backend already deployed to DEVELOPMENT: SPEC-ORG-007 professional context, SPEC-ORG-008 professional clinics, the SPEC-APT-012 `pet_name` amendment, and the relevant scheduled interval correction. Their rollback-only regression suites were run LOCALLY; none has been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- Flow: Professional Home → Professional Clinics → Clinic professional context → Appointment Schedule, on the routes `/professional/clinics` (unchanged URL), `/professional/clinics/[clinicId]`, and `/professional/clinics/[clinicId]/schedule`.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`):
  - a typed `getClinicAppointmentSchedule(clinicId)` wrapper with strict schedule payload validation, mapping SQLSTATE `28000` to the shared session-expired message, `42501` to a calm schedule-access message, and every other failure to a generic schedule error;
  - a Clinic professional landing screen that resolves the Clinic from a fresh `get_my_professional_clinics()` read (never from navigation parameters) and shows the "Agenda de citas / Appointment schedule" action only when the known capability set includes `APPOINTMENT_VIEW_SCHEDULE` — advisory only; the schedule read re-authorizes server-side;
  - a read-only Appointment Schedule screen grouping Appointments into active (`REQUESTED`, `CONFIRMED`; relevant scheduled start ascending) and historical (`COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED`, `NO_SHOW`; relevant scheduled start descending) sections, as presentation-only ordering. Each Appointment shows the Pet's current name (`pet_name`), its lifecycle state, its relevant scheduled interval formatted in the backend-supplied IANA Clinic timezone, its reason for visit, and a pending-reschedule indicator when applicable;
  - focus refresh with stale-response protection, retryable first-load errors, access-lost handling, an empty state, ES/EN strings, and the accessibility behavior of the existing DS v1 screens.
- The two-section grouping above is the presentation as first delivered; `EXPIRED` was later added to the historical section, and the stale-safe NO_SHOW work below replaced the grouping with three sections (Upcoming, Needs resolution, History).
- When delivered, this flow implemented no Appointment mutation: it did not support confirming, cancelling, rescheduling, assigning or reassigning a Veterinarian, completing, or marking no-show; nor Appointment detail, requester or Veterinarian display names, pagination or date-bounded retrieval, or a calendar UI. Confirmation and marking no-show were later added to the schedule screen and have since moved to the professional Appointment detail screen, which also hosts Clinic cancellation (see the stale-safe confirmation, stale-safe NO_SHOW, and professional Appointment detail sections below); every other item in this list remains unsupported.
- Automated mobile validation passes: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (37 suites, 499 tests), and `git diff --check`. No native dependency or config change was required, so the existing development build exercises it through Metro.
- Physical validation against DEVELOPMENT passed for the scenarios exercised: Clinic-scoped professional access reaches the Clinic screen and the schedule; the schedule loads real DEVELOPMENT data and shows Pet names; `REQUESTED` and `CONFIRMED` remain distinguishable; the active and historical sections behave correctly; schedule times use the Clinic timezone rather than the phone's; a pending reschedule is presented without replacing the current interval; the corrected cancelled-after-reschedule interval is shown correctly where tested; access loss produces the calm access-lost state; refresh and offline behavior work as designed; and Guardian-created Appointments are visible in the professional Clinic schedule after creation.
- Production: NOT updated with this flow.

### Professional Context stream — Stale-safe professional Appointment confirmation (SPEC-APT-002, SPEC-APT-012)

Implemented, validated LOCALLY, and deployed to DEVELOPMENT, where its end-to-end loop works. Not applied to Production.

- Specifications: SPEC-APT-002 — Confirm Appointment and SPEC-APT-012 — View Clinic Appointment Schedule remain APPROVED. SPEC-APT-002 now makes confirmation stale-safe (BR-21, BR-22, BR-23): the caller supplies the revision sequence of the `REQUESTED` scheduling context it reviewed, and a confirmation against a superseded requested context fails instead of committing the Clinic to a time its actor did not review. SPEC-APT-012 BR-43 adds the schedule concurrency token that confirmation relies on.
- Forward-only migration `20261003090000_stale_safe_appointment_confirmation.sql` is deployed to the linked DEVELOPMENT Supabase project (a prerequisite of the later `20261004090000` expiry migration, also deployed there). It:
  - adds `current_schedule_revision_sequence integer` to `get_clinic_appointment_schedule(uuid)`: the `revision_sequence` of exactly the Schedule Revision that supplies `current_scheduled_start_at`, `current_scheduled_end_at`, and `current_scheduling_timezone_name`. It is concurrency/version metadata, not presentation identity, and is never shown to users; no Schedule Revision identifier or revision history is exposed. Because the `RETURNS TABLE` column set changes, the read is dropped and recreated (no database object depends on it), with attributes, authorization, SQLSTATEs, filtering, ordering, and ACL unchanged;
  - replaces `confirm_appointment(uuid)` with `confirm_appointment(p_appointment_id uuid, p_expected_requested_revision_sequence integer) returns uuid` (no default). The previous one-argument RPC is dropped and no longer exists; it is not kept as a compatibility overload. A NULL or non-positive expected sequence is rejected as invalid input (`22023`);
  - after the unchanged authorization (`APPOINTMENT_CONFIRM`), Entitlement (`APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT`), locking, and `REQUESTED`-state checks, requires the latest `REQUESTED` revision sequence to equal the expected sequence; otherwise it raises the repository's plain `P0001` stale convention (`Stale requested Appointment schedule revision`), with zero domain, lifecycle, Veterinarian Assignment, Care Authorization, or Communications side effects. The future-time, Clinic Availability, assigned-Veterinarian, and deferred confirmed-commitment conflict checks then run unchanged;
  - on success still performs the existing `REQUESTED → CONFIRMED` transition — one `CONFIRMED` Schedule Revision copied from exactly the matched requested revision, one Lifecycle Event, and the lifecycle-state change — and the existing backend lifecycle trigger still enqueues the existing `APPOINTMENT_CONFIRMED` Communications;
  - changes no table, column, constraint, index, trigger, or existing data, and introduces no schema redesign.
- The dedicated rollback-only regression suite `supabase/tests/appointment_confirmation_spec_apt_002.sql` passed LOCALLY with `ON_ERROR_STOP` after a clean `supabase db reset`. It covers the contract and removed signature, the schedule token's exact correspondence to the returned interval, stale rejection (including same-start and timezone-only revision changes) with zero side effects, invalid sequences, authorization by responsibility and scope, Entitlement states, lifecycle and two-actor behavior, past time, Clinic Availability, Veterinarian validation including deferred-conflict rollback, Communications recipients, and the Clinic and Guardian reads after confirmation. The stale case was proven red/green: the previous one-argument contract confirmed a newer, unreviewed requested revision, and the new contract rejects it. The existing schedule suites `appointment_relevant_scheduled_interval.sql` and `clinic_appointment_schedule_pet_name_spec_apt_012.sql` were updated only for the additive output column in their exact-signature assertions. Every committed SQL regression suite passed LOCALLY after the clean reset. No suite has been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- `docs/PRODUCTION_VALIDATION_RUNBOOK.md` §11 now calls the two-argument contract, obtaining the expected sequence from `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` as the acting Clinic actor.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`): the professional Appointment Schedule now supports the first professional mutation, `REQUESTED → CONFIRMED`:
  - the schedule parser requires `currentScheduleRevisionSequence` (positive integer) and rejects the payload otherwise; the value is never rendered or announced;
  - a typed `confirmAppointment(appointmentId, expectedRequestedRevisionSequence)` wrapper (`src/data/appointment-confirmation.ts`) calling `confirm_appointment` with `p_appointment_id` and `p_expected_requested_revision_sequence`;
  - a Confirm action shown only for a `REQUESTED` Appointment at a Clinic whose known capabilities include `APPOINTMENT_CONFIRM`; unknown capabilities never enable it (since phase 4C of the professional agenda, also only with the Organization's `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT` available — SPEC-APT-012 BR-61, below; the backend stays authoritative);
  - a native confirmation dialog showing the Pet name and the exact date and time displayed on the card;
  - stale-safe submission using exactly the displayed row's revision sequence, never re-read or substituted first; a stale confirmation is never retried automatically;
  - professional-specific error mapping (`src/i18n/appointment-confirmation-error-messages.ts`) for session, capability, Entitlement, stale, lifecycle-changed, past-time, Clinic Availability, assigned-Veterinarian, and generic failures, never showing raw backend text;
  - one in-flight confirmation per schedule screen, with Confirm actions disabled while submitting;
  - a re-read of the Professional Clinic capabilities and the schedule after success or any failure, so the authoritative state decides what remains visible;
  - no client-side Communications: only the existing backend `APPOINTMENT_CONFIRMED` Communications apply, with no mobile or Edge Function duplication.
  - The schedule-card placement described above is as first delivered: Confirm now appears only in the professional Appointment detail screen, with the same eligibility, dialog, stale-safe token, and error mapping (see the professional Appointment detail section below).
- Automated mobile validation passes: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (39 suites, 548 tests), and `git diff --check`. No dependency, native, or config change was required. This mobile build requires the new schedule contract: against a backend without the migration, its strict parser rejects the schedule payload, so the migration must be deployed to DEVELOPMENT before this build is used there.
- DEVELOPMENT: the Guardian request → professional visibility → stale-safe confirmation → Guardian sees `CONFIRMED` loop works end to end. Individual physical validation of stale rejection, VETERINARIAN-only visibility, capability/access loss, Entitlement denial, and confirmation Communications delivery has not been separately recorded.
- Production: NOT updated with this change.

### Appointment stream — Request expiry and pending-request policy (SPEC-APT-015)

Implemented, validated LOCALLY, deployed to DEVELOPMENT with its expiry scheduler enabled, and physically validated there for the scenarios recorded below. Not applied to Production.

- Specifications: SPEC-APT-015 — Expire Unconfirmed Appointment Request is APPROVED; SPEC-APT-001, SPEC-APT-002, SPEC-APT-003, SPEC-APT-004, SPEC-APT-009, SPEC-APT-011, SPEC-APT-012, SPEC-ACC-001, SPEC-MED-001, and SPEC-COM-003 remain APPROVED with the coordinated amendments.
- Forward-only migration `20261004090000_appointment_request_expiry_and_pending_policy.sql` is deployed to the linked DEVELOPMENT Supabase project. It implements:
  - `EXPIRED` as a terminal Appointment lifecycle state, reachable only through `REQUESTED → EXPIRED`, at the logical expiry instant — the latest `REQUESTED` scheduled start;
  - a SYSTEM Lifecycle Event actor: `actor_context = 'SYSTEM'` with no User, permitted only for expiry, while Guardian and Clinic Events still require their User; the Event's `effective_at` is the logical expiry instant and `recorded_at` the materialization time;
  - an immediate Care Authorization pre-care cutoff at the logical expiry instant, through one shared predicate used by both Appointment-derived access paths, independent of the expiry job;
  - the `APPOINTMENT_EXPIRED` Care Authorization ending when expiry is materialized (already `REVOKED`/`ENDED` authorizations are untouched);
  - rejection of confirmation, Guardian and Clinic cancellation, and Guardian rescheduling for a `REQUESTED` Appointment whose requested start has passed, whether or not `EXPIRED` has been materialized;
  - at most one future pending `REQUESTED` Appointment per Pet + Clinic, enforced in `request_appointment` after its existing Organization → Clinic serialization; confirmed, terminal, and lapsed requests never block;
  - the internal, idempotent `expire_lapsed_appointment_requests` operation, not granted to `anon` or `authenticated`;
  - no expiry Communication; Clinical Encounter behavior is unchanged.
- Scheduler: pg_cron job `petcare-appointment-request-expiry-v1` (provisioning `supabase/provisioning/appointment_request_expiry_scheduler.sql`, disable script alongside) runs every 5 minutes in DEVELOPMENT. Before enabling it, duplicate future `REQUESTED` data was diagnosed with the read-only pending-duplicate query; the known duplicate DEVELOPMENT data was resolved through the normal cancellation flow; the diagnostic then returned zero rows; only then was the scheduler enabled.
- LOCAL validation: a clean `supabase db reset`; all 12 committed SQL regression suites pass, including the new rollback-only `supabase/tests/appointment_request_expiry_spec_apt_015.sql` and the 11 previous suites unmodified; mutation checks (no pending check, time-blind pre-care access, no lapsed-cancel check, no lapsed-reschedule check, no-op expiry) are each detected; two-session races pass (concurrent requests by two current Guardians yield exactly one pending request; overlapping expiry runs expire exactly once). No SQL suite has been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`): `EXPIRED` is a known lifecycle state; both the Guardian and professional parsers accept it while still rejecting unknown values; it is historical in both views with its own localized label; a Guardian `EXPIRED` Appointment is read-only and a professional one offers no Confirm. A stored `REQUESTED` whose requested start has passed hides the impossible Guardian Cancel/Reschedule and professional Confirm actions (advisory; the backend is authoritative). The Guardian error mapping now has specific messages for a duplicate pending request and for lapsed-request cancellation/rescheduling rejections. Automated mobile validation passes: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (39 suites, 600 tests), and `git diff --check`.
- Physical validation in DEVELOPMENT: the mobile `EXPIRED` compatibility (parsing, historical presentation, hidden impossible actions) was validated; and a past `REQUESTED` Appointment became `EXPIRED` through the scheduler and appeared as historical in mobile.
- Production: NOT updated with this stream.

### Professional Context stream — Stale-safe professional NO_SHOW (SPEC-APT-010)

Implemented, validated LOCALLY, in DEVELOPMENT, and physically validated there for the path recorded below. Not applied to Production.

- Specification: SPEC-APT-010 — Mark Appointment No-Show remains APPROVED. It now makes NO_SHOW stale-safe: the caller calls `mark_appointment_no_show(p_appointment_id uuid, p_expected_confirmed_revision_sequence integer)`, where the expected sequence is the Clinic schedule's `current_schedule_revision_sequence` for the `CONFIRMED` row the actor reviewed. A stale call fails with plain `P0001` and the stable message `Stale confirmed Appointment schedule revision`. The previous one-argument RPC is retired.
- Forward-only migration `20261005090000_stale_safe_appointment_no_show.sql` implements this contract. It drops `mark_appointment_no_show(uuid)` — the one-argument form no longer exists and is not kept as a compatibility overload — and creates the two-argument form (`returns uuid`, no default) with the previous ACL re-applied. A NULL or non-positive expected sequence is rejected as invalid input (`22023`). Behavior:
  - only `CONFIRMED` Appointments are eligible;
  - the exact reviewed `CONFIRMED` revision is checked before any mutation: after the unchanged authorization, locking, and `CONFIRMED`-state checks, the latest `CONFIRMED` revision sequence must equal the expected sequence;
  - a stale revision fails with zero lifecycle, Care Authorization, or Communication side effects;
  - an Appointment with a Clinical Encounter cannot become `NO_SHOW`;
  - NO_SHOW is allowed once the latest `CONFIRMED` scheduled start has been reached, evaluated against the same revision that passed the stale check; the scheduled end is not required;
  - a successful NO_SHOW records the `CONFIRMED → NO_SHOW` lifecycle outcome, ends the Appointment-linked Care Authorization with `APPOINTMENT_NO_SHOW`, and relies on the existing lifecycle Communications trigger, which produces the existing Guardian `APPOINTMENT_NO_SHOW` Communications;
  - no Entitlement is required to resolve an existing Appointment as NO_SHOW;
  - VETERINARIAN-only authority does not grant `APPOINTMENT_MARK_NO_SHOW`;
  - no table, column, constraint, index, trigger, or existing data changes.
- LOCAL validation: a full `supabase db reset` succeeds; the new rollback-only suite `supabase/tests/appointment_no_show_spec_apt_010.sql` passes with `ON_ERROR_STOP`. It covers the contract and removed signature, invalid sequences, authorization by responsibility and scope, every Entitlement state, lifecycle, the time rule, stale rejection with zero side effects, the Clinical Encounter rule, outcome and Communications recipients, and sequential race outcomes (a second actor, completion, or cancellation winning first). All 13 SQL regression suites pass: the new suite, `appointment_request_expiry_spec_apt_015.sql` with its no-show helper updated to the two-argument contract, and the 11 others unmodified. The NO_SHOW suite caught all 8 deliberate mutants. `git diff --check` is clean. The "at the scheduled start" boundary is exercised with a start equal to the transaction start, because the database clock cannot be frozen; the races are serialized in one session, not run as concurrent sessions. No SQL suite has been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`): the professional Appointment Schedule now supports NO_SHOW:
  - a typed `markAppointmentNoShow(appointmentId, expectedConfirmedRevisionSequence)` wrapper (`src/data/appointment-no-show.ts`) calling `mark_appointment_no_show` with `p_appointment_id` and `p_expected_confirmed_revision_sequence`;
  - stale-safe submission using exactly the displayed schedule row's revision sequence, never re-read or substituted first; the sequence is never rendered or announced; no automatic retry;
  - professional NO_SHOW-specific localized error mapping (`src/i18n/appointment-no-show-error-messages.ts`) for session expiry, lost attendance access, a changed Appointment, a start not yet reached, care already started, the stale schedule, and generic failure; raw backend errors are not shown;
  - a "Marcar como no presentado / Mark as no-show" action that appears only when the lifecycle is `CONFIRMED`, the scheduled start is at or before the current advisory device time, and the Clinic's known capabilities include `APPOINTMENT_MARK_NO_SHOW`; unknown capabilities never enable it;
  - a native confirmation dialog showing the Pet name and the card's exact date and time;
  - one in-flight mutation per schedule screen, shared with Confirm, and a re-read of the Professional Clinic capabilities and the schedule after success or any failure.
  - The schedule-card placement described above is as first delivered: Mark as no-show now appears only in the professional Appointment detail screen, with the same eligibility, dialog, stale-safe token, and error mapping (see the professional Appointment detail section below).
- Professional schedule presentation now has three sections. The grouping is presentation-only and does not change lifecycle semantics:
  - "Próximas / Upcoming": future `REQUESTED` and future `CONFIRMED`. A stored past `REQUESTED` may remain here briefly while expiry materialization is pending; mobile never fabricates `EXPIRED`;
  - "Por resolver / Needs resolution": `CONFIRMED` Appointments whose scheduled start has been reached, ordered oldest scheduled start first so the longest-unresolved obligation is most visible;
  - "Historial / History": `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED`, `NO_SHOW`, and `EXPIRED`.
- Automated mobile validation passes: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, the Prettier format check, `npx jest` (41 suites, 646 tests, all passing), and `git diff --check` (clean). No dependency, native, or config change was required.
- Physical validation in DEVELOPMENT: the stale-safe professional NO_SHOW flow was tested successfully on the observed path — a past `CONFIRMED` Appointment appears under "Por resolver"; an authorized professional sees "Marcar como no presentado"; the action succeeds; the Appointment becomes `NO_SHOW`; and it moves to History. Individual physical validation of stale rejection, the too-early rejection, VETERINARIAN-only hiding, capability or access loss, the Clinical Encounter rejection, and NO_SHOW Communications delivery has not been separately recorded; those are covered only by the automated tests above.
- Production: NOT updated with this change. Neither the migration nor this mobile behavior has been released to Production.

### Professional Context stream — Professional Appointment detail and stale-safe Clinic cancellation (SPEC-APT-003)

Implemented, validated LOCALLY, deployed to DEVELOPMENT, and physically validated there for the paths recorded below. Not applied to Production.

- Specification: SPEC-APT-003 — Cancel Appointment remains APPROVED. It now makes Clinic-side cancellation stale-safe (BR-24 to BR-29): a Clinic actor's cancellation carries the Clinic schedule's `current_schedule_revision_sequence` for the Appointment row the actor reviewed. A stale Clinic cancellation fails with plain `P0001` and the stable message `Stale Appointment schedule revision for cancellation`. Guardian cancellation semantics are unchanged and require no token. Whether a `CONFIRMED` Appointment whose scheduled start has passed may still be cancelled remains an explicitly unresolved policy; this change does not decide it (BR-28, §7).
- Forward-only migration `20261006090000_stale_safe_clinic_appointment_cancellation.sql` implements the new contract and is deployed to the linked DEVELOPMENT Supabase project. It drops `cancel_appointment(uuid, text, text)` — the three-argument form no longer exists and is not kept as an overload — and creates `cancel_appointment(p_appointment_id uuid, p_cancelling_context text, p_reason text default null, p_expected_schedule_revision_sequence integer default null) returns uuid` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, `VOLATILE`, empty `search_path`) with the previous ACL re-applied. Behavior:
  - Guardian calls by named parameters without a token remain compatible and behave as before; a Guardian call with a non-NULL token is rejected as invalid input (`22023`);
  - a Clinic call requires a positive revision token and a non-blank reason; either missing is rejected as invalid input (`22023`) before any Appointment state is consulted;
  - after the unchanged `APPOINTMENT_CANCEL` authorization, locking, and `REQUESTED`/`CONFIRMED` lifecycle check, a Clinic call compares the token with the latest `REQUESTED` revision for a `REQUESTED` Appointment, or the latest `CONFIRMED` revision for a `CONFIRMED` Appointment; an Appointment that is already terminal is rejected as a lifecycle error, never as stale;
  - a stale call writes nothing: no Lifecycle Event, lifecycle change, Care Authorization ending, Communication, Schedule Revision, Proposal, or Veterinarian Assignment change;
  - existing cancellation semantics are unchanged: capability `APPOINTMENT_CANCEL`; no Entitlement requirement; rejection when a Clinical Encounter exists; the pending-request rule for `REQUESTED`; the Care Authorization ends as `APPOINTMENT_CANCELLED`; and the existing lifecycle trigger produces `APPOINTMENT_CANCELLED` Communications for current Guardians;
  - backend behavior for cancelling a past `CONFIRMED` Appointment is unchanged;
  - no table, column, constraint, index, trigger, or existing data changes.
- LOCAL validation: a clean full `supabase db reset`; the new rollback-only suite `supabase/tests/appointment_cancellation_spec_apt_003.sql` passes with `ON_ERROR_STOP`, covering the contract and removed signature, input validation before lookup, Guardian compatibility, current-token success and stale rejection for `REQUESTED` (after a real Guardian reschedule) and `CONFIRMED` (after a real accepted Clinic proposal), same-start, end-only, and timezone-only staleness, refresh-then-succeed, lapsed and `EXPIRED` requests, races with Guardian cancellation, NO_SHOW, and completion, the Clinical Encounter rule, zero-write rejections, Communications recipients, authorization by responsibility and scope, and success without `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT` Entitlement. All 14 SQL regression suites pass, with zero retained synthetic rows. The existing `appointment_no_show_spec_apt_010.sql` and `appointment_request_expiry_spec_apt_015.sql` suites were updated only where Clinic cancellation now requires the token (and the exact signature in an ACL assertion); no assertion was weakened. 4 of 4 deliberate mutation checks were caught: the `REQUESTED` stale check removed, the `CONFIRMED` stale check removed, the missing-token validation removed, and the Clinical Encounter prohibition removed. `git diff --check` is clean. No SQL suite has been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`) — professional Appointment detail:
  - a route `/professional/clinics/[clinicId]/appointments/[appointmentId]` that receives only the Clinic id and Appointment id; on every focus it re-reads `get_my_professional_clinics()`, confirms the Clinic is still offered, re-reads `get_clinic_appointment_schedule(clinicId)`, and locates the Appointment by id (no new backend detail read), with latest-request-wins protection; it shows a calm unavailable state when Clinic access, schedule access, or the Appointment disappears;
  - first-version layout: Pet name, lifecycle badge, derived situation (Upcoming / Needs resolution / History), Clinic and Organization, date and time in the Clinic timezone, reason for visit, and a read-only pending-reschedule indication when present;
  - it intentionally does not show identifiers, the revision token, capabilities, Entitlements, Guardian notes, medical information, or a Veterinarian name (no display-identity contract exists yet). Guardian notes have since been surfaced (see "Pre-Clinic-self-service closure pass").
- Mobile — professional Clinic cancellation lives only in the Appointment detail screen:
  - offered only when the known capabilities include `APPOINTMENT_CANCEL` and the Appointment is `REQUESTED` or `CONFIRMED` with its scheduled start strictly in the future; deliberately hidden for a past `CONFIRMED`, a past `REQUESTED`, `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED`, `NO_SHOW`, `EXPIRED`, and an unknown or missing capability (advisory; the backend is authoritative);
  - an inline cancellation form, as in the Guardian Appointment detail, showing the Pet, lifecycle, date, and time being cancelled and requiring a non-blank multiline cancellation reason ("Motivo de la cancelación / Cancellation reason");
  - a typed `cancelProfessionalAppointment(appointmentId, reason, expectedScheduleRevisionSequence)` wrapper (`src/data/professional-appointment-cancellation.ts`) that always sends `p_cancelling_context: 'CLINIC'` and exactly the displayed row's `currentScheduleRevisionSequence`, never silently substituting a newer revision; no automatic retry;
  - a stale result, like any failure, closes the form and refreshes the Appointment, so another attempt requires explicit review; on success the screen stays and shows the refreshed `CANCELLED` Appointment;
  - professional-specific localized error mapping (`src/i18n/professional-appointment-cancellation-error-messages.ts`) for session expired, capability lost, stale revision, lifecycle changed, expired request, Clinical Encounter already started, missing reason, and generic/network failure; raw backend text is never shown;
  - one in-flight mutation at a time across Confirm, Mark as no-show, and Cancel on the detail screen;
  - directly under the cancellation-reason input, informational helper text (not an error) — "Este motivo será visible para los responsables de la mascota. / This reason will be visible to the pet's guardians." — makes explicit that the Clinic-entered reason becomes Guardian-visible historical information (see the Guardian cancellation-reason section below). It is plain text read by screen readers after the field; it changes no backend rule or cancellation contract, leaves the required-reason validation unchanged, and exposes no Guardian identity.
- Mobile — schedule cards are now pure navigation controls. The rule is: the schedule is for scanning and navigating; the Appointment detail is where actions are performed.
  - Confirm and Mark as no-show were removed from the schedule cards, along with the schedule screen's mutation state; the schedule list contains no mutation button.
  - Each card is one control, announced with the Pet, lifecycle, date, time, reason, and any pending reschedule, plus the hint "Abre los detalles de la cita / Opens the appointment details", and opens the Appointment detail route; decorative icons stay hidden from screen readers.
  - The three schedule sections are unchanged: "Próximas / Upcoming", "Por resolver / Needs resolution", "Historial / History".
  - The action eligibility logic still exists and is used by the detail screen.
- Current professional detail actions:
  - future `REQUESTED`: Confirm and Cancel;
  - future `CONFIRMED`: Cancel;
  - past `CONFIRMED` (Needs resolution): Mark as no-show (and, since the lightweight completion slice, Mark as completed — see that section below);
  - past `REQUESTED` and terminal states: read-only.
  - Complete, confirmed-reschedule proposal, Veterinarian assignment or reassignment, and past-`CONFIRMED` Cancel were not implemented at that point. Confirmed-reschedule proposal and Complete have since been added (see their sections below).
- Automated mobile validation passes: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (43 suites, 725 tests, all passing), and `git diff --check` (clean). No dependency, native, or config change was required.
- Physical validation in DEVELOPMENT passed for these paths: a future `REQUESTED` Appointment card has no inline action; tapping it opens the professional Appointment detail; the detail shows Confirm and Cancel; a future `CONFIRMED` Appointment's detail shows Cancel; terminal (read-only) behavior and navigation were checked where available; and returning from the detail refreshes the schedule correctly for the tested paths. No organic past `CONFIRMED` Appointment remained available on the test device, so the Needs resolution path was checked with a controlled, presentation-only DEVELOPMENT fixture: a past `CONFIRMED` Appointment appeared under "Por resolver / Needs resolution", its schedule card had no inline mutation action, and its detail exposed only "Marcar como no presentado / Mark as no-show", with Clinic cancellation hidden; the fixture was then restored to its original `CANCELLED` state. NO_SHOW was not submitted in this round. With the migration deployed to DEVELOPMENT, one real Clinic-side cancellation was also validated through the mobile professional Appointment detail: an eligible future Appointment was opened from the professional schedule; Cancel was available only in the Appointment detail; a non-blank cancellation reason was submitted; the cancellation succeeded; the Appointment refreshed as `CANCELLED` and read-only; returning to the schedule showed it in History; and the Guardian view showed it as `CANCELLED`. Stale cancellation rejection, capability or access loss, VETERINARIAN-only hiding, the Clinical Encounter rejection, and cancellation email/push delivery have not been separately recorded physically; they are covered only by the automated tests above.
- Production: NOT updated with this change. Neither the migration nor the mobile detail, cancellation, or schedule-card behavior has been released to Production.

### Appointment stream — Guardian cancellation-reason visibility (SPEC-APT-011, SPEC-APT-003)

Implemented, validated LOCALLY, deployed to DEVELOPMENT, and physically validated there for the path recorded below. Not applied to Production.

- Specifications: SPEC-APT-011 — View Guardian Appointments and SPEC-APT-003 — Cancel Appointment remain APPROVED. SPEC-APT-011 BR-31 now states that a `CANCELLED` Appointment shows the reason recorded by its cancellation, whether a Guardian or the Clinic cancelled it; SPEC-APT-003 BR-9 states that a recorded cancellation reason is part of the cancelled Appointment's history that current Guardians may see.
- Forward-only migration `20261007090000_guardian_appointment_cancellation_reason.sql` is deployed to the linked DEVELOPMENT Supabase project. It appends exactly one column, `cancellation_reason text`, to `get_pet_appointments(uuid)`:
  - for a `CANCELLED` Appointment, the immutable reason stored on its single `CANCELLED` Lifecycle Event (`appointment_lifecycle_events.reason`), exactly as written by `cancel_appointment` — `NULL` when a Guardian cancelled without a reason;
  - `NULL` for every other lifecycle state;
  - distinct from `reason_for_visit`, Guardian notes, reschedule-proposal reasons, and medical information; no actor identity, actor context, Event time, or other lifecycle history is exposed;
  - because a `RETURNS TABLE` column set cannot change through `CREATE OR REPLACE`, the read is dropped and recreated (no database object depends on it), with its body, attributes (`SECURITY DEFINER`, `STABLE`, empty `search_path`), authorization, filtering, relevant scheduled interval, pending-Proposal projection, ordering, and ACL otherwise unchanged; `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` is unchanged;
  - no table, column, constraint, index, trigger, or existing data changes. Historical cancelled Appointments need no backfill: their cancellation reason was already stored in `appointment_lifecycle_events.reason`.
- LOCAL validation: a clean `supabase db reset`; all 15 SQL regression suites pass, with zero retained synthetic rows, including the new rollback-only suite `supabase/tests/guardian_appointment_cancellation_reason_spec_apt_011.sql`. It verifies Guardian cancellation with and without a reason; Clinic cancellation of `REQUESTED` and `CONFIRMED` Appointments; an accepted confirmed reschedule followed by cancellation (latest confirmed interval shown, reason from the cancellation); `NULL` for `REQUESTED`, `CONFIRMED`, `COMPLETED`, `NO_SHOW`, and `EXPIRED`; separation from the reason for visit, Guardian notes, and accepted or pending Proposal reasons; no read-side writes; the exact signature and ACL; and current-Guardian access with former-Guardian denial. Three deliberate incorrect implementations were each detected by the suite. The existing `appointment_relevant_scheduled_interval.sql` suite was updated only for the additive column in its exact-signature assertion. `git diff --check` is clean. No SQL suite has been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`):
  - the Guardian Appointment model gains `cancellationReason: string | null`; the `get_pet_appointments` payload validation now rejects the whole read when the column is missing, not a string, empty or blank, or non-`NULL` on a non-`CANCELLED` Appointment, and no other field is ever substituted for it. This build therefore requires the migration in its target environment;
  - the Guardian Appointment detail shows a separate read-only "Motivo de la cancelación / Cancellation reason" section for a `CANCELLED` Appointment that has a reason, directly after and distinct from "Motivo de la visita / Reason for visit"; Appointment list cards are unchanged.
- Automated mobile validation passes: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (43 suites, 741 tests, all passing), and `git diff --check` (clean). No dependency, native, or config change was required.
- Physical validation in DEVELOPMENT: the previously Clinic-cancelled Appointment appears to the Guardian as `CANCELLED`; its existing reason for visit is unchanged; the Clinic-entered cancellation reason appears in the dedicated "Motivo de la cancelación" section; and no backfill was required. A Guardian-entered cancellation reason has not been separately validated physically; it is covered only by the automated tests above.
- Production: NOT updated with this change. Neither the migration nor the Guardian cancellation-reason presentation has been released to Production.

### Appointment stream — Confirmed Appointment reschedule and Clinic withdrawal (SPEC-APT-005, SPEC-APT-006, SPEC-APT-016, SPEC-APT-017)

- Implemented: the slice is fully implemented in code (backend migration, SQL regression suite, mobile data layer and screens).
- Automated validation: green LOCALLY (16 SQL suites, 13/13 deliberate faults caught, 47 Jest suites with 890 tests, typecheck, strict unused-locals/parameters, ESLint, Prettier, `git diff --check`).
- DEVELOPMENT: migration `20261008090000_confirmed_appointment_reschedule_and_withdrawal.sql` has been applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user.
- Physical validation: the core Android workflow has been physically validated by the user on a real device (details below); some advanced edge cases remain physically unverified.
- Production: not deployed and not touched.

- Specifications: SPEC-APT-005 (BR-23–BR-32) and SPEC-APT-006 (BR-28–BR-33) are amended; SPEC-APT-016 — Withdraw Clinic Reschedule Proposal and SPEC-APT-017 — View Professional Reschedule Candidate Times are new and APPROVED; SPEC-APT-011 (BR-32), SPEC-APT-012 (BR-44), SPEC-APT-013 (BR-63, BR-68–BR-70, §10 corrected), and SPEC-COM-003 (BR-70) are amended. `docs/COMMUNICATIONS_OPERATIONAL_POLICY.md`, `docs/RELATIONAL_MODEL.md`, and `docs/DOMAIN.md` record the `WITHDRAWN` state and the delivery rule.
- Contract decisions implemented:
  - proposal creation is start-only for both the CLINIC and GUARDIAN contexts; the server derives the end as the resolved start plus the elapsed duration `D` of the base `CONFIRMED` Schedule Revision, applied in whole microseconds (never calendar-day arithmetic); the Clinic's standard duration is never read or required, and historical durations are preserved without normalization;
  - a CLINIC proposal carries the expected confirmed revision sequence; a stale one fails with `P0001` `Stale confirmed Appointment schedule revision for reschedule proposal` and writes nothing, not even the lazy non-actionable fact of an earlier proposal; a GUARDIAN proposal must not carry one;
  - in the GUARDIAN context, current Guardianship is proven by an unlocked pre-check before any lifecycle state is disclosed (SPEC-APT-005 BR-27), and revalidated on the locked Guardianship row after the Appointment lock;
  - the same resolved start is always rejected; Clinic Availability, the assigned Veterinarian's current Clinic Assignment and Availability, and known overlapping `CONFIRMED` commitments of that Veterinarian (at any Clinic, excluding the Appointment itself) are validated at creation and revalidated at acceptance through shared helpers, with deliberately generic Veterinarian errors; the deferred conflict constraint remains authoritative at commit;
  - a Clinical Encounter prevents creation and acceptance and makes a pending proposal non-actionable in `appointment_reschedule_proposal_is_actionable` (no fact is materialized);
  - acceptance establishes exactly the stored, frozen interval; later standard-duration changes have no effect;
  - Clinic withdrawal (`WITHDRAWN`, immutable, `withdrawn_at` set by the database, `withdrawn_by_user_id`): any current Clinic actor with `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE`, no Entitlement, only for an actionable Clinic-originated proposal, no reason, no Communication; it frees the pending-proposal slot through the unchanged partial unique index;
  - `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_PROPOSED` delivery eligibility now uses the authoritative actionability predicate, which also suppresses it after cancellation (previously missed), withdrawal, an Encounter, a superseded base, authority loss, or a lapsed time.
- Forward-only migration `20261008090000_confirmed_appointment_reschedule_and_withdrawal.sql`:
  - drops `propose_confirmed_appointment_reschedule(uuid, text, date, time, integer, date, time, integer, text)` (no compatibility overload) and creates `propose_confirmed_appointment_reschedule(p_appointment_id uuid, p_proposing_context text, p_start_local_date date, p_start_local_time time, p_start_utc_offset_seconds integer, p_reason text default null, p_expected_confirmed_revision_sequence integer default null) returns uuid`;
  - re-creates `accept_appointment_reschedule_proposal(p_proposal_id uuid, p_accepting_context text) returns uuid` with the same signature;
  - adds `withdraw_appointment_reschedule_proposal(p_proposal_id uuid) returns uuid` and the STABLE `get_appointment_reschedule_candidate_start_times(p_appointment_id uuid, p_start_date date, p_end_date date)` returning `candidate_local_date`, `candidate_local_time`, `utc_offset_seconds`, `candidate_end_at` (APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE plus the proposal Entitlement; no publication requirement; at most 60 days per call; no business horizon; D as both length and cadence with sub-second precision; current start excluded; Clinic and Veterinarian feasibility applied; a duration that fits no covered range, including any of 24 hours or more, yields no candidates);
  - adds `withdrawn_at`/`withdrawn_by_user_id`, the `WITHDRAWN` state, a rebuilt shape constraint, a Clinic-only withdrawal constraint, and the extended integrity trigger;
  - adds internal helpers `veterinarian_interval_is_covered_by_timezone` (with `veterinarian_availability_covers_interval` delegating, unchanged signature and behavior), `veterinarian_has_known_confirmed_commitment_conflict`, `appointment_reschedule_interval_infeasibility`, `assert_appointment_reschedule_interval_feasible`, and `appointment_interval_duration_microseconds`, none executable by `anon`/`authenticated`;
  - re-creates `communication_delivery_is_currently_eligible` with only the PROPOSED branch changed;
  - leaves `get_pet_appointments` and `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` unchanged (they already project only actionable proposals); `resolve_clinic_appointment_interval` is no longer called by any function and is left in place.
- LOCAL validation: a clean `supabase db reset`; the new rollback-only suite `supabase/tests/confirmed_appointment_reschedule_spec_apt_005_006_016_017.sql` passes with `ON_ERROR_STOP` (contract and ACLs, invalid input, preserved duration for both contexts including 7 min / 300 min / 30 min 17 s, spring-forward, fall-back, and a 25-hour duration under a `Europe/Madrid` session, standard-duration changes before and after proposal, same start, stale rejection with zero writes and refresh-then-succeed, feasibility at creation and acceptance with generic errors and self-exclusion, Clinical Encounter, withdrawal, acceptance authority and Entitlement, Communication suppression, the candidate read including exact read/write round-trips and a 60-day timing bound, a non-Guardian receiving the same authorization error for a CONFIRMED and a REQUESTED Appointment, and a retried proposal after a committed one writing nothing while the schedule still projects the committed Clinic proposal). 13 of 13 deliberate behavioral mutants were caught, including one that discloses the lifecycle before Guardian authority. All 16 SQL regression suites pass with zero retained synthetic rows; `appointment_cancellation_spec_apt_003.sql` and `guardian_appointment_cancellation_reason_spec_apt_011.sql` were updated only to the new start-only propose signature. Measured locally, a 60-day Veterinarian-constrained candidate read returned 1,191 candidates in ~150 ms (`EXPLAIN ANALYZE`). `git diff --check` is clean.
- Read-only historical-duration diagnostic: in DEVELOPMENT (linked project, one SELECT through the CLI) there is 1 `CONFIRMED` Appointment, 30 minutes; none is under 10 minutes, over 240 minutes, non-whole-minute, or 24 hours or more. It has NOT been run against Production.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`):
  - data: `src/data/professional-appointment-reschedule.ts` (`getAppointmentRescheduleCandidates`, `proposeClinicReschedule`, `withdrawRescheduleProposal`, strict candidate validation, no client-computed end) and `acceptRescheduleProposal` in `src/data/appointments.ts`; the Guardian read now validates the pending proposal with the same strict parser as the professional schedule (`parsePendingRescheduleColumns`), so a partial or incoherent proposal rejects the whole read instead of becoming "no proposal";
  - professional Appointment detail: a pending-proposal card (exact stored interval, origin, reason); "Proponer nuevo horario / Propose a new time" for a future `CONFIRMED` Appointment with no pending proposal and known `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE`; "Retirar propuesta / Withdraw proposal" with a confirmation dialog for a Clinic proposal; a Guardian proposal is read-only with an explicit Phase 1 limitation note;
  - professional reschedule route `/professional/clinics/[clinicId]/appointments/[appointmentId]/reschedule`: bound to the confirmed revision sequence the detail displayed (stops with "the appointment has changed" otherwise); only server-returned candidates with server-authored ends; the required reason with its Guardian-visibility helper text; a confirmation dialog showing current versus proposed; one submission, never retried;
  - Guardian Appointment detail: a separate proposal section (current and proposed intervals, the Clinic's reason, a note that doing nothing keeps the confirmed time) and "Aceptar nuevo horario / Accept proposed time" with a confirmation dialog; every outcome re-reads. Push navigation for `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_PROPOSED` is wired to this screen through the existing `appointment_id + pet_id` payload;
  - Guardian Appointment list: a compact "Propuesta de cambio de horario / Proposed time change" indicator for an actionable pending proposal, alongside the unchanged lifecycle badge and confirmed time, also in the accessibility label; presentation only (the data already came from `get_pet_appointments`, SPEC-APT-011 BR-32);
  - schedule cards are unchanged and stay navigation-only.
- Refinements made after device validation feedback (all included in the physically validated build, except where listed as unverified below):
  - candidate-load errors use their own copy ("could not load available times"), separate from proposal-submission errors;
  - candidates are read in consecutive 60-date paging windows ("Later dates" / "Earlier dates", never before Clinic-local today) — a per-request span, not a Professional business horizon;
  - lost-response and no-longer-pending outcomes are reconciled against a fresh read and shown neutrally (a committed proposal returns to the detail; a proposal already gone after a withdrawal is shown as no longer pending), with a neutral "rescheduling is no longer available" state instead of "not found";
  - Android Fabric post-submit fix: leaving the reschedule screen is terminal with a single owner, so the popped screen is never re-rendered (it previously crashed with "The specified child already has a parent");
  - the Guardian list indicator above;
  - "Enviar propuesta / Send proposal" stays disabled until a server-returned candidate time is selected and the reason has non-whitespace content (and while loading or submitting) — a client-side UX guard for this form only; the backend keeps all validation.
- Automated mobile validation passes: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (47 suites, 890 tests). No dependency, native, or config change was required. Expo Router typed routes: a clean checkout typechecks without the git-ignored `.expo/types`, so starting Metro is not a prerequisite; an existing checkout whose ignored `.expo/types/router.d.ts` predates the new route may report a route type error until Metro (`npx expo start`) regenerates it.
- Physically validated by the user on a real Android device:
  - Professional: opening a future `CONFIRMED` Appointment and the reschedule flow; candidate dates and times loading; paging with "Later dates" / "Earlier dates", including dates beyond the first 60-day window (no global horizon); selecting a new date and time with the confirmed duration preserved; the required reason; "Send proposal" disabled until a valid time and a non-whitespace reason are present, and disabled again when either is removed; the confirmation dialog explaining that the confirmed time stays active until a Guardian accepts; successful submission, including a re-test after the Android Fabric fix;
  - after the proposal: the Appointment stays `CONFIRMED` at its unchanged confirmed time; the professional detail shows the pending proposal and the professional schedule its indicator; the Guardian list shows "Propuesta de cambio de horario / Proposed time change" with the normal lifecycle badge and confirmed time; the Guardian detail shows the current and proposed intervals, the Clinic's reason, and Accept;
  - Guardian acceptance: the same Appointment stays `CONFIRMED`, the proposed interval becomes the current confirmed interval, the pending proposal disappears, the professional detail and schedule show the new time, and the Guardian list no longer shows the indicator;
  - Clinic withdrawal: the pending proposal is removed without cancelling or changing the Appointment, disappears from the Guardian's view, and frees the slot so the Clinic can propose again immediately;
  - the proposal email Communication was observed after a Clinic proposal was created.
- Not physically verified (covered only by the automated SQL/Jest tests where applicable): push delivery and push navigation for `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_PROPOSED` specifically; a real stale Clinic proposal from two concurrent sessions or devices; real lost-response timing after a server commit; a Clinical Encounter created while a proposal is pending; and the multi-session concurrency harness, which remains outside current project conventions.
- Production: not deployed and not touched; the historical-duration diagnostic has not been run there. (That diagnostic belonged to the superseded rule that preserved the base confirmed duration; under the unified Standard Appointment Duration rule it is no longer required — see "Unified Standard Appointment Duration rule".)

### Appointment stream — Reschedule Proposal normalization (SPEC-APT-005 BR-31/BR-33, SPEC-APT-006 BR-34, SPEC-APT-016)

- Status: Migration 1 normalization is complete and validated in DEVELOPMENT. Production is untouched.
- DEVELOPMENT: before applying, the user ran a read-only preflight mirroring every assumption of the migration (base revisions, duplicated acceptance facts, sourced revisions, recorded non-actionable facts, time assumptions, PENDING uniqueness after normalization, withdrawal facts, and migration prerequisites); it returned `READY` with zero failing checks. The user then applied migration `20261009090000_reschedule_proposal_normalization.sql` to DEVELOPMENT, and the existing DEVELOPMENT proposal data migrated cleanly.
- Physical regression in DEVELOPMENT, performed by the user on Android after applying the migration, passed: existing and pending proposals render; the Guardian and professional pending indicators appear; Guardian acceptance works and the Appointment remains `CONFIRMED` at the accepted new time; Clinic withdrawal works and a new proposal can be made immediately afterwards; the proposal and acceptance Communications are produced; and the Android Fabric post-submit navigation fix shows no regression.
- Behaviour-preserving normalization of the Reschedule Proposal entity; no product change. The model is now structurally state-neutral, but Migration 1 still restricts proposal bases to `CONFIRMED` (`CHECK (base_revision_context = 'CONFIRMED')`); no REQUESTED alternative-time behaviour exists.
- Specifications and model: SPEC-APT-005 (BR-27 wording, BR-31 rewritten, new BR-33, §4, VN 30–32), SPEC-APT-006 (BR-33, new BR-34, VN 27), SPEC-APT-016 (BR-4, BR-5), `docs/RELATIONAL_MODEL.md`, and `docs/DOMAIN.md` describe the state-neutral entity and its states.
- Schema:
  - states `PENDING | ACCEPTED | WITHDRAWN | VOID`; `PENDING` is a stored invariant (base is the current revision, lifecycle equals the base context, no Clinical Encounter); a derived lapse (base or proposed start passed, proposer authority lost) is not stored;
  - removed `accepted_at`, `accepted_by_user_id`, `accepting_context`, `resulting_confirmed_revision_id` (acceptance facts are the resulting revision's `created_at`/`established_by_user_id` and the counterparty rule) and `non_actionable_at`/`non_actionable_reason` (replaced by `voided_at`/`void_reason`);
  - added `base_revision_context`, composite foreign keys (Proposal → base revision on `(id, appointment_id, revision_context)`; revision → source Proposal on `(id, appointment_id, base_revision_context)`) with their superkeys, and CHECKs per state;
  - `UNIQUE (appointment_id) WHERE state = 'PENDING'` replaces the previous pending index;
  - removed the source-Proposal context trigger, the REQUESTED-no-proposal CHECK (both implied by the composite key and the CONFIRMED-only base CHECK), `appointment_schedule_revisions_latest_idx` (redundant with the `(appointment_id, revision_sequence)` unique index), and `resolve_clinic_appointment_interval` (no caller).
- Invalidation: one internal `void_open_reschedule_proposal`, invoked by immediate triggers on Schedule Revision insert (`BASE_REVISION_SUPERSEDED`), lifecycle change (`APPOINTMENT_LIFECYCLE_CHANGED`), and Clinical Encounter insert (`CLINICAL_ENCOUNTER_STARTED`); proposal creation keeps the lazy `PROPOSED_TIME_PASSED`/`PROPOSING_AUTHORITY_LOST`. Every VOID reason is verified when written. Reads never write.
- Acceptance marks the Proposal `ACCEPTED` before inserting its single resulting revision; `appointment_reschedule_proposal_is_actionable` lost its third parameter and now evaluates only the derived lapse. `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_ACCEPTED` is enqueued by the resulting revision insert (same recipients, `created_at`, uniqueness).
- Public contracts unchanged: every RPC signature and every read's output columns (`get_appointment_scheduling_history` derives its acceptance columns). No mobile code was required. `get_pet_appointments` and `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` now look up the open Proposal per Appointment through the normalized PENDING index; in a local benchmark (25,000-Appointment fixture) the Clinic read of 5,000 Appointments went from 2,500 to 500 actionability evaluations and from ~660 ms to ~120–180 ms.
- Migration preflight aborts unless every removed or transformed fact agrees with its replacement; existing recorded non-actionable rows become `VOID` with their reason and time, and permanently dead `PENDING` rows become `VOID` observed at migration time. A local backfill check on an old-schema fixture (every legacy shape) and the abort path on corrupted acceptance facts were both exercised; neither harness is source-controlled.
- LOCAL validation: clean `supabase db reset`; 17 SQL suites pass with zero retained rows, including the new rollback-only `supabase/tests/reschedule_proposal_normalization.sql`; `confirmed_appointment_reschedule_spec_apt_005_006_016_017.sql`, `communications_slice14.sql`, `appointment_relevant_scheduled_interval.sql`, and `clinic_appointment_schedule_pet_name_spec_apt_012.sql` were updated only where their direct fixtures or assertions referenced removed columns or the previous representation of the same facts. 21 of 21 deliberate fault/mutation variants caught. Mobile: no change required; `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, and `npx jest` (47 suites, 890 tests) pass. `git diff --check` is clean. No SQL suite has been recorded as run against the remote DEVELOPMENT database.
- Deferred: `user_has_continuous_clinic_reschedule_authority_since` still hard-codes its responsibility list instead of reusing the capability mapping (separate authorization cleanup); REQUESTED Appointment alternative time (a later migration, not designed or specified yet); the physically unverified edge cases of the confirmed-reschedule slice listed in its section.

### Mobile — Shared Appointment candidate picker

- Candidate date/time presentation and selection logic is shared by the Guardian request, Guardian `REQUESTED` reschedule, and Clinic `CONFIRMED` reschedule screens (`CandidateDateTimePicker`, `CandidateWindowNavigator`, `src/lib/candidate-selection.ts`). Each flow keeps its own candidate read, window policy, loading/empty/error states, and form rules.
- Guardian request and `REQUESTED` reschedule now expose the full already-approved read horizon (SPEC-APT-014 BR-11, Clinic-local today through today + 60) in two windows — today + 0…29, then today + 30…60 via "Later dates" — instead of only the first 30 days. The Clinic reschedule keeps its unlimited 60-date paging.
- Backend contracts are unchanged; no migration.
- REQUESTED Alternative Time was specified (SPEC-APT-018, SPEC-APT-019, SPEC-APT-020) but not implemented at that point; it is now implemented, in DEVELOPMENT, and physically validated on Android for its core workflow (next section).

### Appointment stream — REQUESTED Appointment Alternative Time (SPEC-APT-018, SPEC-APT-019, SPEC-APT-020)

- Implemented: REQUESTED Alternative Time Phase 1 is fully implemented (migration, SQL regression suite, Communications template, mobile data layer and screens).
- Automated validation: all relevant local automated validation is green (details under "LOCAL validation" below).
- DEVELOPMENT: the user deployed the `communications-dispatch` Edge Function and applied migration `20261010090000_requested_appointment_alternative_time.sql` to DEVELOPMENT; the core Android workflow has been physically validated there by the user (details below).
- Push: covered by the automated SQL and Deno tests only; physical push delivery and push navigation for `APPOINTMENT_ALTERNATIVE_TIME_PROPOSED` remain unverified on a device (notifications were disabled on the test device). This does not block closing the slice.
- Production: not deployed and not touched.
- Workflow: the Clinic proposes another time for a future `REQUESTED` Appointment (the request stays `REQUESTED` at the Guardian's time); a current Guardian accepts it (a new `REQUESTED` revision with exactly the frozen interval, sourced from the Proposal, established by the Guardian; still `REQUESTED`, no Lifecycle Event, no Communication); the Clinic later confirms that revision through the unchanged stale-safe confirmation.
- Forward-only migration `20261010090000_requested_appointment_alternative_time.sql`:
  - same unified `appointment_reschedule_proposals`: `base_revision_context` may now be `REQUESTED` or `CONFIRMED`, and a new CHECK keeps a `REQUESTED` base Clinic-originated; one PENDING Proposal per Appointment, the four states, the eager/lazy VOID machinery, and `source_proposal_id` provenance are unchanged;
  - `propose_requested_appointment_alternative_time(p_appointment_id uuid, p_start_local_date date, p_start_local_time time, p_start_utc_offset_seconds integer, p_reason text, p_expected_requested_revision_sequence integer) returns uuid` — Clinic only, `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE` plus the `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT` Entitlement, no publication check, required reason, authorization → lock → lifecycle → stale ordering with a stable `P0001` "Stale requested Appointment schedule revision for alternative time proposal" and zero writes, the current standard duration required and frozen, same-start rejected, Clinic/Veterinarian feasibility through the shared helpers;
  - `accept_requested_appointment_alternative_time(p_proposal_id uuid) returns uuid` — Guardian only, Guardianship proven before any proposal or lifecycle state is disclosed, no Entitlement gate, no stale token, feasibility revalidated;

  - `get_pet_appointments` and `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` append `current_schedule_source_proposal_id` and `current_schedule_source_proposing_context` (SPEC-APT-011 BR-33, SPEC-APT-012 BR-45); the pending-proposal projection is unchanged and generic;
  - `APPOINTMENT_ALTERNATIVE_TIME_PROPOSED`: type and origin-shape constraints, a per-Proposal-and-recipient unique index, the existing Proposal reference (no new column), an origin-integrity rule tying each Proposal-originated type to its base context, enqueueing on a `REQUESTED`-base Clinic Proposal, delivery only while actionable, and the proposed interval in the delivery context;
  - unchanged: `withdraw_appointment_reschedule_proposal` (already base-context neutral), the confirmed propose/accept RPCs (both already reject the other workflow), Guardian reschedule, confirmation, cancellation, and expiry (the existing triggers VOID a pending alternative with `BASE_REVISION_SUPERSEDED` or `APPOINTMENT_LIFECYCLE_CHANGED`).

La cita aún no está confirmada."; push: the same request-not-confirmed wording without Clinic or time. `APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_PROPOSED`/`APPOINTMENT_RESCHEDULE_ACCEPTED` wording is unchanged.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`):

  - Professional: "Proponer otro horario / Propose another time" on the detail of an eligible future `REQUESTED` Appointment; the new route `/professional/clinics/[clinicId]/appointments/[appointmentId]/alternative-time` (shared candidate picker and window navigator, unlimited 60-date paging, required reason with its Guardian-visibility hint, Send disabled until a currently offered time and a non-whitespace reason, a confirmation dialog stating that the Appointment stays requested until a Guardian accepts and the Clinic confirms, the reviewed requested sequence submitted, no retry, terminal single-owner leave); a pending-alternative card with the current and proposed intervals, the reason, "awaiting guardian response", and Withdraw; "the guardian accepted the proposed time — awaiting confirmation" after acceptance, with Confirm still offered; a Confirm dialog warning that confirming discards a pending alternative;
