### Appointment stream — Lightweight Appointment completion (SPEC-APT-009, Migration 1)

CLOSED as a checkpoint. Implemented; validated LOCALLY; migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user; the corresponding mobile build physically validated by the user on Android against DEVELOPMENT for the core workflow. Production untouched. Post-completion Clinical Encounter creation (Migration 2) was not part of this checkpoint; it has since been implemented and applied to DEVELOPMENT (see the next section).

- Specifications: the amended, APPROVED SPEC-APT-009 (completion is the Clinic's administrative assertion that the visit occurred; no Veterinarian Assignment, Clinical Encounter, clinical documentation, or Entitlement is required), SPEC-APT-010 §6/§8, SPEC-APT-012 BR-43/BR-46, SPEC-MED-001 BR-35 (the Encounter is the source of truth for its Appointment relationship), SPEC-ACC-001 BR-42 and SPEC-ACC-002 BR-38/BR-55 (completion does not change the Care Authorization), DOMAIN.md, and RELATIONAL_MODEL.md.
- Scope boundary: this is Migration 1 only. Establishing a Clinical Encounter for an already `COMPLETED` Appointment (SPEC-MED-001 BR-34, Migration 2) was NOT implemented by this checkpoint: `assert_clinical_encounter_establishment` still required a `CONFIRMED` Appointment. It has since been implemented by Migration 2 and applied to DEVELOPMENT (see the next section).
- Forward-only migration `20261011090000_lightweight_appointment_completion.sql` (applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user; not applied to Production):
  - drops `complete_appointment(uuid, uuid)` (the Encounter-evidence form; not kept as an overload) and creates `complete_appointment(p_appointment_id uuid, p_expected_confirmed_revision_sequence integer) returns uuid` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, `VOLATILE`, empty `search_path`, `EXECUTE` for `authenticated` only), mirroring stale-safe NO_SHOW: a NULL or non-positive sequence is `22023` before any Appointment state is read; `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETE` under the existing Organization → Clinic lock, with no Entitlement gate; Appointment row lock; `CONFIRMED` (lifecycle precedes staleness); the latest `CONFIRMED` revision must have the expected sequence, otherwise plain `P0001` `Stale confirmed Appointment schedule revision` with zero writes; that revision's start must have been reached; then one `CONFIRMED → COMPLETED` Lifecycle Event (Clinic actor, acting User, effective = recorded = operation time, no Encounter reference) and the `COMPLETED` state. It returns the Appointment id, like confirmation, cancellation, and NO_SHOW. It reads or writes no Veterinarian Assignment, Clinical Encounter, or Care Authorization; an existing Encounter does not block it. A pending Reschedule Proposal becomes `VOID` (`APPOINTMENT_LIFECYCLE_CHANGED`) and the existing `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETED` Communications are enqueued by the existing lifecycle triggers;
  - Lifecycle Event → Encounter becomes legacy provenance: `appointment_lifecycle_events_encounter_shape_check` is now `to_state = 'COMPLETED' OR clinical_encounter_id IS NULL`, and a new BEFORE INSERT trigger (`appointment_lifecycle_events_no_new_encounter_reference`) rejects any Encounter reference on a new Event. Events were already immutable, so historical COMPLETED Events keep their reference; the column is not dropped;
  - the deferred `assert_appointment_lifecycle_coherence`, `assert_appointment_lifecycle_event_provenance`, and `assert_clinical_encounter_appointment_coherence` no longer treat an Encounter as completion evidence. For a `COMPLETED` Appointment they now require: a legacy reference, where present, to identify an Encounter of the same Appointment, Pet, and Clinic; every Appointment-backed Encounter to have started no later than the completion Event; an Encounter's Pet, Clinic, and Care Authorization to match its `COMPLETED` Appointment and agree with any legacy reference; and (provenance, Encounter-less Events) the completion Event not to precede the latest confirmed start. Without the third change, an Encounter established before an Encounter-less completion would fail its deferred check on its next UPDATE (for example conclusion). Every other lifecycle, NO_SHOW, cancellation, and `EXPIRED` rule is carried over verbatim;
  - unchanged: an Encounter still blocks NO_SHOW, Clinic and Guardian cancellation, and rescheduling/proposals; no table, column, or index was added or removed, and no existing row changed.
- Encounter uniqueness audit (requested while working in this area): "at most one Encounter per Care Authorization" has no unique constraint. It is protected by `create_clinical_encounter` locking the Care Authorization row (`FOR UPDATE`, after the Organization → Clinic → professional-context locks) before its existence check, with clients having no table privileges. A real two-session LOCAL race on one Care Authorization confirmed it: the second session blocked until the first committed, then was rejected (`This Care Authorization already established a Clinical Encounter`), leaving exactly one Encounter. Not a blocking issue.
- LOCAL validation:
  - a clean `supabase db reset` applies every migration;
  - the new rollback-only suite `supabase/tests/appointment_completion_spec_apt_009.sql` passes with `ON_ERROR_STOP`: contract, removed signature, and ACL; the legacy-only Encounter-reference shape; unauthenticated/incoherent callers; invalid sequences before lookup; authorization by responsibility and scope (OWNER, Org/Clinic ADMINISTRATOR, Org/Clinic RECEPTIONIST, and a VETERINARIAN only through a current Clinic Assignment; other-Clinic actors, ended Membership or Grant, current and former Guardians denied with zero writes); success under PENDING, PAST_DUE, EXPIRED, and no Subscription; lifecycle rejections; the time rule (before, at, between, after; latest accepted reschedule); stale `P0001` with zero writes, including same-start, end-only, and timezone-only revisions, then refresh-then-succeed; a current Veterinarian Assignment, an `ACTIVE` Encounter, and a `CONCLUDED` Encounter neither blocking nor changing; Draft creation, finalization, and Encounter conclusion after completion with deferred coherence enforced; the exact Event shape, Appointment id returned, Care Authorization left `ACTIVE`, one `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETED` Communication per current Guardian (none for the former Guardian or the Clinic actor), both reads showing `COMPLETED` with the unchanged confirmed revision; pending-proposal `VOID`; serialized races (double completion, NO_SHOW or cancellation first, completion first); and historical compatibility (a valid legacy Encounter-referencing completion accepted and its Encounter still concludable; an incompatible legacy reference, an Encounter disagreeing with a legacy reference, care starting after completion, and an Encounter-less completion before the confirmed start each rejected; no new Event, COMPLETED or otherwise, may reference an Encounter; historical references immutable);
  - 19 of 19 SQL suites pass with zero retained synthetic rows. Four existing suites were updated: `appointment_cancellation_spec_apt_003.sql` and `appointment_no_show_spec_apt_010.sql` now complete through the new stale-safe signature (the cancellation suite's "completion wins first" fixture moved to a past start, since completion now requires the start to have been reached; its Encounter is kept and no longer blocks completion); `appointment_request_expiry_spec_apt_015.sql`'s future, early-care Appointment now asserts that completion is rejected before the confirmed start (SPEC-APT-009 BR-7) instead of completing; and `communications_slice14.sql` no longer builds an Encounter chain only to satisfy the old shape constraint, since its completion Event now carries no Encounter reference. No unrelated assertion was weakened;
  - 17 of 17 deliberate faults were caught by the new suite (stale check, invalid-sequence validation, start rule, `CONFIRMED` check, Encounter evidence required again, Veterinarian Assignment required, Care Authorization changed, Entitlement gate added, wrong capability, wrong actor context, the three relaxed coherence checks each reverted or removed, the two provenance checks removed, the insert-time trigger dropped, and the old shape check restored). The trigger-dropped, shape-reverted, and Care-Authorization faults were re-run with the suite's metadata assertion removed and were still caught behaviorally;
  - real two-session LOCAL checks (committed local fixtures, then a clean reset): two concurrent completions produced one completion and a lifecycle rejection; an Encounter concluded in a committed transaction after an Encounter-less completion passed the deferred checks at COMMIT;
  - Communications: no template or Edge Function change; the existing `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETED` wording ("La cita de … ha finalizado") makes no clinical claim. The Communications Deno tests were not re-run because no Communications function or template changed in this slice;
  - `git diff --check` is clean.
- Mobile (`apps/mobile/`):
  - `src/data/appointment-completion.ts`: typed `completeAppointment(appointmentId, expectedConfirmedRevisionSequence)` calling `complete_appointment` with exactly `p_appointment_id` and `p_expected_confirmed_revision_sequence`; local refusal of malformed input; `28000` normalized; no retry;
  - `src/i18n/appointment-completion-error-messages.ts`: session expired, capability lost, stale schedule, lifecycle changed, start not reached, and a generic fallback; raw backend text never shown;
  - `src/lib/clinic-schedule-display.ts`: `offersAppointmentCompletion` (a `CONFIRMED` Appointment whose start has been reached on the advisory device clock, at a Clinic whose known capabilities hold `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETE`; Veterinarian Assignment and Encounter are deliberately not consulted), `completionRequestFor` (the displayed row's exact confirmed revision), and `isAppointmentNowCompleted` (completed-state reconciliation);
  - professional Appointment detail: "Marcar como completada / Mark as completed" beside "Marcar como no presentado / Mark as no-show" for a past `CONFIRMED` Appointment; when both are offered, a short hint explains completed (the visit took place) versus no-show (the Pet did not attend), Complete is the primary button, and No-show becomes secondary. A native confirmation dialog shows the Pet, date, and time and states that no Veterinarian assignment or clinical record is needed. One in-flight mutation shared with the other actions; the reviewed revision is submitted; every outcome re-reads; a failed call whose re-read shows the Appointment `COMPLETED` (a lost response or another actor) is reported neutrally as "already completed"; the screen stays (no navigation). A Veterinarian whose capabilities hold `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETE` but not `APPOINTMENT_MARK_NO_SHOW` sees only Complete;
  - Guardian reads and screens are unchanged; they already render `COMPLETED`;
  - automated validation: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, and `npx jest` (53 suites, 1049 tests) pass. No dependency, native, or config change; no new route; no new development build required.
- DEVELOPMENT: the user applied `20261011090000_lightweight_appointment_completion.sql` to DEVELOPMENT and installed the corresponding mobile build (migration first, because the build calls the new two-argument `complete_appointment`). Because the Clinic had no Sunday Availability, the user prepared the test Appointment with a manual DEVELOPMENT-only SQL fixture that went through the real domain operations — a temporary one-day OPEN Clinic Appointment Availability exception, a Guardian requested-time reschedule, and a stale-safe Clinic confirmation, in separate transactions — with no constraint or trigger bypassed and no Veterinarian Assignment or Clinical Encounter created.
- Physically validated by the user on a real Android device against DEVELOPMENT (all passed):
  - future `CONFIRMED`: before the confirmed scheduled start, "Marcar como completada / Mark as completed" is not offered;
  - start reached: once the confirmed scheduled start is reached, Complete appears, No-show appears for a professional holding that capability, and the wording and hint clearly distinguish Complete (the visit took place) from No-show (the visit did not take place);
  - lightweight completion: a `CONFIRMED` Appointment with no Veterinarian Assignment and no Clinical Encounter was completed successfully;
  - result: the lifecycle became `COMPLETED`; the Professional sees it in History; the Guardian sees it as `COMPLETED`; the Guardian UI does not imply that a Clinical Encounter, report, or medical record exists; and the `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETED` Communication was received;
  - pending confirmed-reschedule proposal at the start: a `CONFIRMED` Appointment had a pending reschedule proposal. When the Appointment's confirmed scheduled start was reached, the pending proposal automatically stopped being actionable and disappeared from the UI before Complete was performed. This is the expected behaviour, not a Complete failure: proposal actionability requires the base confirmed start to still be in the future (SPEC-APT-005 BR-31), so the proposal lapses when that start is reached, and Complete is not what removes it from the UI. The subsequent `COMPLETED` transition records the permanent invalidation (`VOID`, `APPOINTMENT_LIFECYCLE_CHANGED`) through the existing normalized lifecycle machinery, which the SQL suite verifies; that stored `VOID` was not inspected physically. This is recorded as physical confirmation that expired-at-start proposal actionability behaves correctly.
- Not physically validated (automated coverage only; neither blocks closing this checkpoint):
  - stale completion from two live sessions: covered by the automated stale and concurrency regression tests (stale `P0001` with zero writes, serialized races, and a real two-session LOCAL completion race);
  - an existing Clinical Encounter → Complete → later Encounter conclusion: not practical on a device because no Encounter UI exists yet; covered by the SQL suite and by a committed LOCAL transaction that exercised the deferred checks at COMMIT.
- Not part of this checkpoint: establishing a Clinical Encounter for an already `COMPLETED` Appointment (SPEC-MED-001 BR-34) — since implemented as Migration 2 and applied to DEVELOPMENT (next section).
- Production: NOT updated. Neither the migration nor this mobile behavior has been released to Production.

### Clinical stream — Clinical Encounter after Appointment completion (SPEC-MED-001 BR-6/BR-34/BR-36, Migration 2)

CLOSED as a backend checkpoint: implemented and validated in DEVELOPMENT. Validated LOCALLY; migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user; the backend behaviour is available there. Physical device validation is deferred until an Encounter UI exists. Production untouched.

- Specifications: the APPROVED SPEC-MED-001 BR-6 (the responsible Clinic Assignment is current and began no later than the care start), BR-34 (an Appointment-backed Encounter for a `CONFIRMED` or a legitimately confirmed `COMPLETED` Appointment, care starting between the confirmation and, once completed, the completion), BR-35, and BR-36 (establishment never changes the Appointment); SPEC-ACC-001 BR-41/BR-42 and SPEC-ACC-002 BR-55 (an `ACTIVE` Care Authorization of a `COMPLETED` Appointment supports at most one Encounter and its documentation; Guardian revocation blocks it); DOMAIN.md and RELATIONAL_MODEL.md. All three paths — `CONFIRMED → COMPLETED`, `CONFIRMED → Encounter → COMPLETED`, and `CONFIRMED → COMPLETED → Encounter later` — are now valid.
- Forward-only migration `20261012090000_clinical_encounter_after_appointment_completion.sql` (applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user; not applied to Production) replaces only `assert_clinical_encounter_establishment`, the BEFORE INSERT establishment rule on `public.clinical_encounters`:
  - the Appointment may be `CONFIRMED` or `COMPLETED`; `REQUESTED`, `CANCELLED`, `NO_SHOW`, and `EXPIRED` stay rejected;
  - a legitimate confirmation is proven by history (exactly one `REQUESTED → CONFIRMED` Event, care start at or after it), and a `COMPLETED` Appointment additionally needs exactly one `CONFIRMED → COMPLETED` Event with the care start at or before its effective time — a `COMPLETED` state alone is not trusted;
  - for every Encounter (Appointment-backed or Clinical Intake), the responsible Veterinarian Clinic Assignment must be current at establishment and must have begun at or before `started_at`. This also closes the pre-existing gap where a Veterinarian assigned at 15:00 could record care as having begun at 10:00 on a `CONFIRMED` Appointment. No fallback to an ended Assignment; no historical-authority reconstruction;
  - `created_at` may be any time after the completion (the delay is in documentation, not care); there is no documentation window;
  - every other establishment rule is carried over verbatim. `create_clinical_encounter(p_care_authorization_id uuid, p_started_at timestamptz, p_appointment_id uuid)` keeps its signature and body: the Veterinarian context, `CLINICAL_CARE` capability, and `CLINICAL_CARE` Entitlement are evaluated at documentation time; the Care Authorization must be `ACTIVE`, `APPOINTMENT_CARE`, of this Appointment; Migration 1's deferred coherence already accepted an Encounter of a `COMPLETED` Appointment and is unchanged;
  - establishing an Encounter writes no Appointment state, Lifecycle Event, Schedule Revision, or Communication, and does not change the Care Authorization; an Encounter still blocks NO_SHOW, cancellation, and rescheduling;
  - the legacy Lifecycle Event Encounter reference is unchanged (historical rows only; new Events carry none); no table, column, index, constraint, trigger, grant, or existing row changes.
- Encounter uniqueness: still guaranteed by `create_clinical_encounter` locking the Appointment and then the Care Authorization row (`FOR UPDATE`) before its one-Encounter-per-Authorization check; there is no unique constraint and clients have no table privileges. Re-confirmed below by a real two-session race on a `COMPLETED` Appointment.
- LOCAL validation:
  - a clean `supabase db reset` applies every migration;
  - the new rollback-only suite `supabase/tests/clinical_encounter_after_completion_spec_med_001.sql` passes with `ON_ERROR_STOP`: the unchanged contract; `CONFIRMED` regression; a real `complete_appointment` followed by an Encounter for care at 10:07 created later, with the Appointment, Lifecycle Events, Schedule Revisions, Care Authorization, and Communications unchanged and no Encounter reference added to the completion Event; Draft creation, update, finalization, Encounter conclusion, and a Clinical Amendment afterwards; a second Encounter rejected; care starting exactly at the confirmation or completion accepted, before the confirmation (for `CONFIRMED` and `COMPLETED`), after the completion, or in the future rejected; the Assignment rule (an Assignment begun earlier accepted; begun after the care start, ended, or re-assigned later rejected; also for a `CONFIRMED` Appointment; the rule rejects an ended Assignment even on a direct trusted insert); Administrator, Receptionist (even with a Profile and Clinic Assignment), and Guardian lacking the Veterinarian context, and a Veterinarian without the Entitlement; another Appointment's authorization and Pet/Clinic/Organization mismatches; a Guardian revocation after completion blocking creation (and the rule rejecting a revoked authorization on a direct insert); `REQUESTED`, `EXPIRED`, `CANCELLED`, and `NO_SHOW` rejected, including the lifecycle rule on its own; a `COMPLETED` state without a completion or a confirmation Event rejected; Encounter-blocks-NO_SHOW/cancel; Encounter-then-Complete and Complete-then-Encounter; NO_SHOW, cancellation, or revocation first refusing the Encounter; no Appointment Communication from Encounters; and a legacy Encounter-referencing completion staying valid, admitting no second Encounter, and concludable;
  - 20 of 20 SQL suites pass with zero retained synthetic rows; no existing suite needed changes (the new lifecycle message keeps the previous wording as its prefix);
  - 10 of 10 deliberate faults were caught by behavioral assertions: any lifecycle state allowed, `CONFIRMED`-only restored (the pre-migration behaviour), completion upper bound omitted, confirmation lower bound omitted, Assignment start bound omitted, current-Assignment requirement skipped in the establishment rule and separately in the professional-context resolver, Care Authorization activity skipped (operation and rule), duplicate-Encounter protection skipped, and the completion-history proof skipped. Two were detected through a defense-in-depth layer with a different rejection rather than an acceptance (the omitted upper bound by Migration 1's deferred coherence; the resolver fault by the capability check);
  - real two-session LOCAL races (committed fixtures, then a clean reset): Encounter vs Encounter on a `COMPLETED` Appointment (the second blocked, then `This Care Authorization already established a Clinical Encounter`; exactly one row); Guardian revocation first vs a retrospective Encounter (the Encounter blocked, then rejected); Encounter first vs NO_SHOW and vs Clinic cancellation (each blocked, then rejected for the existing Encounter). Committed sequences also passed their deferred checks at COMMIT: Complete then a later Encounter; Encounter, then Complete, then conclusion;

### Appointment stream — Unified Standard Appointment Duration rule (SPEC-APT-005, SPEC-APT-006, SPEC-APT-013, SPEC-APT-017, SPEC-APT-018, SPEC-APT-020)

CLOSED: the unified Standard Appointment Duration rule is implemented; migration `20261015090000_unified_standard_appointment_duration.sql` has been applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user; the core `CONFIRMED` reschedule Android flow has been physically validated there; automated regression and DST coverage are green; the old duration model has been removed from live code; existing proposals are compatible. Production untouched.

- Rule (SPEC-APT-013 BR-68): every operation that constructs a new Appointment interval uses the Clinic Standard Appointment Duration current at that operation and freezes the resulting interval. Operations that adopt an existing interval use it exactly and do not re-read Standard Appointment Duration. Configuration changes never rewrite existing Schedule Revisions or pending Reschedule Proposals.
  - CREATE: Guardian request, Guardian requested reschedule, Clinic Alternative Time, Guardian or Clinic confirmed-reschedule proposal, and the candidate reads offering starts for them.
  - ADOPT: Clinic confirmation, Alternative Time acceptance, confirmed-reschedule acceptance.
  - The earlier `CONFIRMED`-specific rule that preserved the elapsed duration of the base confirmed revision (recorded in the confirmed-reschedule section above) is superseded.
- Simplification and debt reduction — an architectural outcome of this change, not a side effect:
  - one duration source for every CREATE operation (previously two: the standard duration, or the base confirmed elapsed duration);
  - one no-duration rule (previously `REQUESTED` was blocked while `CONFIRMED` was allowed);
  - the base-duration helper `appointment_interval_duration_microseconds` and the historical-duration branches in the confirmed proposal and its candidate read are removed;
  - the historical-duration special cases are gone: durations outside 10–240 minutes or not whole minutes, sub-minute candidate cadence, and "a duration of 24 hours or more yields no candidates";
  - the legacy-duration tests and the old "keeps its current duration" copy and comments are removed or updated;
  - Proposal and Schedule Revision integrity are unchanged: they were already duration-neutral (frozen start/end, exact adoption, immutability).
- Forward-only migration `20261015090000_unified_standard_appointment_duration.sql` (functions only; no table, column, constraint, trigger, signature, or grant change):
  - `propose_confirmed_appointment_reschedule`: for both GUARDIAN and CLINIC, end = resolved start + current standard duration; `Clinic has no currently configured standard Appointment duration` (`23514`) when none is configured, checked after the lazy VOID and before the start is resolved (so the rejection rolls the VOID back), as in the REQUESTED Alternative Time path; the same start is still rejected whatever the duration;
  - `get_appointment_reschedule_candidate_start_times`: the current standard duration is the candidate length and cadence, and is required (same message);
  - `appointment_interval_duration_microseconds` dropped (no caller, trigger, view, or grant remained);
  - `appointment_reschedule_candidate_positions` kept unchanged: its microsecond step is a unit, not a policy; both Professional candidate reads pass the standard duration through it, and its microsecond arithmetic still anchors grids at Availability range starts stored as `time(6)`;
  - acceptance functions unchanged: they already adopt the frozen interval.
- Standard duration NULL: `CONFIRMED` proposal creation (Guardian and Clinic) and the Professional confirmed candidate read now reject when no standard duration is configured; acceptance of an already frozen proposal remains valid. Covered by automated tests; not physically validated. Mobile maps the rejection to calm copy (`professionalReschedule.noStandardDuration`, EN/ES).
- Existing proposals: stored proposals remain valid; no backfill was required; acceptance adopts their frozen interval unchanged.
- Mobile: the confirmed-reschedule hint says proposed times use the clinic's standard appointment duration (EN/ES); the no-duration mapping above; the Appointment hours duration intro and not-set hint mention proposed times; the data-layer contract comment and the parser fixtures are updated. No component, dependency, or native change.
- Automated validation (LOCAL):
  - clean `supabase db reset`; 23 of 23 SQL suites pass with `ON_ERROR_STOP` and zero retained rows;
  - `confirmed_appointment_reschedule_spec_apt_005_006_016_017.sql` covers the new rule: current standard duration for Clinic and Guardian proposals (including 30 → 45 and 60 → 30), candidate length and cadence, no standard duration rejecting both proposal contexts and the candidate read with zero writes, the frozen proposal surviving later standard-duration changes and removal and being accepted unchanged while the earlier revision keeps its interval, feasibility on the actual proposed end, DST elapsed time, same start rejected after a duration change, read/write round-trips, and the dropped helper's absence;
  - `requested_appointment_alternative_time_spec_apt_018_019_020.sql` now has dynamic DST coverage (SPEC-APT-018 VN 9) on the real propose, candidate, and accept path, with the next Europe/Madrid spring-forward and fall-back dates computed from the time-zone database (never hard-coded, never skipped);
  - 15 of 15 deliberate faults caught (base duration in proposal or candidates, NULL check removed or falling back to the base duration, Guardian-only NULL bypass, either acceptance re-reading the standard duration, same start allowed when the duration differs, a hard-coded duration, wall-clock end in either proposal path, a later standard change rewriting pending proposals, Alternative Time using the base duration). The wall-clock Alternative Time fault was missed by the previous suite and is now caught. The fault harness is not source-controlled, as before;
  - mobile: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (57 suites, 1,186 tests);
  - `git diff --check` clean;
  - a read-only post-refactor audit found no remaining live old-model behaviour; its only residue (one SQL test comment, two mobile parser fixture values, one historical status sentence) was cleaned without behaviour change.
- Physically validated by the user on a real Android device against DEVELOPMENT (`CONFIRMED` reschedule):
  1. a `CONFIRMED` Appointment existed under a 30-minute standard duration;
  2. the Clinic standard duration was changed to 45 minutes;
  3. the `CONFIRMED` reschedule screen reflected the new rule: 45-minute candidate cadence, 45-minute proposed interval, and the updated hint;
  4. the Clinic sent the proposal;
  5. before Guardian acceptance, the standard duration was changed back to 30 minutes;
  6. the Guardian still saw and accepted the frozen 45-minute proposal;
  7. the accepted `CONFIRMED` revision kept the 45-minute interval;
  8. the earlier 30-minute confirmed history remained unchanged;
  9. a subsequent new reschedule used the then-current 30-minute standard duration again.

  This physically confirms both halves of the rule: CREATE uses the current standard duration, and ADOPT establishes the frozen interval.
- Not physically validated: the NULL standard-duration case (automated only); REQUESTED Alternative Time under this change, whose regression and DST coverage are validated automatically (its earlier physical validation is recorded in its own section).
- Production: NOT deployed and not touched.

### Appointment stream — Derived Proposal current feasibility and Guardian current acceptability (SPEC-APT-005 BR-34/BR-35, SPEC-APT-006 BR-36, SPEC-APT-011 BR-32, SPEC-APT-012 BR-44, SPEC-APT-018/019 §5, SPEC-COM-003 BR-70/BR-72)

CLOSED: derived current scheduling feasibility and Guardian current acceptability are implemented; migration `20261016090000_reschedule_proposal_current_feasibility.sql` has been applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user; the REQUESTED Alternative Time and CONFIRMED reschedule core flows have been physically validated on Android there; proposal state and actionability are unchanged; Clinic withdrawal is preserved; acceptance remains authoritative; automated regression and performance validation are green. Production has not been deployed or touched.

- Model:
  - Proposal state: persisted state remains `PENDING | ACCEPTED | WITHDRAWN | VOID`. No new persisted state and no new VOID reason.
  - Actionability: `appointment_reschedule_proposal_is_actionable` is unchanged. Clinic Availability, the scheduling timezone, Veterinarian constraints, and Entitlement are not actionability conditions.
  - Current scheduling feasibility: the Clinic read `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` derives `pending_reschedule_currently_feasible` for the projected actionable PENDING proposal. It evaluates a valid current Clinic scheduling timezone and the proposal's frozen start/end against current Clinic Availability, the current Veterinarian Assignment and Availability where a Veterinarian is assigned, and current confirmed-booking conflicts of that Veterinarian — the same scheduling evaluation both acceptance operations apply, through the existing `appointment_reschedule_interval_infeasibility`. It reads neither the standard Appointment duration nor Entitlement, nor the proposal's stored timezone.
  - Guardian current acceptability: the Guardian read `get_pet_appointments` derives `pending_reschedule_currently_acceptable`: a Clinic proposal on a REQUESTED base → current scheduling feasibility; a Clinic proposal on a CONFIRMED base → current scheduling feasibility AND the Organization's current `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT` Entitlement (non-locking predicate); a Guardian-originated proposal → `false`. The value is generic and never reveals why acceptance is unavailable.
  - Both values are NULL when no proposal is projected, are derived at read time, and are never stored.
- Forward-only migration `20261016090000_reschedule_proposal_current_feasibility.sql` (read side only; no table, column, index, trigger, proposal state, or VOID reason): two internal helpers (`appointment_reschedule_proposal_is_currently_feasible(uuid, text)`, `appointment_reschedule_proposal_is_guardian_acceptable(uuid, text)`, not executable by `anon`/`authenticated`) and both reads dropped and re-created with one trailing column each, same grants, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `STABLE`, empty `search_path`, and every existing column and rule unchanged.
- Unchanged on purpose: actionability; withdrawal (an infeasible proposal stays projected, `PENDING`, in the one-PENDING slot, and withdrawable); both acceptance operations (still authoritative: they revalidate authority, lifecycle, Encounter, Entitlement for CONFIRMED, timezone, and feasibility; the read value is never trusted); the Entitlement policy.
- Communications: current feasibility and Guardian current acceptability are not delivery-eligibility conditions. Communication behaviour is unchanged; no new Communication type and no new suppression rule were introduced.
- Mobile: one strict shared parser for both reads, parameterized by each read's derived column — a boolean exactly when a proposal is present and null otherwise; anything else (missing, non-boolean, the other read's column) rejects the whole read, never defaulted to `true`. Guardian Accept (`offersRescheduleAcceptance`, `offersAlternativeTimeAcceptance`) additionally requires `currentlyAcceptable === true`; when it is `false` the proposal stays visible with generic copy (EN/ES): "This proposed time can't be accepted right now. Your appointment keeps its confirmed time." / "… Your request keeps its current time." The authoritative re-read after a failed acceptance is kept. Professional detail: an infeasible proposal stays visible with Withdraw and "This proposed time is no longer available." / "Este horario propuesto ya no está disponible." Guardian list and Professional schedule cards are unchanged.
- Compatibility: no proposal-table or schema column was added; no backfill was required; existing pending proposals immediately receive derived read values with their rows untouched; old mobile builds tolerate the extra read columns (the parsers read named fields only), while the new build requires them. Deployment order remains migration first, then the mobile build.
- Automated validation (LOCAL):
  - clean `supabase db reset`; 24 of 24 SQL suites pass with `ON_ERROR_STOP` and zero retained test rows;
  - the rollback-only suite `reschedule_proposal_current_feasibility_spec_apt_005_011_012.sql` covers, for both REQUESTED and CONFIRMED proposals: a `CLOSED` exception making feasibility false and clearing it restoring true; Veterinarian Availability loss and restoration; a competing `CONFIRMED` booking of the assigned Veterinarian appearing and disappearing; an ended Veterinarian Clinic Assignment; a missing or invalid current Clinic timezone (and a different valid one being the one evaluated, the stored timezone untouched); standard-duration changes and removal having no effect; the proposal remaining projected while infeasible; withdrawal succeeding while infeasible with the slot freed; acceptance rejected authoritatively while false with zero writes; successful acceptance of the same proposal once conditions recover; lapsed proposals absent rather than projected as false; a pre-existing proposal receiving derived values without backfill; and Communications remaining eligible while the proposal is actionable. A CONFIRMED proposal's Guardian acceptability follows the Organization's current `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT`, while the Clinic feasibility value does not change with it; a Guardian-originated proposal is `false` to the Guardian. Five existing suites were updated only for the appended result columns;
  - 15 of 15 new feasibility/acceptability deliberate faults caught behaviourally (read always acceptable; Clinic Availability or Veterinarian constraints ignored; stored timezone used; standard duration read; Entitlement ignored; Guardian proposal acceptable; infeasibility folded into actionability — also via a wrapper the static contract check cannot see; withdrawal blocked while infeasible; either acceptance skipping revalidation; a reason column exposed; derived value computed for a non-actionable proposal; notification suppressed while infeasible); the 15 of 15 unified-duration faults are still caught. The fault harness is not source-controlled, as before;
  - mobile: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (57 suites, 1,210 tests);
  - `git diff --check` clean.
- Read performance (local rollback-only benchmark, 5,000 Appointments at one Clinic, medians of 7 runs, noisy Docker-on-Windows timings): with no pending proposal no meaningful added cost (≈ 23 ms baseline); 3 pending proposals ≈ 41 ms before / 67 ms after; 20 pending proposals with a deliberately heavy Veterinarian-assignment fixture (2,000 assignments, half the proposals conflicted) ≈ 49 ms before / 86–137 ms after. An initial implementation repeated the timezone validation (`clinic_timezone_name_is_valid`, which scans `pg_timezone_names`) per proposal and measured ≈ 550 ms; it was corrected before validation, so timezone validity is now calculated once per Clinic with pending proposals, not once per proposal. The remaining cost is that one validation plus the existing per-proposal Veterinarian-conflict check; it is not a performance blocker.
- Physically validated by the user on a real Android device against DEVELOPMENT; all planned core device checks passed:
  - REQUESTED Alternative Time:
    1. the Clinic created an Alternative Time proposal;
    2. the Guardian initially saw the proposal with Accept;
    3. the Clinic added a `CLOSED` exception covering the proposed interval;
    4. the Guardian still saw the proposal, but Accept disappeared;
    5. the Guardian saw the generic "can't be accepted right now / your request keeps its current time" state;
    6. the Professional still saw the proposal;
    7. the Professional saw the unavailable-time notice;
    8. Withdraw remained available;
    9. clearing the exception made the same proposal feasible again;
    10. the Guardian's Accept reappeared;
    11. the Guardian successfully accepted the same proposal.
  - CONFIRMED reschedule:
    12. the Clinic created a confirmed-reschedule proposal;
    13. the Guardian initially saw Accept;
    14. closing the proposed interval made Accept disappear without removing the proposal;
    15. the existing confirmed Appointment remained unchanged;
    16. reopening the interval restored Accept;
    17. the Guardian successfully accepted the same proposal.
  - The optional checks — loss and restoration of `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT` changing CONFIRMED Guardian acceptability without disclosing the reason, a standard-duration change while a proposal is pending not affecting feasibility or acceptability, withdrawal while the proposal is infeasible, and unchanged notification delivery — were not individually reported as physically tested; they are covered by the automated suite above.
- UX result: physical validation confirms the intended distinction — a proposal can remain `PENDING` and visible; its current feasibility can flip false → true without any proposal-state change; Guardian Accept follows current acceptability; Professional Withdraw remains available; the acceptance RPC remains authoritative. The earlier repeated-failure UX (Accept still offered after an acceptance failed for an unavailable time) is resolved: after a re-read, a currently unacceptable proposal remains visible but no longer offers Accept.
- Still out of scope, unchanged: proposal replacement (atomic withdraw-and-replace), detailed infeasibility reasons, persisted feasibility state, new VOID reasons, and any change to the Entitlement policy, timezone policy, or standard-duration semantics.
- Production: NOT deployed and not touched.

### Known-debt closure pass (SPEC-APT-013 BR-58, SPEC-APT-018 BR-16, SPEC-APT-013 BR-74)

CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT: implemented and validated locally; the user applied both migrations (`20261017090000_guardian_candidate_grid_sub_minute_anchor.sql`, `20261017100000_requested_alternative_time_clinic_only_lazy_void.sql`) to DEVELOPMENT. The items in this section are covered by automated validation only; none was separately validated on a device (the professional-context read-only fallback included). Production has not been deployed or touched. No Specification changed in this section; the two policy items it left open were resolved separately (BR-56 past exceptions and weekly stale-edit protection, both below).

Resolved by this pass and its follow-ups:

- stale Appointment-hours `23503` read handling removed;
- unreachable Guardian lazy-VOID branch of the REQUESTED Alternative Time proposal removed;
- confirmed-reschedule DST tests made dynamic;
- Guardian and Professional sub-minute candidate grids aligned (SPEC-APT-013 BR-58);

- the cross-Clinic Veterinarian conflict lock pinned by regression;
- the BR-56 historical-exception rule (see "BR-56 currently effective Availability");
- weekly Appointment Availability stale-edit protection (see "Weekly Appointment Availability stale-edit protection" — the only item of this group validated on a device).

Remaining conscious debt, deliberately kept:

- a generic reusable multi-session / concurrency harness — larger future work; narrow races are covered deterministically and by recorded LOCAL two-session checks;
- the setup duplication between the two Professional candidate reads — kept, because the shared grid is already extracted and the remaining guard ordering is Spec-specific per read;
- seven exports used only inside their own module — cosmetic, kept.

- Guardian candidate grid (forward-only migration `20261017090000_guardian_candidate_grid_sub_minute_anchor.sql`): `get_requestable_appointment_start_times` converted covered ranges to whole minutes, so a range starting at `09:00:30` (Availability is `time(6)`, and the write RPCs accept seconds) lost its first position and offered 09:30, 10:00, … while both Professional reads offered 09:00:30, 09:30:30, …. It now steps in whole microseconds from the exact range start, as `appointment_reschedule_candidate_positions` does (BR-58). Output for whole-minute Availability is unchanged.
- REQUESTED Alternative Time (forward-only migration `20261017100000_requested_alternative_time_clinic_only_lazy_void.sql`): the unreachable Guardian `PROPOSING_AUTHORITY_LOST` lazy-VOID branch of `propose_requested_appointment_alternative_time` is removed. On a `REQUESTED` Appointment the open Proposal has a `REQUESTED` base (base-revision trigger, lifecycle VOID trigger, immutable context), which `appointment_reschedule_proposals_requested_base_clinic_check` makes Clinic-only. The confirmed propose path is unchanged.
- Mobile: the Appointment hours read no longer special-cases SQLSTATE `23503` (since `20261014090000` the read returns `42501` for an unknown Clinic; the writes' "Clinic does not exist" message mapping is still live and kept). When the professional-context read fails (other than an expired session), Appointment hours now shows the already-authorized configuration read-only with "Your editing permissions couldn't be confirmed right now…" and Try again, instead of an error screen; the editors show the same notice and offer no Save.
- Tests: the confirmed-reschedule DST case now uses the next Europe/Madrid transitions computed from the time-zone database (never skipped) instead of 2035 dates; slice 16 covers second- and microsecond-precision grids, a request round trip, and Guardian/Professional grid parity; the Alternative Time suite proves the Clinic-only CHECK holds with triggers bypassed and is validated; the confirmation suite pins that the cross-Clinic Veterinarian conflict check locks the Professional Profile row.
- Concurrency evidence (LOCAL, not source-controlled): two real sessions confirming overlapping Appointments of one Veterinarian at two Clinics — the second commit waits for the first and fails with `23P01`; with the Profile lock removed both commit (a double booking) while all 24 single-session suites still pass, which is why the lock is now pinned deterministically.
- Validation (LOCAL, before the DEVELOPMENT application): clean `supabase db reset`; 24 of 24 SQL suites, zero retained rows; deliberate faults caught: minute-truncating Guardian grid, wall-clock confirmed proposal end (dynamic DST case), Clinic authority-lost lazy VOID removed, Clinic-only CHECK dropped, Profile lock removed, `23503` mapping re-added, context failure shown as an error again. Mobile typecheck, strict unused, ESLint, Prettier, and Jest pass.

### Scheduling configuration — BR-56 currently effective Availability (SPEC-APT-013 BR-56)

CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT: implemented and validated locally; the user applied `20261018090000_br56_past_exceptions_not_currently_effective.sql` to DEVELOPMENT. Backend-only (there is no timezone-editing UI), so no device validation was required or performed. Production has not been deployed or touched.

- Result: Clinic or Veterinarian exceptions that are past under both the current and the requested timezone no longer trigger the `PROFESSIONAL_SCHEDULING` gate; current and future exceptions and recurring Availability still do; the structural timezone dependency is unchanged. The deterministic UTC-date fault coverage (below) is green.

- Policy: SPEC-APT-013 BR-56 (and Verification Note 39, plus two scenarios) now defines exceptions as currently effective unless past — local date before the Clinic-local current date under both the Clinic's current scheduling timezone and the requested timezone. An exception for today under either timezone counts; every exception counts while the current timezone is not valid. Recurring Clinic periods still count; Veterinarian Availability still counts only for a currently valid Assignment, with the same date rule for its exceptions. SPEC-SUB-003 defers to BR-56 and is unchanged.
- Forward-only migration `20261018090000_br56_past_exceptions_not_currently_effective.sql` replaces `set_clinic_scheduling_timezone` in place (same signature and ACL). Intentional order change: the requested timezone is validated (`22023`) before the Entitlement gate (`42501`), both still after the capability under the Organization → Clinic lock.
- Unchanged: past exceptions stay stored, and the structural timezone dependency (`assert_clinic_timezone_dependency`) still requires a valid scheduling timezone for them. No table or data change.
- Tests: `scheduling_timezone_gating_and_structural_integrity_spec_apt_013.sql` replaces the pinned "past-only exception gates" baseline with a case table — recurring, future, today, past-only (Clinic and Veterinarian), date-line crossings in both directions (Pacific/Kiritimati ↔ Pacific/Pago_Pago, Pacific/Kiritimati → Pacific/Tongatapu), invalid current timezone with and without an exception, ended Assignment, Opening Hours only, and nothing, plus a UTC-date probe chosen for the run instant (asserted to separate BR-56 from a UTC-date rule at every half hour of the day, so that fault is caught whatever hour the suite runs) — with dates computed at run time, the timezone and every row unchanged on denial, every row unchanged on success, and the structural dependency still holding for a past-only Clinic.
- Validation (LOCAL): clean `supabase db reset`; 24 of 24 SQL suites, zero retained rows; 10 of 10 deliberate faults caught (all exceptions counting, current timezone only, requested timezone only, UTC date, Clinic or Veterinarian past exception counting, ended Assignment counting, invalid current timezone failing open, the old gate-before-validation order, structural dependency weakened). No mobile change.

### Scheduling configuration — Weekly Appointment Availability stale-edit protection (SPEC-APT-013 BR-74, BR-75)

CLOSED: implemented and validated locally; the user applied `20261019090000_weekly_appointment_availability_revision.sql` to DEVELOPMENT; the stale-editor workflow was physically validated on Android against DEVELOPMENT. The earlier whole-week last-write-wins behaviour is superseded. Production has not been deployed or touched.

- Final model:
  - `clinics.weekly_appointment_availability_revision bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (non-negative) identifies only the stored recurring weekly Appointment Availability periods;
  - the management read `get_managed_clinic_appointment_availability` exposes it;
  - `replace_clinic_appointment_availability` requires the expected revision; the comparison happens under the existing Organization → Clinic lock; a mismatch fails with `P0001` and zero writes;
  - a real weekly change increments the revision exactly once; an identical weekly save does not;
  - Standard Appointment Duration, exceptions, timezone, Opening Hours, and Veterinarian Availability never affect it.
- Problem: weekly replacement covers all seven days, so a save prepared from an outdated week silently undid another actor's change.
- Specs: SPEC-APT-013 BR-74 (the read returns the weekly revision), BR-75 (whole-week replacement is stale-protected; exceptions, duration, and timezone stay last-write-wins), Verification Notes 45–46, two scenarios. `docs/RELATIONAL_MODEL.md` records the column.
- Forward-only migration `20261019090000_weekly_appointment_availability_revision.sql`:
  - `clinics.weekly_appointment_availability_revision bigint not null default 0` with a non-negative CHECK; existing Clinics start at 0 (an optimistic-concurrency token, not history; no backfill), and the first real change makes 1;
  - `get_managed_clinic_appointment_availability` returns it as one trailing column, read in the same statement as the periods; authorization, `STABLE`, no lock, no Entitlement, and grants unchanged;
  - `replace_clinic_appointment_availability(uuid, jsonb)` is dropped (no compatibility overload) and replaced by `(p_clinic_id uuid, p_periods jsonb, p_expected_weekly_availability_revision bigint) returns uuid`. Order: authentication (`28000`) → expected revision NULL or negative (`22023`) → existing Organization → Clinic lock, capability, and `PROFESSIONAL_SCHEDULING` → revision compared under the Clinic lock (`P0001` "Stale weekly Clinic Appointment Availability revision", nothing written) → the unchanged week validation → an identical set (any order or time spelling) returns without a write or a new revision → otherwise full replacement and exactly one increment.
  - Exceptions, duration, timezone, Opening Hours, and Veterinarian Availability RPCs are untouched and never change the revision.
- Mobile: the management parser requires the revision (a non-negative safe integer; missing or malformed rejects the read); the weekly editor keeps the draft together with the revision of the read it was built from — later focus reads never replace either — and sends that revision; a stale rejection is normalized (`APPOINTMENT_HOURS_WEEKLY_STALE`), followed by a re-read, and the draft is replaced by the latest week and revision with "The appointment hours changed while you were editing. We've loaded the latest version. Review the changes and try again." (EN/ES); never merged or retried. Lost-response reconciliation is unchanged: a re-read week equal to the submitted one is success (also when another editor saved the same week). The decision logic is pure and unit-tested (`weeklyEditorFrom`, `weeklyEditorAfterRead`, `weeklySaveFailure`).
- Tests: `clinic_appointment_availability_management_spec_apt_013.sql` replaces the last-write-wins case with revision 0 → 1 → 2, two editors (stale rejected, first editor's week and revision intact), stale before payload validation, identical set (reordered, respelled) without a new revision, duration / exception set-change-clear / timezone leaving the revision unchanged so an earlier weekly draft still saves, NULL and negative tokens, authorization before the stale check, and the contract (old signature gone, column shape). `professional_management_reads_baseline.sql` and `scheduling_timezone_gating_and_structural_integrity_spec_apt_013.sql` were updated only for the new read column and write argument.
- Two-session check (LOCAL, not source-controlled): sessions A and B both started from revision 0; A held its transaction and the Clinic lock, then committed its weekly change; B waited, and after A committed it re-checked the revision under the lock and failed with the stale `P0001`; A's week (and revision 1) remained intact. With the revision checked before the lock (control fault), B overwrote A's change — which is why the comparison must happen under the lock.
- Validation (LOCAL): clean `supabase db reset`; 24 of 24 SQL suites, zero retained rows; 12 of 12 deliberate faults caught — 8 SQL (stale check omitted or before the lock, no increment, increment on an identical save, a stale save deleting periods, exception or duration changes incrementing, the old signature kept) and 4 mobile (a later read replacing the base revision, stale handled as a generic failure, stale not normalized, a missing revision defaulted to 0); the real two-session check above passed; Jest 58 suites / 1,238 tests; typecheck, strict unused-variable check, ESLint, and Prettier pass; `git diff --check` clean.
- Deployment order: migration before this mobile build (the build requires the new read column and the 3-argument write; older builds fail their weekly save against the new database).
- Physical validation (Android, DEVELOPMENT, by the user), with a second authorized editor simulated from the Supabase SQL Editor through the real `replace_clinic_appointment_availability(...)` RPC — all passed:
  1. Android opened the weekly Appointment hours editor at revision N;
  2. the Android draft was modified and kept unsaved;
  3. the second editor was simulated through the real RPC;
  4. it changed the stored week and advanced the revision to N+1;
  5. Android then saved its stale draft with revision N;
  6. the stale write was rejected;
  7. the second editor's week was not overwritten;
  8. the revision remained N+1 after the stale attempt (zero writes);
  9. Android stayed in the editor instead of navigating away;
  10. the stale message was shown;
  11. the stale local draft was discarded;
  12. the editor loaded the latest server week and revision;
  13. Save stayed disabled until the user made another change;
  14. a new edit based on the refreshed N+1 state saved successfully;
  15. the original test week was restored afterwards through the real RPC, without resetting the revision backwards.

  This physically confirms that the draft keeps the revision of the state it was built from, that a later server change does not silently replace that base revision, that stale detection prevents silent lost updates and stale saves write nothing, that the app refreshes instead of merging or retrying silently, and that editing continues successfully after the refresh.
- Independent configuration (approved contract, covered by the automated suite; not physically validated): Standard Appointment Duration, exception, and timezone changes do not make a weekly draft stale.
- Lost-response reconciliation is unchanged and covered automatically (not physically validated): after a failed response the app re-reads; a stored week equal to the submitted one is treated as success; a stale editor whose desired week differs from the server is never mistaken for success.

CLOSED. Atomic Organization + Owner + initial Subscription onboarding remains future work (details below).

- Organization establishment grant defect (fixed): `create_veterinary_organization(text)` was still executable by `authenticated` (granted in `20260825112945_organization_foundation.sql`), so any verified User could establish an Organization, become its `OWNER` with no Subscription, and then create and publish Clinics — contrary to ADR-009 and SPEC-ORG-001 BR-10/BR-12. The function still derives the initial Owner from `auth.uid()`, so the trusted path remains the operator database session that impersonates the designated Owner (as `supabase/provisioning/production_validation_data.sql` does, now calling it as the session role with an EXECUTE preflight instead of `set local role authenticated`). This only withdraws the client grant: the atomic, idempotent Commercial Onboarding operation of ADR-009 (Organization + OWNER + initial Subscription) and the legacy-Organization inventory remain future work. New rollback-only suite `supabase/tests/organization_establishment_trusted_only_adr_009.sql` (ACL contract; verified, unverified, existing-OWNER, and anon client calls rejected with no Organization, Membership, or responsibility row; trusted operator session and `service_role` with a designated Owner still establish; `service_role` without a subject rejected). `standard_appointment_duration_and_candidate_times_slice16.sql` fixtures now establish their Organizations as the trusted session role. Validation: clean `supabase db reset`; 25 of 25 SQL suites, zero retained rows; red before the migration; 5 of 5 ACL faults (re-grant to `authenticated`, `anon`, or `public`; `service_role` revoked; `SECURITY INVOKER`) caught, and the behavioural assertions alone catch the `authenticated` re-grant and the `service_role` revoke.
- Guardian notes surfaced to the Clinic: the pet guardian's optional request notes (`appointments.guardian_notes`, SPEC-APT-001 §4, preserved by SPEC-APT-004 BR-16) were already returned by `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` and parsed by mobile but never shown. The professional Appointment detail now shows them verbatim under the reason for visit ("Notas del responsable de la mascota / Notes from the pet guardian"), in every lifecycle state, only when present (`guardianNotesToShow`). No backend or read-contract change.
- Optional Veterinarian Assignment copy: the Guardian Appointment detail showed "Todavía no hay un veterinario asignado / No veterinarian is assigned yet" on every Appointment without an assignment — in practice all of them, terminal ones included — implying an assignment would follow. The section now appears only while a current Veterinarian Assignment exists ("A veterinarian is assigned…", no identity), per SPEC-APT-012 BR-17 (`showsVeterinarianAssigned`); the "not assigned yet" copy is removed.
- Mobile validation: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (58 suites, 1,252 tests); 5 of 5 deliberate mobile faults caught (notes never shown, blank notes shown, notes reformatted, Veterinarian section always shown, an ended assignment counted as current). No dependency, native, or config change. Not physically validated on a device.
