### Appointment stream — Guardian cancellation cutoff at the confirmed start and late Clinic cancellation (SPEC-APT-003 BR-31, SPEC-APT-011 BR-34, SPEC-APT-012 BR-47)

CLOSED. Implemented and validated LOCALLY; the user applied `20261020090000_withdraw_client_organization_establishment.sql`, `20261021090000_guardian_confirmed_cancellation_cutoff.sql`, and `20261022090000_clinic_schedule_cancellation_reason.sql` to DEVELOPMENT and physically validated the full flow on Android there; all planned device checks passed. Production has not been deployed or touched.

- Final rule: a Guardian may cancel a `CONFIRMED` Appointment only while its latest confirmed start is strictly in the future according to backend time; at `start <= now` Guardian cancellation is rejected. `REQUESTED` cancellation is unchanged. Clinic cancellation remains allowed after the start; Complete and NO_SHOW remain the normal Clinic resolutions after the start. No Veterinarian Assignment, Clinical Encounter, or clinical documentation is required to resolve the Appointment.
- Physical validation (Android, DEVELOPMENT, by the user) — all passed:
  1. a future `CONFIRMED` Appointment shows Guardian Cancel;
  2. once the start is reached the Appointment remains `CONFIRMED` until Clinic resolution, Guardian Cancel disappears, and the unresolved-past copy is shown;
  3. a past unresolved `CONFIRMED` Appointment appears under "Activas / Active", not Upcoming or History;
  4. the Guardian opened the Cancel panel before the start and left it open;
  5. after the start passed, typing in the reason field did not close the panel;
  6. submitting after the start reached the backend;
  7. the backend rejected it with the specific "appointment can no longer be cancelled" behaviour;
  8. the panel closed after the authoritative rejection;
  9. the Appointment was re-read;
  10. Cancel remained unavailable;
  11. the unresolved-past copy appeared;
  12. the professional post-start detail showed Complete, No-show, and the less prominent Clinic cancellation action;
  13. the Clinic post-start cancellation required a reason and succeeded;
  14. the cancellation reason was visible in both the Guardian detail and the professional detail;
  15. on a separate Appointment, resolving as Complete or No-show produced the correct terminal state and removed the unresolved-past note.
- Not device-validated beyond these checks; other cases (exact boundary, latest-revision selection, races, Encounter interaction, zero-write rejection) are covered by the automated suites.
- Guardian list grouping: the nonterminal section is "Activas / Active", because `REQUESTED` and `CONFIRMED` Appointments may remain active after their scheduled time has passed; an unresolved past `CONFIRMED` Appointment must not be labelled Upcoming; terminal states remain in History.
- Professional cancellation reason: `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` explicitly exposes `cancellation_reason` for `CANCELLED` Appointments, pinned by SPEC-APT-012 BR-47 — Guardian and Clinic cancellation reasons are returned verbatim; non-`CANCELLED` rows expose none; proposal, reason-for-visit, and Guardian-note text never populate it; the professional detail shows it only when present; no storage duplication was added.
- Open panel and backend authority: the device clock is presentation guidance only — it decides whether the Guardian can open Cancel; an already-open panel is not closed merely because local time crosses the start; submit still reaches the backend; backend time is authoritative; the cutoff response triggers the existing re-read and reconciliation; no automatic retry occurs.
- Latest integrated automated validation (LOCAL): 25 of 25 SQL suites after a clean `supabase db reset`, zero retained rows; 10 of 10 cancellation SQL faults caught; 5 of 5 Organization ACL faults caught; the `cancellation_reason` read-contract regression green; Jest 58 suites / 1,309 tests; typecheck, strict unused-variable check, ESLint, and Prettier pass; `git diff --check` clean.
- Not to be reopened: Guardian post-start cancellation, late Clinic cancellation, Active grouping, professional cancellation reason, panel lifecycle, the Organization client grant, Guardian notes, and the optional Veterinarian copy.

The implementation history of this checkpoint follows.

- Rule: a Guardian may cancel a `CONFIRMED` Appointment only while the start of its latest confirmed Schedule Revision is strictly later than backend time; at and after that start the Guardian cancellation is rejected with zero writes, and the Clinic resolves the Appointment (`COMPLETED`, `NO_SHOW`, or Clinic cancellation). `REQUESTED` cancellation (BR-23, both actors) and Clinic cancellation of a `CONFIRMED` Appointment before or after its start are unchanged.
- Forward-only migration `20261021090000_guardian_confirmed_cancellation_cutoff.sql`: two internal helpers, `appointment_latest_confirmed_start_at(uuid)` (twin of `appointment_latest_requested_start_at`) and `appointment_confirmed_start_is_in_future(uuid, timestamptz)` (`CONFIRMED` and latest confirmed start `>` the reference instant; fails closed without a confirmed revision), neither executable by `anon`/`authenticated`/`public`; and `cancel_appointment` replaced in place with the identical signature, ACL, and body plus one check. The check runs only for `GUARDIAN` + `CONFIRMED`, after authentication, Guardian authority, the Appointment row lock, and the lifecycle check, in the same position as the pending-request rule and before the Clinical Encounter prohibition, with `clock_timestamp()` evaluated after the lock. It reads the latest confirmed revision server-side; the Guardian contract gains no revision token, so no client interval is trusted, a pending proposal never moves the cutoff, and an accepted reschedule does through the revision it created.
- Error contract: SQLSTATE `23514` (this operation's existing non-retryable business-rule code) with the stable, distinct message `A Guardian cannot cancel a CONFIRMED Appointment whose confirmed start has been reached`. It raises before any write.
- SQL: `appointment_cancellation_spec_apt_003.sql` gains section 10 (cases 40–59): helper ACLs; the exact predicate boundary (start − 1 µs future; start and start + 1 µs not), latest-revision lookup, `REQUESTED` never future; Guardian cancellation well ahead and just before the start with the unchanged effects; a start reached during the transaction (seeded at `clock_timestamp()`, so transaction-time evaluation would wrongly allow it) and a past start, each rejected with the exact SQLSTATE/message and a zero-write fingerprint (Appointment, Events, revisions, Care Authorization still `ACTIVE`, a stored `PENDING` proposal still `PENDING`, assignments, Communications); earlier-passed/latest-future cancels and earlier-future/latest-reached is rejected; Clinic cancellation after the start; lapsed `REQUESTED` keeps its own rule; Encounter before the start → Encounter rule, after → cutoff; serialized races with completion, no-show, and Clinic cancellation in both orders (after the start the Guardian never wins; before it the Guardian can win and later Clinic resolutions are lifecycle rejections); Communications only for successful cancellations. Races are serialized single-session: the windows are disjoint (Guardian cancellation needs start `>` now, completion and no-show need start `<=` now) and row-lock serialization is unchanged, so no new two-session check was added.
- Validation (LOCAL): clean `supabase db reset`; 25 of 25 SQL suites with zero retained rows; the extended suite fails against the previous `cancel_appointment` body; 10 of 10 deliberate faults caught — cutoff omitted, applied to Clinic too, earliest instead of latest confirmed revision (also caught behaviourally without the predicate assertions), transaction time instead of `clock_timestamp()`, `>=` boundary letting the exact start pass, rejection ending the Care Authorization, rejection writing a Lifecycle Event and state, applied to `REQUESTED` too, evaluated after the Encounter rule, retryable `40001`.
- Mobile (Guardian): `canCancelAppointment` offers Cancel on a `CONFIRMED` Appointment only while its start is in the future on the device clock (advisory); `REQUESTED` unchanged. A started, unresolved `CONFIRMED` Appointment stays `CONFIRMED` and shows "La hora de la cita ya ha pasado. La clínica actualizará su estado. / The scheduled appointment time has passed. The clinic will update its status." (`isAwaitingClinicResolution`). If the device clock still offered Cancel and the backend rejects with the cutoff, the error maps to "Esta cita ya no se puede cancelar. / This appointment can no longer be cancelled.", the panel closes, the Appointment is re-read, and Cancel stays closed for that same confirmed start even if the device clock is behind (`guardianCancellationView`); never retried.
- Mobile (Professional): Clinic cancellation is now offered for a `CONFIRMED` Appointment after its start too (`offersClinicCancellation`), as a tertiary "Registrar cancelación por la clínica / Record a clinic cancellation" below Complete and No-show (`isPostStartClinicCancellation`), with the existing required reason, stale-safe token, and a panel hint that it is only for a visit the Clinic called off. Future `REQUESTED`/`CONFIRMED` cancellation is unchanged; a lapsed `REQUESTED` still offers none. The stale "policy is undecided (BR-28)" comment is replaced.
- Mobile validation: `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check`, `npx jest` (58 suites, 1,280 tests); 6 of 6 deliberate mobile faults caught (old always-cancellable rule, backend rejection ignored, cutoff error unmapped, post-start Clinic Cancel hidden again, post-start Cancel not secondary, status note on `REQUESTED`). No dependency, native, or config change.
- Deployment order when applied: migration before this mobile build is not required for correctness (older builds keep offering Cancel and get the mapped generic error), but the migration should precede the build so the new copy reflects real backend behaviour.
- Physical-validation findings (Android, first round, reported by the user) and LOCAL fixes:
  1. A started, unresolved `CONFIRMED` Appointment appeared under the Guardian list section "Próximas / Upcoming". Root cause: the list grouped by lifecycle state only (REQUESTED/CONFIRMED → first section) under a time-implying label. Fix: the section is renamed "Activas / Active" (`appointments.activeSectionTitle`; `upcomingSectionTitle` removed) and the grouping moved to the pure, tested `partitionGuardianAppointments`; the Appointment stays active (never History, never hidden). SPEC-APT-011 §6 gains a presentation clarification. The professional schedule (Upcoming / Needs resolution / History) is unchanged.
  2. The Professional Appointment detail did not show the reason the Clinic recorded for a cancellation. Root cause: `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` never exposed it (only `get_pet_appointments` did, since `20261007090000`). Fix: forward-only migration `20261022090000_clinic_schedule_cancellation_reason.sql` drops and recreates the Clinic read with one trailing `cancellation_reason text` — the reason on the single `CANCELLED` Lifecycle Event of a `CANCELLED` Appointment, `NULL` otherwise; the same source and rule as the Guardian read; no storage duplicated; body, attributes, authorization, ordering, derived values, and ACL otherwise unchanged. The mobile parser requires the column (null, or a non-blank reason only on `CANCELLED`; anything else rejects the read), and the detail shows "Motivo de la cancelación / Cancellation reason" verbatim (multiline kept) after the reason for visit (`cancellationReasonToShow`). This build therefore requires the migration in its target environment. Basis: SPEC-APT-003 BR-8/BR-9 and SPEC-APT-012 BR-47 (with §4 and Verification Notes 42–48), which now make this read contract explicit: the verbatim reason from the `CANCELLED` Lifecycle Event for Guardian and Clinic cancellations, `null` otherwise, equal to the Guardian read, never from proposal or other free-text fields, and shown on the professional detail when present.
  3. An open Guardian cancellation panel closed by itself after the confirmed start. Root cause: both the Cancel action and the open panel rendered under the device-clock decision, so any render after the start (typing in the reason field) unmounted the panel and the backend path was never exercised. Fix: `guardianCancellationPresentation` — the device clock decides only whether the panel may be opened; an opened panel stays while the Appointment is `REQUESTED`/`CONFIRMED`, submission always reaches the backend (no client time gate), and the panel closes on the backend cutoff rejection (mapped copy, re-read, Cancel stays closed for that start, the "time has passed" note appears) or on a terminal re-read. The note is withheld while the panel is open so form and note never contradict each other. No automatic retry.
  - Validation (LOCAL): clean `supabase db reset`; 25 of 25 SQL suites, zero retained rows; five suites updated only for the appended Clinic-read column in their exact-signature assertions; `guardian_appointment_cancellation_reason_spec_apt_011.sql` gains Clinic-read cases 11–14 (contract, reasons the Clinic and a Guardian recorded, parity with the Guardian read, no reason for other states or from proposals, read writes nothing), fails against the previous Clinic read, and catches 3 of 4 read faults (wrong Event, always null, proposal reason leaked; dropping the redundant `lifecycle_state = 'CANCELLED'` join guard is an equivalent mutant — only a CANCELLED Appointment has a CANCELLED Event). Mobile: `tsc`, strict unused, ESLint, Prettier, Jest 58 suites / 1,309 tests; 8 of 8 effective mobile faults caught (panel gated by the clock again, cutoff not closing the panel, note beside the open panel, past `CONFIRMED` moved to History, `CONFIRMED` always History, parser accepting a reason on a non-`CANCELLED` row, detail showing a reason for any state).

### Clinic self-service Phase 1 (SPEC-CLI-007, SPEC-CLI-002, SPEC-CLI-003, SPEC-CLI-004, SPEC-APT-013 BR-76)

CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT. Slice A, Slice B, and Slice C are each CLOSED: applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user and physically validated there (see "Slice A", "Slice B", "Slice C", and "Phase 1 closure" below). **Production has not been deployed or touched.**

- Purpose: a pre-existing, provisioned Clinic can be configured, published, and unpublished from the professional app without SQL or developer intervention.
- Scope: the managed configuration read; public profile and contact editing; location editing; scheduling timezone editing; publication status, Publish, and Unpublish; a professional Clinic settings overview with readiness. Excluded: Clinic creation (trusted provisioning; ADR-009 unchanged), Opening Hours and Service Offerings editing (later self-service slices; their management reads and writes already exist), staff Invitation/acceptance/Membership ending (the next major product area, subject to prioritization), Veterinarian Assignment/Availability, clinical tooling.
- Specifications:
  - new SPEC-CLI-007 — View Managed Clinic Configuration: `get_managed_clinic_profile(p_clinic_id uuid) returns jsonb` (any of `CLINIC_MANAGE_PUBLIC_PROFILE`/`_LOCATION`/`_PUBLICATION`/`_OPENING_HOURS`; unpublished Clinics included; no Entitlement; non-locking, `STABLE`; `42501` for nonexistent and inaccessible alike); the derived, non-persisted statuses Ready to publish, Published, and Online Appointment setup complete (advisory, never "bookable"); the settings overview and information architecture;
  - SPEC-CLI-004: whole-profile replacement, backend trim set, blank = absent, all-absent removes the profile while unpublished, published name retained, no new format/length validation (hardening is future work), last-write-wins;
  - SPEC-CLI-002: Phase 1 location = address text + coordinates, backend validity = non-blank address + coordinate ranges only (no address/coordinate correspondence check, no geocoding), explicit map-pin establishment (never an untouched default position), no removal, last-write-wins; BR-5 clarified; later amended with BR-18, optional explicit current-device-location marker assistance (still no geocoding);
  - SPEC-CLI-003: Publish pinned (name + location only, idempotent, no scheduling or Entitlement prerequisites) and Unpublish specified as `unpublish_clinic(p_clinic_id uuid) returns uuid` (`CLINIC_MANAGE_PUBLICATION`, no Entitlement, Organization → Clinic locking, idempotent, publication state only, no Communication); existing Appointments, Revisions, Proposals, Care Authorizations, and Communications unaffected and their lifecycles continue;
  - SPEC-APT-013 BR-76: the existing timezone write as a Clinic self-service operation (`CLINIC_MANAGE_OPENING_HOURS`, valid IANA name, no clearing, BR-56 authoritative, last-write-wins, explicit selection only, Receptionist guidance);
  - SPEC-APT-004 edge case "Clinic becomes unpublished" aligned with SPEC-CLI-003 BR-18;
  - DOMAIN.md (derived statuses, reversible publication), RELATIONAL_MODEL.md (stale "no Phase 1 timezone-change workflow" wording replaced), REQUIREMENTS.md (traceability to SPEC-CLI-007).
- Known conformance gaps for implementation (documented, not changed): `set_clinic_location` validates input (`22023`) before establishing the Clinic and capability; `set_clinic_location`, `set_clinic_public_profile`, `publish_clinic`, and `set_clinic_scheduling_timezone` (through the shared `assert_clinic_capability_after_lock`) report a nonexistent Clinic as `23503` before capability. The specified contract is `42501` for nonexistent and inaccessible alike, with input validation only for authorized callers (SPEC-CLI-002 BR-17, SPEC-CLI-003 BR-20, SPEC-CLI-004 BR-20, SPEC-APT-013 BR-76). `assert_clinic_capability_after_lock` is shared by many out-of-scope writes; normalizing it is an implementation decision that must not change their other semantics. Resolved: Slice B (`20261024090000…`) fixed the Location and Profile writes and Slice C (`20261025090000…`) fixed `publish_clinic` and `set_clinic_scheduling_timezone`, each by replacing only those functions; the shared helper is unchanged.
- Intended sub-slices, in order:
  - A — management read migration + Clinic settings overview/readiness (read-only); the read must be deployed before the mobile build that parses it;
  - B — profile and location editors (existing writes; disclosure-order normalization for them);
  - C — timezone editor + Publish/Unpublish (new `unpublish_clinic` migration; Publish ships only together with Unpublish).
- Concurrency: last-write-wins for profile, location, and timezone, deliberately, with no revision tokens; Publish/Unpublish idempotent; clients re-read after every save and reconcile lost responses.
- Existing data: no backfill; existing unpublished Clinics become manageable, missing values show as missing, published Clinics and all public Guardian reads are unchanged.
- Phase 1 closure — Clinic self-service Phase 1 CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT:
  - completed slices:
    - Slice A — managed Clinic configuration read + Clinic settings overview/readiness;
    - Slice B — Public Profile + Location editors, including current-device-location marker assistance;
    - Slice C — Scheduling timezone + Publish + Unpublish;
  - an authorized Clinic administrator can now self-manage, without SQL or developer intervention, the core configuration needed for public Clinic identity (Public Profile), public Location, scheduling timezone, and publication visibility (Publish/Unpublish). This does not mean every Clinic-management CRUD is complete;
  - fail-closed normalization complete for every Phase 1 self-service write: Profile and Location (`20261024090000…`), scheduling timezone, Publish, and Unpublish (`20261025090000…`) — (1) authentication, (2) unknown and inaccessible Clinics fail closed identically (`42501`), (3) capability, (4) input/state/business rules; the Organization → Clinic lock order is unchanged; the shared `assert_clinic_capability_after_lock` is unchanged;
  - deliberately outside Phase 1 (future work or separate product areas, not Phase 1 debt): Opening Hours management UI; Service Offerings management UI; staff Membership / Responsibility management (Staff Phase 1 has since closed, see its section); Organization establishment (trusted Commercial Onboarding, ADR-009); Clinic creation (operational self-service, not governed by ADR-009 — see "Clinic creation self-service Phase 1 — Add Clinic"); geocoding / address autocomplete; Location deletion; and the other deferred areas already recorded in the Slice A–C sections;
  - Appointment hours navigation (settled): the direct Appointment hours entry on the Professional Clinic screen remains permanently because Receptionists can manage Appointment hours but cannot access Clinic settings; it is not duplication debt;
  - DEVELOPMENT fixtures: the SQL-created two-Organization / two-Clinic administrator fixture in DEVELOPMENT has NOT been cleaned up and is deliberately retained as DEVELOPMENT data for multi-Organization context, multi-Clinic isolation, and later visual/navigation review. Those SQL-created Memberships are DEVELOPMENT test data, not production onboarding;
  - next planned activity (at Phase 1 closure): **Clinic self-service visual/UX review using Android screenshots of the completed Phase 1 across the DEVELOPMENT Clinics/Organizations** — since done and CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT as the "Post-Phase-1 Clinic self-service UX/context polish" (see below); the project can continue to the next prioritized product area;
  - **Production has not been deployed or touched.**

#### Slice A — managed Clinic configuration read + Clinic settings overview (SPEC-CLI-007)

CLOSED: implemented and validated LOCALLY; the user applied `20261023090000_managed_clinic_profile_read.sql` to DEVELOPMENT and physically checked the overview in the current Android/Metro workflow there. Read-only: no profile, location, or timezone editor, no Publish or Unpublish, and no write RPC change. Clinic self-service Phase 1 remains open. Production has not been deployed or touched.

- Backend: forward-only migration `20261023090000_managed_clinic_profile_read.sql` adds `get_managed_clinic_profile(p_clinic_id uuid) returns jsonb` with the exact SPEC-CLI-007 BR-1 payload (every member always present, values as stored; no duration, Availability, Opening Hours, Services, Subscription, or Entitlement data).
  - Works for published and unpublished Clinics; no publication or Entitlement requirement.
  - Authorized by any one of `CLINIC_MANAGE_PUBLIC_PROFILE`, `CLINIC_MANAGE_LOCATION`, `CLINIC_MANAGE_PUBLICATION`, or `CLINIC_MANAGE_OPENING_HOURS`, through the non-locking `user_has_clinic_capability` (not `assert_clinic_capability_after_lock`).
  - Inaccessible and nonexistent Clinics fail closed with the same `42501` "Applicable Clinic-management capability is required"; `28000` when unauthenticated.
  - `STABLE`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, empty `search_path`, authenticated-only; one `SELECT` builds the payload (one snapshot); no Organization or Clinic row lock.
  - No table, constraint, data, or direct table grant added; no write RPC changed. The four self-service writes and their documented disclosure-order gaps are untouched (Slices B/C).
- Clinic settings overview: the Professional Clinic surface now has a read-only Clinic settings overview (`/professional/clinics/[clinicId]/settings`). It shows the status of the Clinic profile/name, the location, the scheduling timezone, the standard Appointment duration, Appointment hours, and publication/visibility, and presents separately whether patients can currently find the Clinic and whether the known configuration prerequisites for online Appointment requests are complete. The latter is advisory, is never called "bookable", and does not guarantee candidate times. Only the duration and Appointment hours rows navigate, to the existing Appointment hours screens; profile, location, time zone, and visibility editing are stated as coming later.
- Readiness model (derived on the client from current reads; no persisted readiness state):
  - Ready to publish — a non-empty display name and a stored location;
  - Published — the stored `is_published`;
  - Online Appointment setup complete — the known configuration prerequisites are present: published, a valid timezone, a standard Appointment duration, recurring Appointment Availability or a current/future (Clinic-local) OPEN exception, and the Organization's `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT`. A prerequisite known to be missing makes it incomplete; a failed scheduling read yields Unknown rather than falsely reporting Complete or Missing.
- Workflow gating: the Clinic settings entry is shown only to professionals holding at least one of the four Clinic-management capabilities above; Receptionists and Veterinarians without such a capability do not see it; existing Schedule and Appointment hours access is unchanged.
- Partial-read behaviour:
  - a failure of the core managed Clinic read shows the load error (or "unavailable" when denied) and never invented or partial profile data;
  - a failure of the Appointment hours read leaves profile, location, and publication visible but marks the scheduling-related rows Unknown, with a notice and Retry;
  - a professional-context failure makes the plan state Unknown;
  - an expired session remains terminal.
- Receptionist timezone guidance: the existing Appointment hours missing-timezone notice now tells the reader (for example a Receptionist) to ask a clinic owner or administrator to set it. No capability was changed.
- Existing data and public compatibility: no backfill; authorized professionals can now inspect unpublished Clinics; missing configuration stays represented as Missing/`null`, never as fabricated defaults; Guardian/public reads and behaviour are unchanged; existing writes are unchanged. No dependency, native, or config change.
- Physical validation (DEVELOPMENT, by the user, Android/Metro): the Clinic settings entry and overview render correctly; the displayed Clinic configuration and readiness appear correct; navigation and read-only behaviour appear correct. No further device checks are claimed; automated coverage remains authoritative for the exact role, error, and readiness matrix.
- Automated validation (LOCAL):
  - clean `supabase db reset`; 26 of 26 SQL suites pass with zero retained rows, including the new rollback-only `supabase/tests/clinic_self_service_spec_cli_007.sql` (contract and ACL; exact payloads for published/unpublished, empty/partial/complete configuration; each of the four capabilities alone sufficient and eight others not, by substituting the authoritative predicate inside the rolled-back transaction; the allowed/denied role matrix; identical outcome for unknown and inaccessible Clinics; no Entitlement; sibling isolation; no row lock, with a locking Clinic write as control; no side effects; `STABLE`);
  - 18 of 18 deliberate SQL faults caught (publication required, Entitlement required, each of the four capabilities denied, an extra capability granted, capability check removed, Clinic scoping removed, unknown Clinic disclosed as `23503`, profile and location leaked from a sibling Clinic, publication hard-coded and inverted, row locks taken, not `STABLE`, an extra duration member, timezone dropped);
  - a real two-session non-locking proof passed (committed fixture removed afterwards, zero retained rows): while another session held an uncommitted `set_clinic_scheduling_timezone` with its Organization and Clinic row locks, the read returned at once under a 1 s `lock_timeout`, showing the pre-commit snapshot;
  - mobile: Jest 62 suites / 1,420 tests; `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, and `npm run format:check` pass. Every behavioural mobile mutant was caught; one further mutant, removing a redundant `Number.isFinite` from the coordinate check, is equivalent (the range checks already reject non-finite values) and is not an uncovered behaviour;
  - `git diff --check` clean.
- Navigation decision (settled during the Slice B polish): the Professional Clinic screen keeps the direct Appointment hours entry permanently, alongside access to Appointment hours from Clinic settings. This is not duplication or debt: Receptionists can manage Appointment hours but cannot see Clinic settings, so the direct entry is their only path, and Appointment hours is a frequent operational task while Clinic settings is lower-frequency configuration.
- Next (at Slice A closure): **Slice B: Public Profile + Location editors** — since CLOSED (see "Slice B" below); Slice C (scheduling timezone editor, Publish, Unpublish) is also CLOSED.

  - the overview's Profile row now reads "Perfil público / Public profile" with the subtitle "Nombre, descripción y datos de contacto / Name, description and contact details" (it opens the whole public profile, not only the name); the stored name is still shown as the row's value; the editor's title is "Perfil público / Public profile" and its intro names the same public information;
  - the overview's Location row reads "Ubicación / Location" with the subtitle "Dirección pública y posición en el mapa / Public address and map position";
  - the Location editor keeps the two concepts visibly separate: "Dirección que ven los clientes / Address shown to patients" ("Escribe la dirección pública de la clínica. Cambiarla no mueve el marcador del mapa.") and "Posición en el mapa / Map position" ("Coloca el marcador en la ubicación exacta. Esta posición se usa para las búsquedas cercanas…", moving it doesn't change the address); English copy says "marker" consistently;
  - manual positioning is intentional and unchanged: map tap, draggable marker, the accessible "place the marker at the centre of the map" action, and the position announced as text;
  - no-location behaviour re-audited and pinned by a step-by-step test: no pin is fabricated, the neutral map region is presentation only, an address alone or a pin alone cannot be saved, an explicit pin plus a non-blank address enables Save, and a saved location reopens as a confirmed pin; an existing location still allows address-only and pin-only saves with six-decimal canonical coordinates;
  - row copy moved into the pure `SETTINGS_ITEM_COPY` model, with a test that the Profile row means the whole public profile and the Location row covers address and map position.
- Product decision — no location deletion in Phase 1: a Clinic location can be created or replaced but not removed (SPEC-CLI-002 BR-15); a published Clinic requires a location; an unpublished Clinic with a location corrects or replaces it instead of deleting it; once Slice C exists, Unpublish (SPEC-CLI-003 BR-16), not location deletion, is how a Clinic leaves public discovery. No Remove/Clear action, delete RPC, or destructive button exists.
- Geocoding decision (replaces the earlier "Find this address on the map" follow-up): Phase 1 deliberately adds no address geocoding and no external or paid geocoding dependency — no provider, account, API key, secret, or Edge Function, and no address autocomplete or reverse geocoding — so no recurring external service cost is introduced. Manual marker placement plus optional current-device-location assistance is considered sufficient; address geocoding may be reconsidered later only if real usage shows meaningful friction. No provider ADR is needed because no provider is selected.
- Final Slice B refinement — current-device-location marker assistance (SPEC-CLI-002 BR-13, BR-18, Verification Notes 20–28; implemented LOCALLY, see "Current-location implementation" below):
  - one optional, explicitly user-triggered action in the Location editor, "Usar mi ubicación actual / Use my current location", with supporting copy "Usa la ubicación de este dispositivo para colocar el marcador. Podrás ajustarlo antes de guardar. / Use this device's location to place the marker. You can adjust it before saving.";
  - on invocation only: request foreground permission if needed, read the current position once, centre the map, place the marker there (an explicitly established marker), let the actor adjust it; only Save persists. Never on load, never silently moving a stored or placed marker, never touching the address (no reverse geocoding), never saving by itself, never background location;
  - denial, unavailable services, timeout, or failure: calm message; typed address and any stored or manual marker kept; manual placement and saving never depend on device location;
  - privacy: the position stays on the device, is not sent to any external provider, logged, or recorded for analytics, and becomes the Clinic's coordinates only through an explicit save;
  - device location is a positioning convenience, not proof that the Clinic is at the device's position;
  - uses the installed `expo-location` foreground calls only; no new dependency.
- Current-location implementation (LOCAL, mobile only; no backend, SQL, migration, Location RPC, or Spec change — the prior 27/27 SQL result stands):
  - new `src/lib/device-location-assist.ts`: `readDevicePosition` (checks foreground permission; asks only if not granted and the system still allows asking, so a permanent denial never re-prompts; reads the position once with balanced accuracy and a 15 s timeout; canonicalizes to the editor's six decimals; returns located / denied (with `canAskAgain`) / location services off / unavailable — never a fabricated coordinate) and `createDeviceLocationAssist` (one pending lookup at a time; a marker placed by hand or leaving supersedes a pending lookup, whose late result is then ignored; only the latest lookup applies);
  - Location editor: "Usar mi ubicación actual / Use my current location" beside "place the marker at the centre of the map", with its supporting copy; runs only on tap (nothing on load, a stored marker stays until tapped); on success it establishes the draft marker, centres the map, and announces the position for screen readers, leaving the address untouched; while locating only that action shows a loading state (address editing and map interactions stay available); denial, permanent denial, services off, and unavailable/timeout each show calm copy and keep the address and any marker; manual placement and Save never depend on it;
  - no geocoding, reverse geocoding, external provider, logging, or analytics; the position is persisted only through an explicit Save (existing RPC); no recurring external cost.
- Native permission text (changed in `apps/mobile/app.config.ts`, `expo-location` plugin `locationWhenInUsePermission`): now `'MenizVet usa tu ubicación para mostrarte clínicas veterinarias cercanas y, si gestionas una clínica, para ayudarte a situarla en el mapa.'`, covering both approved foreground uses. It only becomes iOS `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription`, baked into the binary: iOS needs a future native (EAS) build to show it — Metro alone does not change it. Android shows no app-provided purpose text and already declares `ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION`/`ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`, so the current Android development build can test the feature through Metro. No background location is configured on either platform. No build was made.
- Validation (LOCAL, current-location refinement): new `device-location-assist.test.ts` (permission flow, no re-prompt after permanent denial, rounding, services off, unavailable/non-finite/out-of-range/timeout, coordinator, and editor scenarios: nothing on initialization, new and existing locations, address untouched, Save only with a valid address, denial/failure keep the draft, manual placement after denial, manual marker beats a late result, leaving ignores a late result, one lookup for repeated taps, neutral centre never a marker); 7 of 7 behavioural mutants caught (one further mutant — removing the redundant token bump in `supersede` — is equivalent because clearing the pending lookup already invalidates it); Jest 65 suites / 1,537 tests; `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, `npm run format:check` pass; `git diff --check` clean. The screen wiring itself (calling `supersede` from manual placement and Back) is covered by the device checklist, not by a component test.

#### Post-Phase-1 Clinic self-service UX/context polish

**CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT.** A refinement after Phase 1, from the Android screenshot review — not a reopening of Clinic self-service Phase 1 and not a new product feature. Implemented and validated LOCALLY, then physically validated by the user on Android in DEVELOPMENT, where all planned re-test checks passed (see "Closure" at the end of this section). Clinic self-service Phase 1 remains CLOSED. No backend, SQL, migration, RPC, capability, Spec, or domain-policy change. Production has not been deployed or touched.

- Professional Clinics respects the selected Organization:
  - root cause: `/professional` let the User select one Organization, but `/professional/clinics` deliberately listed the Clinics of every Organization (grouped by Organization) — an implementation choice recorded in the SPEC-ORG-008 mobile section, not a Spec requirement: SPEC-ORG-008 BR-8 defines one self-scoped read returning all Organizations' Clinics, and §9 leaves client presentation and navigation out of scope. No Spec was amended;
  - now the list shows only the selected Organization's Clinics (matched by Organization id, never by display name), with the Organization named once under the title instead of on every card; there is no all-Organizations view; the Professional Clinics entry on `/professional` sits with the selected Organization and appears only when that Organization has Professional Clinics;
  - the selection is in-memory presentation state shared by `/professional` and the list (`src/lib/professional-organization-selection.ts`), not a route param — the Clinic screens return with `dismissTo('/professional/clinics')`, which replaces route params. It is re-resolved against every fresh professional-context read (previous pick while still present, else the first Organization); never persisted, never sent to the backend;
  - fails safe: with no selection, or one matching none of the Clinics read (a direct route, ended access), the list shows no Clinic and offers "Volver a MenizVet Profesional / Back to MenizVet Professional"; switching Organizations leaves no stale Clinic (filtered at render from the current selection). The backend read and every Clinic operation remain authoritative.
- Scheduling timezone selected-state feedback: a search result and the device-timezone suggestion now select through one model (`timezoneSelectionState`); an unsaved choice appears right under the current value as a "Zona horaria seleccionada / Selected timezone" block (check icon, city, canonical IANA name, "Pulsa Guardar para aplicarla / Tap Save to apply it") and is announced for screen readers; re-selecting the stored timezone is not an unsaved change; the footer keeps a compact "Sin guardar / Not saved yet" line; Save stays explicit; no auto-save; no backend change.
- Clinic settings visual hierarchy: the clinic name is shown prominently; a "Estado / Status" section holds two separate cards — Visibility (Published / Not published, one short sentence, and the only Publish/Unpublish controls) and Online appointment requests (Setup complete / incomplete / couldn't be checked, one short sentence, the plan notice) — then the "Configuración / Setup" list with uniform rows (title, supporting text, current value, status icon + label, chevron column), dividers, and full wrapping of long values (no truncation of the clinic name or address). Read-only rows show no chevron and are not buttons; the Visibility row stays a read-only status. Visibility, ready-to-publish, and online-request readiness remain distinct and nothing is called "bookable". Publish/Unpublish, row navigation, capability gating, and the direct Appointment hours entry (for Receptionists) are unchanged.
- Validation (LOCAL): Jest 67 suites / 1,602 tests (Organization filtering A/B, switching both ways, same-named Clinics/Organizations not leaking, no or stale selection, backend inclusion kept; selection store; timezone selected-state model for both paths and unchanged-value case; status-card separation, row tones, actionability); 11 of 11 deliberate mobile mutants caught; `npx tsc --noEmit`, strict unused-locals/parameters, `npx eslint .`, and `npm run format:check` pass; `git diff --check` clean. ### Staff access lifecycle Phase 1 (SPEC-ORG-009, SPEC-ORG-010, SPEC-ORG-002, SPEC-ORG-003, SPEC-ORG-004, SPEC-ID-006 BR-40, SPEC-COM-001 BR-43–BR-46)

**Staff access lifecycle Phase 1: CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT. Slice A — View Organization Staff: CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT. Slice B1 — Invite + Revoke Organization Invitation: CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT. Slice B2 — Invitation Preview + Accept: CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT. Slice C — End access: CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT.** The user applied, in order, `20261026080000_temporal_professional_authority.sql` and `20261026090000_organization_staff_read.sql` to DEVELOPMENT. The Staff read and the read-only mobile Staff screen were physically validated on Android there, and the final Professional Home / Staff visual polish was physically re-tested successfully on Android. For Slice B1 the user applied `20261027090000_staff_invitation_invite_and_revoke.sql` to DEVELOPMENT and deployed only the updated `communications` Edge Function; B1 was physically validated on Android there, including a real invitation email delivered end to end (see "Slice B1 — DEVELOPMENT closure" below). For Slice B2 the user applied `20261028090000_staff_invitation_preview_and_accept.sql` to DEVELOPMENT; with the mobile JS loaded through Metro on the existing Android development build, B2 was physically validated on Android there, including the Android-fix re-test (see "Slice B2 — DEVELOPMENT closure" below). For Slice C the user applied `20261029090000_staff_end_access.sql` to DEVELOPMENT; with the mobile JS loaded through Metro on the existing Android development build, Slice C was physically re-tested on Android there after the final Staff member-identity polish and all planned checks passed (see "Slice C — DEVELOPMENT closure" below). **Status: Slice A, Slice B1, Slice B2 and Slice C all CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT; Staff access lifecycle Phase 1 CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT (see "Staff Phase 1 closure" below). Production has not been deployed or touched.** Clinic self-service Phase 1 remains CLOSED. Reusable local verification instructions are in [TESTING.md section 25](TESTING.md#25-local-sql-regression-and-professional-authority-mutation-tooling).

- Purpose: authorized Organization administrators invite, see, and remove staff from the professional app without SQL or `service_role`, and invited people accept through the email link.
- Approved slices:
  - A — Staff read + Staff screen: new SPEC-ORG-009 `get_organization_staff(p_organization_id uuid) returns jsonb` (active Members, their active Responsibility Grants, pending Invitations; requires `ORGANIZATION_INVITE_MEMBERS` or `ORGANIZATION_END_MEMBERSHIPS`; no new capability; nonexistent and inaccessible Organization fail identically with `42501`; `STABLE`, non-locking, one snapshot, no Entitlement). Screen at `Professional → selected Organization → Staff`, route `/professional/organizations/[organizationId]/staff`; the entry shows only with the advisory capabilities above; no all-Organizations screen.
  - B1 — Invite + Revoke: SPEC-ORG-002 §10 (one role/scope per Invitation: Organization Administrator, Organization Receptionist, Clinic Administrator, Clinic Receptionist; no Owner; Veterinarian not offered; client sends no `recipient_user_id`, expiry, or responsibility array; one pending Invitation per Organization and normalized address; former Members re-invitable; active-Member reconciliation without consulting Auth emails) and new SPEC-ORG-010 `revoke_organization_invitation(p_invitation_id uuid) returns jsonb` (`ORGANIZATION_REVOKE_INVITATIONS`; outcomes `REVOKED`/`ALREADY_REVOKED`/`ACCEPTED`/`EXPIRED`/`REJECTED`; idempotent; accepted Invitations not transformed; undelivered email suppressed; Organization → Invitation lock order).
  - B2 — Acceptance: SPEC-ORG-003 §10–§12, `preview_organization_invitation(id, secret)` and `accept_my_organization_invitation(id, secret)`, both `authenticated`-only; the accepting User comes from `auth.uid()`; the secret is hashed inside the database; no `service_role` caller from mobile; the existing service-role `accept_organization_invitation` stays only for trusted compatibility.
  - C — End access: SPEC-ORG-004 §10, `end_organization_staff_membership(p_membership_id uuid) returns jsonb` (`ORGANIZATION_END_MEMBERSHIPS`; all active Grants and the Membership end at one instant; outcomes `ENDED`/`ALREADY_ENDED`; rejects self and any active Owner target); client `EXECUTE` on the more permissive generic `end_organization_membership` is to be withdrawn.
- 7-day expiry: `expires_at = created_at + 7 days`, fixed by trusted backend time; not configurable; never chosen by the client (SPEC-ORG-002 BR-18). The deployed Edge route currently accepts a client `expires_at`; implementation must replace that contract.
- Bearer-secret acceptance model: the Invitation reference proves nothing; acceptance requires an authenticated User with a confirmed email, the reference, and the valid bearer secret; the signed-in email need not match the invited address. Before proof every failure is the uniform `42501` "Invitation unavailable"; after proof, expired/revoked/already-a-Member/unavailable are reported. A lost-response retry by the same User resolves as `ALREADY_ACCEPTED` through Membership provenance, never for another User.
- Staff identity visibility (SPEC-ID-006 BR-40): profile `display_name`, active roles and scopes with Clinic names, Membership start, `is_self`, and for invitation-derived Memberships the administrator-supplied invitation address. Never the Auth email. Fallback "Unnamed member / Miembro sin nombre". Pending Invitations show recipient email, role/scope, Clinic, created and expiry times.
- Owner protection: no End access on any Owner row; an Administrator cannot end a co-Owner through Staff; no self-ending; ownership transfer/removal remains unspecified. Structural "at least one active Owner" and OWNER-Grant protections are unchanged.
- Fail-closed normalization: (1) authentication, (2) nonexistent and inaccessible targets identical `42501`, (3) capability, (4) lifecycle/input. Documented exception: the bearer-secret operations check the caller's own account state, then proof, then lifecycle.
- Immediate authorization loss: capability helpers read live Membership/Grant state and the JWT carries identity only, so ending a Membership removes authority on the next protected call without sign-out (SPEC-ORG-004 BR-25).
- Deferred: post-acceptance Responsibility editing (next follow-up, via `replace_organization_responsibility` one Grant at a time; never end + re-invite), Veterinarian invitation/setup, decline/reject, self-departure, resend, ended-history UI, ownership workflows, iOS universal links. (Clinic creation is a separate product area, not part of Staff; see "Clinic creation self-service Phase 1 — Add Clinic".)
- Android deep-link architecture: email link `https://app.menizvet.com/invitations/organization/accept?invitation=<id>#secret=<secret>`; mobile route outside authenticated navigation; existing Android App Link `pathPrefix /invitations`; secret held in SecureStore while authenticating, never logged, cleared on terminal outcome and sign-out; explicit Accept; extra confirmation when the signed-in email differs; web fallback page for `/invitations/*` is a deployment task.
- Data compatibility: no table change and no backfill; existing Memberships/Grants (including SQL-created DEVELOPMENT Memberships with `originating_invitation_id = null`) and legacy Invitations remain valid; stuck pending Invitations become revocable once SPEC-ORG-010 ships.
- Concurrency rules (all Organization-serialized): duplicate invite race yields one pending Invitation; accept vs revoke yields one winner; accept at the expiry boundary is decided under the Organization lock at the single acceptance instant (backend statement time), which is also the Membership/Grant effective start; double acceptance yields one Membership; ending twice records one end; reads observe one snapshot and never a half-applied transition.
- Slice A final delivered scope (CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT):
  - Backend read: `get_organization_staff(...)` exposes Organization identity, active Memberships, their active Responsibility Grants, and pending Organization Invitations. It is Organization-scoped, read-only, non-locking, requires no Entitlement, is authorized through the approved Organization Staff capabilities (`ORGANIZATION_INVITE_MEMBERS` or `ORGANIZATION_END_MEMBERSHIPS`), and fails closed (identical `42501`) for unknown and inaccessible Organizations.
  - Identity/privacy: approved profile display name only; the Invitation recipient email only as approved Invitation provenance/context; no Auth email; no User id; safe "Miembro sin nombre / Unnamed member" fallback for trusted/SQL fixtures without approved display identity. OWNER and existing VETERINARIAN Responsibilities are visible, although Veterinarian invitation remains deferred.
  - Mobile route: `Professional → selected Organization → Staff`, with explicit Organization route context (`/professional/organizations/[organizationId]/staff`).
  - Professional Home: keeps Organization selection; shows the compact read-only card "Tu acceso en esta organización / Your access in this organization", which does not repeat the selected Organization name and shows one line per active Responsibility; keeps `Gestión / Management`; offers `Personal / Staff` only when the advisory capabilities allow it; Professional Clinics behaviour unchanged.
  - Staff screen hierarchy: `Personal / Staff` title; Organization identity; `Miembros / Members`; `Invitaciones pendientes / Pending invitations`.
  - No mutation actions are part of Slice A.
- Slice A local implementation: `20261026090000_organization_staff_read.sql` adds `get_organization_staff(uuid)` with the exact SPEC-ORG-009 envelope and canonical ordering, a materialized authorization gate, one payload statement/snapshot, and one `statement_timestamp()` instant. `STABLE`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, empty `search_path`, `authenticated`-only execution (including removal of Supabase's automatic `service_role` grant); no table grants, schema structures, backfill, or Staff mutation. This migration contains only the new read and its ACL; shared temporal hardening is separated into the preceding migration.
- Slice A mobile: advisory selected-Organization Staff entry; explicit `/professional/organizations/[organizationId]/staff` route; active Staff and pending Invitations shown separately with ES/EN role/scope labels, safe Invitation identity context and unnamed fallback, start/created/expiry instants, and readable non-button rows. Fresh keyed state isolates Organization changes; latest read wins, blur/unmount invalidate outstanding reads, leaving is terminal, ordinary failed refresh preserves the last result, and `42501` clears managed content and returns calmly to Professional. A successful direct read restores the route's Organization selection for the landing page without using it as read authority.
- Temporal-authority prerequisite: `20261026080000_temporal_professional_authority.sql` precedes Staff and defines one instant per statement, `t = statement_timestamp()`. Evaluation: eight shared evaluators/reads (Organization capability, Organization-scoped and Clinic-scoped Clinic capability, Veterinarian Assignment currentness (Membership dependency only), both locked Veterinarian context resolvers, Professional Context, and reschedule-authority continuity's current-capability gate) treat a Membership/Grant as current iff `effective_start <= t AND effective_end IS NULL`; a recorded future end is not an approved scheduled ending in Phase 1 (SPEC-ORG-004 BR-13, SPEC-ORG-006 section 3). Writes: Organization establishment, Invitation acceptance, add/replace/end Responsibility and end Membership record `t` as effective start/end, never earlier than the start they extend or supersede (previously `clock_timestamp()`, later than `t`, so a composite statement such as ADR-009 interim provisioning could not use the Owner it had just established). Acceptance decides expiry at that same instant (SPEC-ORG-003 BR-5); replacement is contiguous at one instant; audit timestamps are unchanged. Capability mappings, Entitlements, historical interval coverage/provenance, tables, existing data and existing ACLs are unchanged. The Clinic capability wrapper and Professional Clinics read delegate to the corrected evaluators.
- Temporal-authority closure (delivered with Slice A as an explicit cross-cutting prerequisite/correction, not Staff-specific behaviour; applied to DEVELOPMENT): canonical currentness of Organization Memberships and Responsibility Grants in current-authority evaluation is `effective_start <= evaluation instant AND effective_end IS NULL`. The hardening prevents future Memberships/Grants from granting authority early; keeps Organization and Clinic capability evaluation, Professional Context, and Veterinarian current-context evaluation (where Membership/Grant currentness applies) consistent; aligns Membership/Grant writers with the single statement evaluation instant; preserves create-then-act inside trusted atomic operations and provisioning atomicity; and keeps temporal-history semantics where they are intentionally historical. The original same-statement timestamp blocker was resolved by aligning writers and evaluators on that instant, not by splitting provisioning into separate statements.
- Known non-blocking temporal limitations (pre-existing, consciously deferred, not implemented; not blockers for Slice A or B1):
  - Clinic Assignment `effective_start`: current Veterinarian Clinic Assignment currentness does not evaluate `effective_start`. Deferred because scheduled Clinic Assignments are outside the approved current product model, a future-start Assignment is fixture/direct-SQL-only, and approved product APIs do not create that state. If scheduled Assignment starts are ever introduced, both currentness evaluation and its writer timestamp semantics must be revisited.
  - Inline target-row checks: some Organization write RPC target-row checks follow the current Phase 1 model based primarily on ended/non-ended state, rather than independently applying future-start filtering to malformed/direct-SQL target fixtures. Deferred because approved APIs no longer produce lasting future-start Membership/Grant target states, such fixture-only states grant no actor authority, and shared actor capability evaluation is already temporally hardened.
- Provisioning scripts: `production_validation_data.sql` and `production_validation_data_retire.sql` called `replace_clinic_appointment_availability(uuid, jsonb)`, removed by `20261019090000`; both now pass the weekly revision from `get_managed_clinic_appointment_availability`, and are consistent with same-statement temporal authority. Locally (fake project ref, local Vault secret) fresh provisioning, idempotent re-run and retirement all completed in their intended single-transaction form. Not run against any remote project. The retirement script's obsolete "no unpublish RPC exists" comment was later corrected (comment only) to note `unpublish_clinic(uuid)` from Clinic self-service Slice C; its executable statements and retirement behaviour are unchanged.

- Slice B1 delivered scope (CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT; implementation as recorded below):
  - Migration `20261027090000_staff_invitation_invite_and_revoke.sql` (no table, column, constraint, capability, Communication type or backfill):
    - private `assert_organization_invitation_authority(subject, organization)` and service-role `authorize_organization_invitation_creation(subject, organization) returns timestamptz`: authentication (`28000`), then inviter verification (`42501`, hint `ACCOUNT_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED`), then nonexistent and inaccessible Organization as one outcome (`42501`, hint `ORGANIZATION_UNAVAILABLE`), before any input is judged; returns this database's `statement_timestamp()` truncated to milliseconds as the creation instant T;
    - service-role `create_staff_organization_invitation_with_communication(subject, organization, recipient_email, responsibility, scope_type, clinic_id, created_at, digest, key_version, nonce, ciphertext) returns uuid`: re-checks authority under the Organization lock; one responsibility and scope — `ADMINISTRATOR`/`RECEPTIONIST` in either scope, Organization-scoped `VETERINARIAN` kept as a valid backend shape (SPEC-ORG-002 §10, BR-17), never `OWNER`, Clinic-scoped `VETERINARIAN` rejected (`22023` `INVALID_ROLE_SCOPE`); nonexistent and foreign Clinic one outcome (`INVALID_CLINIC`); minimal address syntax (`INVALID_RECIPIENT`); T must be millisecond-precise, not later than the command instant and within one minute of it; stores `created_at = T` and derives `expires_at = T + 168 hours`; one pending Invitation per Organization and normalized address (`23505` `PENDING_INVITATION_EXISTS`); an active Member who joined through an Invitation to the same normalized address is rejected (`23505` `ACTIVE_MEMBER`), never by Auth email; inserts the single Invitation Communication, whose envelope context the existing integrity trigger verifies against the derived expiry;
    - authenticated-only `revoke_organization_invitation(p_invitation_id uuid) returns jsonb` (SPEC-ORG-010): `ORGANIZATION_REVOKE_INVITATIONS`; unknown and inaccessible Invitation one `42501`; Organization → Invitation lock order; one instant `greatest(statement_timestamp(), created_at)`; outcomes `REVOKED`/`ALREADY_REVOKED`/`ACCEPTED`/`EXPIRED`/`REJECTED`, only `REVOKED` writes (`revoked_at`, `revoked_by_user_id`); the digest is retained; no Communication;
    - `mark_communication_delivery_submission_started` now share-locks the Organization Invitation before its final eligibility check, so the provider handoff serializes with revocation (otherwise unchanged).
  - Edge route `communications/create-organization-invitation`: JWT required, subject verified with `auth.getUser`; body exactly `organization_id`, `recipient_email`, `responsibility`, `scope_type`, `clinic_id` (any other member, including `expires_at`, `recipient_user_id` and `intended_responsibilities`, is `400 INVALID_REQUEST` before any call); authorize first, then value checks, then the secret and AES-GCM envelope bound to T + 7 days, then the create command once (never retried). Success `201 { invitation_id }` only. Errors `{ error }`: `401 AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`; `403 ACCOUNT_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED | ORGANIZATION_UNAVAILABLE`; `400 INVALID_REQUEST | INVALID_RECIPIENT | INVALID_ROLE_SCOPE | INVALID_CLINIC`; `409 PENDING_INVITATION_EXISTS | ACTIVE_MEMBER`; `500 INTERNAL_ERROR` — never SQLSTATE, raw errors, digest or envelope. Logic in `_shared/communications/organization-invitation.ts`. The trusted legacy `create_scoped_organization_invitation_with_communication` is unchanged and no longer called by the route.
  - Communication suppression: an Invitation email is not submitted to the provider if revocation commits before the submission-start mark (`mark_communication_delivery_submission_started`); the existing eligibility checks (materialization, claim, context, submission start) apply this. Inherent window: submission-start may win first, and a revocation may then commit after it but before the external provider call; that revocation succeeds (`REVOKED`) but does not claim the already-started handoff was recalled. An email the provider already accepted is not recalled and its history is unchanged; revocation creates no Communication.
  - Staff read: unchanged `get_organization_staff` remains the single source of truth; created Invitations appear on re-read, revoked and terminal ones do not.
  - Mobile: Staff offers `Invitar / Invite` only with advisory `ORGANIZATION_INVITE_MEMBERS`, and `Revocar invitación / Revoke invitation` on each pending Invitation only with `ORGANIZATION_REVOKE_INVITATIONS` (from the same read's professional context; unreadable context → no actions); active members have no actions. Invite route `/professional/organizations/[organizationId]/staff/invite`: email, Administrator/Receptionist, whole Organization or one Clinic of the route Organization (Professional Clinics filtered to that Organization); switching scope clears the Clinic; Send only when complete; 7-day note; no expiry, User, Owner or Veterinarian. Revoke requires explicit confirmation; every outcome and every apparent failure is followed by the authoritative re-read. Lost invite response: never retried; success only when a pending Invitation absent from the pre-submit read matches address, role, scope and Clinic exactly; an older pending one is reported as the conflict; otherwise "couldn't confirm". Lost revoke response: "no longer pending" when the re-read shows it gone, never "revoked". `42501` leaves calmly to Professional. Keyed routes isolate Organizations. No native dependency; the design-system Button gained an optional `accessibilityLabel`.
  - Automated validation (LOCAL): clean reset applies the migration; all **32 SQL suites PASS**, including `staff_invitation_invite_revoke_spec_org_002_010.sql`; **31/31 SQL faults caught** (`run_staff_invitation_faults.py`); **7 real two-session races PASS** on committed-then-deleted LOCAL fixtures (`run_staff_invitation_concurrency.py`: duplicate invite, revoke vs revoke, revoke→accept, accept→revoke, across the expiry boundary, revoke→handoff, handoff→revoke); Communications Deno tests **23/23 PASS**; **15/15 Edge faults caught** (`run_edge_invitation_faults.py`); Deno fmt/lint and Communications typecheck PASS; mobile **77 suites / 1,715 tests PASS**; **17/17 ad hoc mobile faults caught**; typecheck, strict unused, ESLint and Prettier PASS; zero retained fixture rows.
  - Delivered scope summary:
    - Invite: authenticated Organization-management workflow; mobile sends only `organization_id`, `recipient_email`, `responsibility`, `scope_type`, `clinic_id`; the client cannot choose `expires_at`, `created_at`, a recipient User id, the secret, digest or envelope; trusted backend creation instant; expiry exactly 168 hours; secret generated only in trusted Edge code; no secret, digest or envelope returned to mobile; one pending Invitation per Organization + normalized email; active-Member provenance conflict handled; former Members can be invited again; the Phase 1 mobile form offers Administrator / Receptionist for Organization / Clinic scope; backend compatibility for the approved Organization-scoped `VETERINARIAN` shape remains while mobile does not offer it; `OWNER` and Clinic-scoped `VETERINARIAN` remain unsupported.
    - Revoke: authenticated RPC requiring `ORGANIZATION_REVOKE_INVITATIONS`; outcomes `REVOKED` / `ALREADY_REVOKED` / `ACCEPTED` / `EXPIRED` / `REJECTED`; only `REVOKED` writes; digest retained; an accepted Membership is untouched; no new Communication; undelivered email suppressed under the submission-start ordering rule above.
    - Staff integration: `get_organization_staff` remains the authoritative source; a successful invite appears as pending; a revoked Invitation disappears from pending; no secondary invitation-management read was added.
    - Lost-response semantics: the invite mutation is never retried automatically; mobile reconciles as success only when an authoritative newly-created matching pending Invitation proves it; revoke reconciliation never says "revoked" merely because the Invitation is no longer pending; the authoritative re-read wins.

- Slice B2 delivered scope (CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT; implementation as recorded below, closure evidence in "Slice B2 — DEVELOPMENT closure"):
  - Migration `20261028090000_staff_invitation_preview_and_accept.sql` (no table, column, constraint, capability, Communication type or backfill):
    - `preview_organization_invitation(p_invitation_id uuid, p_acceptance_secret text) returns jsonb` — `authenticated` only, `SECURITY DEFINER`, empty `search_path`, `STABLE`, writes nothing, takes no row lock, no confirmed email required (BR-22). Order: authentication (`28000`) → same-User provenance (`ACCEPTED_BY_YOU`, BR-21) → proof (SHA-256 of the exact UTF-8 secret against the stored digest; otherwise the uniform `42501` "Invitation unavailable") → lifecycle at one backend instant. States `PENDING`/`EXPIRED`/`REVOKED`/`ALREADY_MEMBER`/`ACCEPTED_BY_YOU`/`UNAVAILABLE`; payload exactly SPEC-ORG-003 §10 (Organization display name, intended responsibilities with Clinic identity, masked recipient `first character + *** + @domain`, `invited_email_matches_current_account` boolean only, `expires_at`); `UNAVAILABLE` discloses nothing else; never the secret, digest, envelope, Organization id, User ids or unmasked address.
    - `accept_my_organization_invitation(p_invitation_id uuid, p_acceptance_secret text) returns jsonb` — `authenticated` only, `SECURITY DEFINER`, empty `search_path`; no User/Membership/responsibility/Organization/Clinic/digest input. Order: authentication + coherent domain User (`28000`) → confirmed Auth email (`42501`, hint `ACCOUNT_VERIFICATION_REQUIRED`) → Organization → Invitation `FOR UPDATE` (unknown = uniform) → same-User provenance (`ALREADY_ACCEPTED`, creates nothing) → proof (uniform) → lifecycle, writing nothing: revoked (`23514` `INVITATION_REVOKED`), rejected / bound to another User / responsibilities no longer valid (`23514` `INVITATION_UNAVAILABLE`), expired (`23514` `INVITATION_EXPIRED`), caller already an active Member (`23505` `ALREADY_MEMBER`) → Clinics in id order, one Membership and exactly the recorded Grants at one effective start `greatest(statement_timestamp(), created_at)` (also the expiry instant), Grants keep their Invitation origin and inviting User, digest erased — atomically. Result `{ invitation_id, organization_id, membership_id, outcome: ACCEPTED | ALREADY_ACCEPTED }`. No Communication.
    - Idempotency / lost response (BR-21): a retry by the same accepting User resolves through Membership provenance (`originating_invitation_id`) as `ALREADY_ACCEPTED` (preview: `ACCEPTED_BY_YOU`) with the same Membership, creating nothing; there is never a duplicate active Membership; any other User gets the uniform unavailable failure and never learns who accepted; once that Membership has ended, a retry is the uniform failure and the Membership is not reactivated.
    - Post-proof precedence (both operations): revoked, rejected, expired, bound to another User, invalid responsibilities, already a Member — recorded terminal facts before elapsed time, matching `revoke_organization_invitation`. The Auth email is never proof; a different verified signed-in address may accept with the secret.
    - The trusted service-role `accept_organization_invitation` keeps its contract, error outcomes and ACL (no client role executes it); it now shares the private responsibility-validity predicate and Membership/Grant/digest writer with the authenticated operation, so the two cannot drift. Private helpers are executable by no role.
  - Mobile (no native dependency, no `app.config.ts` change):
    - Route `/invitations/organization/accept` (`src/app/invitations/organization/accept.tsx`), registered unconditionally in the root layout like reset-password, so it opens signed out, cold and warm.
    - Fragment capture: Expo Router 57 copies a URL fragment into route params (`#`), so `src/app/+native-intent.ts` (`redirectSystemPath`, cold-start initial URL and running-app URLs) parses the full URL, stages `{ invitationId, secret }` in memory for the screen, and rewrites the target to the bare route; the secret never enters route params, navigation state or history (pinned against Expo Router's own `getStateFromPath`). Shape validation: UUID reference in the query, exactly one base64url `secret` in the fragment; a secret in the query or any other fragment member is invalid and nothing is stored.
    - SecureStore pending proof: key `menizvet.pendingOrganizationInvitation.v1`, value `{ version: 1, invitationId, secret }` only; a newer link replaces the older one; corrupt/stale/ill-shaped values are deleted; a failed keystore write keeps the proof in process memory only (and drops any older stored proof); writes and reads are serialized; only the key name is ever logged. Cleared after every terminal outcome (including the uniform failure), after "Not now", after acceptance, and in the centralized sign-out — never on opening sign-in/registration, on unmount or on backgrounding.
    - Signed out: generic copy only ("Has recibido una invitación para unirte a una organización en MenizVet…"), Sign in / Create account / Not now; no preview call. The screen stays mounted under the auth screens and previews once a session exists; after an app restart (for example registration + email confirmation outside the app), the root `usePendingOrganizationInvitationResume` reopens the screen on the next sign-in when a proof is pending and the screen is not already open. Bootstrap with an existing session and password recovery never resume; nothing auto-accepts.
    - Signed in: Organization, role/scope with Clinic names (ES/EN labels, no raw enums), masked invited address, validity; explicit Accept and Not now; no Decline. Unconfirmed account (advisory session flag, backend authoritative): Accept disabled with a confirm-email message. Different address: warning with the masked address, then a second explicit confirmation stating that access will be attached to the currently signed-in MenizVet account (Confirm / Cancel).
    - Terminal states `EXPIRED`, `REVOKED`, `ALREADY_MEMBER`, `ACCEPTED_BY_YOU`, `UNAVAILABLE`/uniform: calm dedicated copy, proof cleared, no retry action; `ALREADY_MEMBER` and `ACCEPTED_BY_YOU` offer the Professional area.
    - Success: proof cleared → professional context re-read → the accepted Organization/Membership must appear → selection set → `/professional`. No optimistic authority. Context re-read failure: calm "accepted, couldn't load" state with "Load my access" (re-reads context only, never re-accepts). Any other acceptance failure or lost response re-runs the preview; only when it reports `ACCEPTED_BY_YOU` is the idempotent acceptance called once more to obtain the Membership; a still-pending Invitation is shown with a calm failure and never retried automatically.
  - Mobile, after the DEVELOPMENT Android findings (JS only; no native or config change):
    - "Not now / Ahora no" is local abandonment only: it drops a not-yet-taken staged link, clears the in-process proof and the SecureStore key `menizvet.pendingOrganizationInvitation.v1`, cancels in-flight invitation flow work, and replaces to `/(auth)` signed out or `/(app)` signed in (the repository's convention); repeated taps are ignored. It calls no RPC, does not revoke or reject the server Invitation, and does not sign out. Root cause of the earlier defect: it replaced to `/`, which Expo Router resolves to `(app)/index` regardless of session (both `(app)/index` and `(auth)/index` serve `/`); signed out, `Stack.Protected` has removed `(app)`, so the action was dropped and the screen stayed. The terminal-state Close had the same defect and is fixed the same way.
    - Initial-URL timeout gap (a real app issue, independent of the development client): Expo Router 57 on Android reads the initial URL as `Linking.getInitialURL()` raced against a 150 ms timeout and substitutes the root URL when it loses, which could drop an Invitation link on a slow cold start. The initial `redirectSystemPath` call also checks the launch URL cached natively at Activity creation (expo-linking `getLinkingURL()`) through the same interception point, and clears that cache once an Invitation link is captured so a JavaScript reload cannot re-capture it.

  - Android-fix pass (LOCAL, JS only): final mobile Jest **88 suites / 1,847 tests PASS** (adds route-resolution of the exit destinations against Expo Router's own route tree, the Not-now lifecycle, and native-intent cold-start intake order); **8/8 mutation checks of the two fixes caught**; the earlier **22/22 B2 mobile deliberate faults still caught**; typecheck, strict unused-locals/parameters, ESLint, Prettier PASS; `git diff --check` clean. SQL was not rerun for this pass: no backend file changed after the green B2 backend validation above.

  - Registration / email-verification continuation (the real invitation recipient, who initially had no MenizVet account): invitation opened signed out → Create account → registration completed → email confirmation and sign-in completed → the preview resumed automatically, without reopening the email. This physically validates the SecureStore proof surviving registration, verification and authentication.

  - Correct-account acceptance: the pending invitation was accepted successfully; the app returned to MenizVet Professional; the new Organization/Clinic access appeared immediately, without logout/login or token refresh; the authoritative professional context reflected the new Membership and Responsibility.
  - Different-email acceptance: the signed-in account's email differed from the invited address; the preview showed the masked invited address; the first Accept did not mutate; an explicit second confirmation was required; after confirming, acceptance succeeded for the currently signed-in verified account. Intentional policy: possession of the mailbox secret is the proof.
