#### Professional Longitudinal History — DEVELOPMENT closure (user-confirmed)

- DEVELOPMENT state and ACL verification (checked by the user in DEVELOPMENT; nothing below is claimed beyond what was physically checked):
  - applied: `20261105090000_professional_longitudinal_history.sql`;
  - all six checked functions are SECURITY DEFINER with `search_path=""`:
    - `clinical_history_amendments(uuid)` (private shared projection): STABLE, `anon` false, `authenticated` false, `service_role` false;
    - `clinical_history_page(uuid,integer,timestamptz,uuid)` (private shared projection): STABLE, `anon` false, `authenticated` false, `service_role` false;
    - `clinical_provenance_display_name(uuid)` (private): STABLE, `anon` false, `authenticated` false, `service_role` false;
    - `view_appointment_professional_medical_history(uuid,uuid,integer,timestamptz,uuid)`: VOLATILE, `anon` false, `authenticated` true, `service_role` false;
    - `view_guardian_pet_medical_history(uuid,integer,timestamptz,uuid)`: VOLATILE, `anon` false, `authenticated` true, `service_role` false;
    - legacy `view_professional_pet_medical_history(uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid,integer,timestamptz,uuid)`: VOLATILE, `anon` false, `authenticated` false, `service_role` true;
  - the new Appointment-keyed Professional read is the authenticated client entry point; the legacy Professional read is no longer authenticated-client callable and remains trusted/internal; the shared helpers are private; Guardian access remains authenticated. VOLATILE is intentional for the two public reads: they write medical-access audit events.
- Delivered Professional history model: an authorized Veterinarian acts through one exact Clinic / Appointment context. That Clinic-specific access grant authorizes a longitudinal read of the Pet's complete FINALIZED clinical history across the current Clinic, sibling Clinics of the same Organization, and other Organizations. The access grant is Clinic-specific; the returned historical content is not restricted to the current Clinic or Organization. This is not Organization-wide authority.
- Access window (SPEC-MED-005 BR-40, unchanged):
  - pre-care — a future `REQUESTED` Appointment with no Encounter, or a `CONFIRMED` Appointment with no Encounter; `ACTIVE` Appointment-derived Care Authorization; `CLINICAL_CARE` capability and `CLINICAL_CARE` Entitlement required;
  - during care — Encounter `ACTIVE`, Care Authorization `ACTIVE`, Entitlement present;
  - denied — a `REQUESTED` Appointment whose start has passed; `COMPLETED` with no Encounter; a `CONCLUDED` Encounter; `REVOKED` or `ENDED` Care Authorization; lapsed `CLINICAL_CARE` Entitlement; a foreign Clinic or unauthorized caller;
  - retrospective `COMPLETED` documentation — before the Encounter starts, history unavailable; while the retrospective Encounter is `ACTIVE`, available; after Conclude, unavailable. Physically validated on Android.
- No Appointment Veterinarian Assignment and no Veterinarian Availability are required. The clinical authority chain remains: active Membership → Organization-scoped `VETERINARIAN` Responsibility → Professional Profile → current Clinic Assignment → required clinical capability → Entitlement where SPEC-MED-005 requires it. This preserves the small-clinic / low-configuration workflow.
- Read on demand: the Appointment detail never reads clinical history automatically; **Ver historial clínico / View medical history** is an explicit action, so opening the Appointment detail stays audit-neutral for medical history. History is read on explicit open, Retry and Load more — never on every focus — which intentionally avoids medical-access audit spam.
- Navigation: the Professional history route is Appointment-keyed. Entry points: the Appointment detail during valid pre-care access, and the Clinical Record screen while its Encounter is `ACTIVE`. Not offered on a `COMPLETED` Appointment with no Encounter, after Encounter Conclude, or after longitudinal-access loss. No top-level history module, Clinic selector or Organization selector was added.
- Provenance identity (Professional-only, SPEC-ID-006 BR-41): the historically attributed User's `user_profiles.display_name`, the Clinic display name and the Organization display name. Resolving the historical author requires no current Membership, no current `VETERINARIAN` status and no current Clinic Assignment, so an offboarded historical author remains meaningfully attributed; a renamed User shows their current display name; a missing or blank name falls back to **Veterinario / Veterinarian**. Never exposed: Auth email, invitation address, registration identifier, internal User ids, Membership ids, Clinic Assignment ids, inferred `Dr.` / `Dra.` titles. Guardian Veterinarian-name visibility remains a separate deferred decision.
- Provenance roles: the original author is preserved; the finalizer is represented only when meaningfully different (it never replaces the original authorship); each Amendment carries its own author provenance; original authorship survives Staff offboarding.
- Payload safety: the Professional payload exposes only product-safe fields; the only client-visible ids intentionally retained are the Entry id and the Amendment id — no User, Auth, Membership, Clinic Assignment, Care Authorization, Encounter or unrelated Appointment ids. The Guardian history payload was hardened in the same slice: internal User / Clinic Assignment / workflow ids removed; Guardian presentation unchanged; Guardian Veterinarian names NOT added; the Attachment id remains where the existing Guardian download requires it.
- Ordering and pagination: a flat Entry timeline, newest first, ordered by `coalesce(clinical_event_at, encounter.started_at)` with a stable Entry-id tie-breaker; `finalized_at` is secondary provenance, not the care date; Amendments never move their parent Entry in the timeline. Keyset pagination, default page size 50; authorization is re-checked on every page, and a denied page clears the already loaded clinical content.
- Amendment presentation: Professional longitudinal history is read-only. Each FINALIZED Entry stays visible as the original, shows its correction count, and lists its Amendments below it with reason, content, time and provenance. No client-side "effective corrected record" replaces the original. No **Añadir corrección** action exists from longitudinal history; Amendments remain actionable from the Appointment Clinical Record under the approved D2 rules.
- Audit: one medical-history audit event per successful page/call — none per Entry or per Amendment; denied cases are audited according to the resolved Appointment/context rules; an unresolvable target before authorized Appointment resolution creates no false medical-access event; the audit event is written in the same transaction before the page is released, so an audit failure never returns unaudited medical data.
- Physical validation in DEVELOPMENT (Android; user-confirmed):
  1. a future `REQUESTED` Appointment: the Appointment detail does not fetch history automatically; tapping **Ver historial clínico** opens the history;
  2. the history displays correctly: current-Clinic records, cross-Clinic records, cross-Organization records where fixture data exists, Clinic names, Organization names and Veterinarian names;
  3. historical / offboarded provenance remained meaningful where tested;
  4. Amendments: the original remains visible, corrections display below it, the correction count is shown, and no Amendment action is offered from history;
  5. no Appointment Veterinarian Assignment is required;
  6. no Veterinarian Availability is required;
  7. `CONFIRMED` / pre-care: history available; after the Encounter starts it remains available while `ACTIVE`; after Conclude it becomes unavailable;
  8. `COMPLETED` without Encounter: history unavailable; after the retrospective Encounter starts it becomes available while `ACTIVE`; after Conclude it becomes unavailable;
  9. additional exploratory Android testing was performed and no other defect was confirmed.
- Android validation finding resolved during validation (UX only — mobile presentation simplification; no backend field, no new audited read, no Spec or confidentiality change):
  - finding: a `CONFIRMED` Appointment with an Encounter kept showing **Atención clínica iniciada / Clinical care started** on the Appointment detail after the Encounter had been CONCLUDED;
  - root cause: `has_clinical_encounter` is intentionally lifecycle-agnostic under SPEC-APT-012 BR-48 (true for an `ACTIVE` and a `CONCLUDED` Encounter alike, with no Encounter state). It was not fixed by exposing Encounter state;
  - final decision: the Appointment detail no longer shows the clinical-care-started notice in any lifecycle; no replacement `Atención terminada` message was added; the Encounter lifecycle remains visible only inside the Clinical Record; the action suppression based on Encounter existence (Cancel, No-show, Propose a new time) and Complete are unchanged;
  - mobile validation after the notice removal: affected Jest 166/166 PASS; full Jest **112 suites / 2,289 tests PASS**; typecheck, strict unused locals/parameters, ESLint, Prettier PASS; `git diff --check` clean.
- Automated validation figures for the slice (LOCAL, unchanged by the UX finding): clean local reset; **44/44 SQL suites PASS**, zero retained fixture rows; Professional history deliberate faults **33/33** caught; Professional history real two-session races **9/9** PASS, no deadlocks; all regression runners PASS — Clinical Record faults 42/42, Clinical Amendments faults 29/29, temporal 14/14 + 5/5, Staff 22/22, B1 31/31, B2 29/29, End access 19/19, Clinic creation 20/20, Subscription 23/23 + 4/4, Trusted establishment 25/25, Workforce 45/45, Edge invitation 15/15, harness 9/9. Mobile: **112 suites / 2,290 tests** at implementation time, **112 suites / 2,289 tests** after the UX simplification — the difference is the removed obsolete notice/helper test, not a regression.
- Automated-only (not physically reproduced on device; covered by the automated suites): Guardian revocation during longitudinal access; Subscription / Entitlement lapse; read vs revoke race; read vs Staff offboarding race; read vs Amendment race; read vs Finalize race; pagination vs concurrent Amendment; foreign / unknown target confidentiality cases.
- Legacy path disposition: `view_professional_pet_medical_history` is no longer authenticated-client callable; it remains trusted/internal; the Appointment-keyed `view_appointment_professional_medical_history` is the Professional product entry point. Carried forward as non-blocking hardening before Production (**not fixed**): `view_clinical_record_entry` is still `authenticated`-callable and returns internal ids; `get_professional_pet_active_care_authorizations` still exposes Care Authorization ids and has no consumer.
- Clinical Phase 1 status: **Clinical Phase 1 remains OPEN pending the final P0 scope/closure review.** The history / cross-Clinic / provenance P0 gap is now CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT. Questions to review before Clinical Phase 1 closure include: Clinical Intake / walk-in scope; Guardian Care Authorization revocation UI; normal Care Authorization ending / the unresolved-Appointment policy; the overall Phase-1 closure criteria. Attachments remain P1/deferred and are not promoted to P0; Guardian Veterinarian-name exposure remains separate/deferred.
- Next prioritized activity: **Clinical Phase 1 P0 closure review** — a review, not another large implementation started automatically. It decides exactly: (1) whether Clinical Intake / walk-in is required for Phase-1 closure; (2) whether Guardian Care Authorization revocation UI is required for closure; (3) whether normal Care Authorization ending must be resolved now; (4) whether unresolved past `CONFIRMED` Appointments block closure; (5) whether any other P0 remains. After those decisions, choose the smallest remaining implementation slice.
- Production: **Production has not been deployed or touched.** `20261105090000_professional_longitudinal_history.sql` remains pending there. The Production rollout must retain the migration order, the existing preflight requirements and the privacy-hardening follow-ups above; Production clinical use is not ready yet.

### Clinical Phase 1 P0 closure review — approved decisions

The read-only closure review is done. Decisions approved by the user (closed):

- **D-CLOSE-1 — Clinical Intake / walk-in client workflow: DEFERRED.** REQ-MED-013's P0 representability is already satisfied by the domain/backend (`CLINICAL_INTAKE` Authorization, Encounter without Appointment, trusted writers). No walk-in UI, Pet search, Intake mobile flow or Intake authorization ending in Phase 1. Its client grant is withdrawn as hardening (below).
- **D-CLOSE-2 — Guardian revocation: DELIVERED and CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** by the slice below.
- **D-CLOSE-3 — generic normal Care Authorization ending: DEFERRED by Phase-1 policy.** No generic normal-end operation (SPEC-ACC-001 BR-42, SPEC-ACC-002 §14). After conclusion, an `ACTIVE` authorization grants only the Encounter's own record and Amendments (no longitudinal browsing); the Guardian can close that too.
- **D-CLOSE-4 — past unresolved `CONFIRMED` Appointment access: NOT a DEVELOPMENT closure blocker; MANDATORY policy review before Production clinical use** (it keeps pre-care longitudinal access open, without a time bound, while the authorization is `ACTIVE`; see "Known unresolved policy").
- **D-CLOSE-5 — closure terminology:** Clinical Phase 1 core / P0 may close while P1 follow-ups remain open. Applied: **Clinical Phase 1 core / P0 is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (below); this does not claim every Clinical Phase-1 P1 item is complete.
- Also recorded: Attachments remain P1 (professional upload not delivered); Appointment Veterinarian Assignment and Veterinarian Availability remain deferred and are not prerequisites.

### Guardian Appointment Medical Access Revocation — Phase 1 (SPEC-ACC-002 §16/§17 BR-56–BR-63, SPEC-APT-011 BR-35, SPEC-ACC-001 BR-41)

- Status: **Guardian Appointment Medical Access Revocation — Phase 1: CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT.**
  - Implemented and fully validated locally; the user applied `20261106090000_guardian_appointment_medical_access_revocation.sql` to DEVELOPMENT, physically verified the ACLs there, and physically validated the Guardian revocation workflow and its UX polish on Android (see the DEVELOPMENT closure below).
  - **Production has not been deployed or touched.** `20261106090000` remains pending there.
- Pre-implementation audit: no contradiction with the approved rules. Any current Guardian may revoke (SPEC-ACC-002 BR-3); revocation keeps the Appointment (BR-16) and every clinical row (BR-12/BR-13); the existing predicates already deny professional longitudinal history after revocation (SPEC-MED-005 BR-40), keep an `ACTIVE` Encounter's own record (SPEC-MED-009 BR-4) and deny a `CONCLUDED` + `REVOKED` record and client Amendments (decision D2). The old client paths were unsuitable: `revoke_appointment_care_authorization` revealed Appointment / Care Authorization existence (`23503`) before authority and reported raw errors, `revoke_pet_care_authorization` required a Care Authorization id; neither had a mobile caller. SPEC-APT-011 BR-20 already allows surfacing authorization status where the product requires it.
- Specs: SPEC-ACC-002 §16 (BR-56 operation, BR-57 authority before existence, BR-58 outcomes, BR-59 replay, BR-60 Appointment and clinical record unchanged, BR-61 effects through the existing predicates, BR-62 withdrawn client paths, BR-63 read state, mobile presentation) and §17 (Notes 40–50); SPEC-APT-011 BR-35 and Notes 36–37; SPEC-ACC-001 BR-41 note (no Intake client workflow).
- Migration (no table, column, constraint or trigger change):
  - `revoke_my_appointment_care_authorization(p_appointment_id uuid) → jsonb`: SECURITY DEFINER, VOLATILE, `search_path=""`, `authenticated` only (`public`, `anon`, `service_role` revoked). Order: authenticate (`28000`) → shape (`22023 INVALID_APPOINTMENT`) → the caller's current Guardianship of the Appointment's Pet resolved together with the Appointment (foreign, unknown, formerly guarded: one uniform `42501 Appointment medical access unavailable`, no hint) → locks Appointment (`for share`) → Guardianship (`for update`, re-verified) → the Appointment-derived Care Authorization (`for update`) → reconcile → the existing trusted `revoke_care_authorization_after_guardianship_lock` (no reason). Outcomes `{"outcome":"REVOKED"}` / `{"outcome":"ALREADY_INACTIVE"}` only. No audit event (revocation is not a medical access; the row keeps revoker and time);
  - `get_pet_appointments` gains the trailing `medical_access_state` (`ACTIVE` / `REVOKED` / `INACTIVE`) of the Appointment's own Appointment-derived Care Authorization — no identifier or metadata; the Clinic schedule is unchanged;
  - authenticated `EXECUTE` withdrawn from `revoke_pet_care_authorization(uuid,text)`, `revoke_appointment_care_authorization(uuid,text)` and `grant_pet_care_authorization(uuid,uuid)` (deferred Clinical Intake; no mobile caller); `service_role` keeps them; nothing is deleted.
- Behaviour pinned: Appointment lifecycle, schedule, Lifecycle Events, Reschedule Proposals and Assignments unchanged; no cancellation; Encounters, Entries, Drafts, Amendments and audit events unchanged; Guardian history unchanged; professional longitudinal history denied immediately (before care and during an `ACTIVE` Encounter); an `ACTIVE` Encounter keeps its own read, Draft, Finalize, Amendment and Conclude; after conclusion (or when already `CONCLUDED`, `CONFIRMED` or `COMPLETED`) its record and client Amendments are denied; no Encounter can be established afterwards. Available whenever the authorization is `ACTIVE`, including `COMPLETED` Appointments. No Clinic notification, no reason, no Professional / Receptionist / Administrator surface change.
- Mobile (Guardian Appointment detail): a **Acceso médico / Medical access** card while `medical_access_state` is `ACTIVE` (any lifecycle), with one explanatory line and **Retirar acceso médico / Withdraw medical access**; a confirmation dialog (the Appointment stays, existing records stay, the Clinic stops seeing the Pet's broader history for this Appointment, care already in progress can be finished; no reason; **Volver / Go back** changes nothing); single-flight; after any outcome a fresh `get_pet_appointments` read decides the view — `REVOKED` shows the calm **Acceso médico retirado / Medical access withdrawn** card (also announced for accessibility), so a lost response or exact retry is never an error; `INACTIVE` after a concurrent cancellation shows a calm note; a real failure keeps the action with a retry message; the uniform denial shows a calm unavailable message. No Care Authorization, capability, Entitlement, state or id wording. The data layer validates `medical_access_state` strictly and sends only the Appointment id. No dependency, no native change, no new build.
- UX/copy refinement (mobile only; no backend, Spec, lifecycle or access change): the action uses the app's destructive (soft, non-primary) button hierarchy with the accessibility label **Retirar acceso médico de esta cita / Withdraw medical access for this appointment**; the card says the Clinic has access to the Pet's medical history through this Appointment; the confirmation (**Retirar acceso médico**, **Volver** / **Retirar acceso**) states, in order, that the Appointment and existing clinical records stay while the Clinic loses the Pet's clinical history **including its own earlier records at that Clinic**; that care not yet started cannot be started with this Appointment while care already started can be finished and recorded; and that the action **cannot be undone for this Appointment** — a new Appointment is needed to authorize medical access again. The withdrawn card reads "La cita se mantiene, pero esta clínica ya no tiene acceso médico mediante esta cita." with no restore action. Recorded product limitations: Phase-1 Guardian revocation is intentionally irreversible for the originating Appointment; reauthorization on that Appointment is not implemented; a future Appointment establishes a new access context; whether a Clinic should keep access to its own historical records after revocation is a future product-policy review — the current model revokes longitudinal access uniformly.
- Callers migrated: the MED-001 after-completion, Minimal Clinical Record, Amendment and Professional history suites revoke through the new client operation; four suites pin the extended Guardian read signature; `appointment_request_expiry_spec_apt_015.sql` grants its Intake authorization as the owner. Existing race runners call the generic revoke as the local superuser and are unaffected. Mobile fixtures gain `medicalAccessState`.
- Automated validation (LOCAL, clean `supabase db reset --local`): **45/45 SQL suites PASS** (new `guardian_appointment_medical_access_revocation_spec_acc_002.sql`, prefix `d0370000-`), zero public/Auth rows afterwards; **22/22 revocation deliberate faults caught** (`run_care_authorization_revocation_faults.py`); **16/16 real two-session races PASS** (`run_care_authorization_revocation_concurrency.py`, prefix `d0372000-`: revoke vs history read, Draft update during an `ACTIVE` Encounter, post-care Amendment, Clinic cancellation, the Guardian's own cancellation, `NO_SHOW`, request expiry and a second revocation, each in both orders), no deadlock, zero retained fixtures; regressions PASS with restoration verified — temporal 14/14 + 5/5, Staff 22/22, B1 31/31, B2 29/29, End access 19/19, Clinic creation 20/20, Subscription 27/27, Trusted establishment 25/25, Workforce 45/45, Clinical record 42/42, Clinical Amendments 29/29, Professional history 33/33, Edge invitation 15/15, every race runner, harness 9/9; mobile **Jest 114 suites / 2,330 tests**, typecheck, strict unused locals/parameters, ESLint, Prettier PASS; **8/8 ad hoc mobile mutants caught**; `git diff --check` clean.
- UX-only polish validation (mobile copy/style; no SQL change, so SQL was not rerun for it): affected Jest 3 suites / 114 tests PASS; full **Jest 114 suites / 2,331 tests PASS**; typecheck, strict unused locals/parameters, ESLint, Prettier PASS; `git diff --check` clean.
- Known non-blocking follow-ups (not fixed, carried forward as before-Production hardening): `view_clinical_record_entry` (authenticated, internal ids, Drafts); `get_professional_pet_active_care_authorizations` and `get_guardian_pet_active_care_authorizations` (authorization ids, no consumer). D-CLOSE-4 policy review before Production clinical use.
- DEVELOPMENT checklist (prepared before the rollout; since executed by the user — only what is recorded in the DEVELOPMENT closure below is claimed): (1) apply `20261106090000`; (2) ACLs — `revoke_my_appointment_care_authorization(uuid)`: SECURITY DEFINER, VOLATILE, `search_path=""`, `anon` false, `authenticated` true, `service_role` false; `revoke_pet_care_authorization(uuid,text)`, `revoke_appointment_care_authorization(uuid,text)`, `grant_pet_care_authorization(uuid,uuid)`: `authenticated` false, `anon` false, `service_role` true; `get_pet_appointments(uuid)`: STABLE, `authenticated` true, `anon` false, last column `medical_access_state text`; (3) as a real Guardian open an Appointment with active medical access; (4) see **Retirar acceso médico**; (5) the dialog says the Appointment stays and records remain; (6) **Volver** changes nothing; (7) confirm; (8) the Appointment stays visible and unchanged; (9) the action disappears and **Acceso médico retirado** shows; (10) the Pet's Guardian medical history is unchanged; (11) as the Veterinarian, **Ver historial clínico** for that Appointment is unavailable; (12) with an `ACTIVE` Encounter: its Clinical record still works (Draft, Finalize, Amendment, Terminar atención) while longitudinal history is unavailable; (13) after `CONCLUDED` + revoked: the Clinical record is unavailable and no correction can be added; (14) a `COMPLETED` Appointment with active access: the action is offered and works; (15) reopening or retrying shows no error and creates nothing new (one `REVOKED` row, unchanged revoker/time); (16) no Appointment Veterinarian Assignment or Availability is involved.

#### Guardian Appointment Medical Access Revocation — DEVELOPMENT closure (user-confirmed)

- DEVELOPMENT state and ACL verification (checked by the user in DEVELOPMENT; nothing below is claimed beyond what was physically checked):
  - applied: `20261106090000_guardian_appointment_medical_access_revocation.sql`;
  - all five checked functions are SECURITY DEFINER with `search_path=""`:
    - `get_pet_appointments(uuid)`: STABLE, `anon` false, `authenticated` true, `service_role` true;
    - `grant_pet_care_authorization(uuid,uuid)`: VOLATILE, `anon` false, `authenticated` false, `service_role` true;
    - `revoke_appointment_care_authorization(uuid,text)`: VOLATILE, `anon` false, `authenticated` false, `service_role` true;
    - `revoke_my_appointment_care_authorization(uuid)`: VOLATILE, `anon` false, `authenticated` true, `service_role` false;
    - `revoke_pet_care_authorization(uuid,text)`: VOLATILE, `anon` false, `authenticated` false, `service_role` true;
  - the Appointment-keyed `revoke_my_appointment_care_authorization` is the authenticated client entry point; the generic revoke operations are trusted / `service_role` only; the Clinical Intake grant is not client-callable while Intake remains deferred; the Guardian Appointment read keeps its existing `authenticated` / `service_role` availability.
- Final Guardian revocation semantics: a current Guardian withdraws the Appointment-derived medical access while keeping the Appointment itself. Revocation changes the Care Authorization `ACTIVE → REVOKED`; it does not cancel the Appointment, delete clinical records, change finalized history, remove Guardian history, notify the Clinic or require a reason. The client sends only the Appointment id; an unknown, foreign or no-longer-authorized Appointment fails uniformly and reveals no existence.
- Idempotency and replay: the first successful revoke returns `REVOKED`; a retry, a lost response or an already inactive authorization returns `ALREADY_INACTIVE`; no duplicate transition is written; the first revoker and time stay authoritative; concurrent terminal Appointment transitions (cancellation, no-show, expiry) reconcile calmly.
- **Irreversible in Phase 1 (intentional temporary limitation, not an error):** revocation is irreversible for its originating Appointment. There is no Restore action, no Reauthorize action, no undo and no Care Authorization reactivation. If the Guardian later wants to authorize medical access again, a future Appointment establishes a new access context.
- Access consequences:
  - care **not started**: Professional longitudinal medical history is unavailable, and the Clinic cannot start a new Clinical Encounter / clinical record with that Appointment;
  - Encounter **already `ACTIVE`**: Professional longitudinal history is denied immediately; the existing clinical episode keeps its already-approved continuity — its own record read, Draft work, Finalize, Amendment where the continuity rules permit, and **Terminar atención**. Revocation does not abruptly stop care already in progress;
  - Encounter **`CONCLUDED`** with the Care Authorization `REVOKED`: the Appointment Clinical Record is denied to the Professional client and new client Amendments are denied; existing records remain preserved.
- Same-Clinic history: current Phase-1 revocation removes longitudinal access uniformly. The Clinic does **not** keep longitudinal professional access merely because some historical records were originally created by that same Clinic. This is current policy and behaviour. Carried forward as a **future product-policy review**: whether a Clinic should retain access to historical records it originally created after Guardian revocation. Not resolved here.
- Final UX intent (product meaning): the Guardian action uses a secondary / destructive, non-primary style; its confirmation makes explicit that the Appointment and existing records remain; the Clinic loses longitudinal medical-history access, including its own earlier records; if care has not started the Clinic cannot start a clinical record with that Appointment, and if care already started it may finish that episode; the action cannot be undone for that Appointment; a new Appointment is required to authorize access again. No backend terminology is shown.
- Physical validation in DEVELOPMENT (Android; user-confirmed):
  1. a `CONFIRMED` Appointment with active medical access shows **Retirar acceso médico**;
  2. **Volver** changes nothing;
  3. confirming succeeds; the Appointment remains; the state becomes **Acceso médico retirado**;
  4. on the Professional side, after revocation and before clinical care started: no longitudinal history, and the Clinical Record / Encounter cannot be started;
  5. the existing Guardian medical history remains available;
  6. UX polish: the action appears as secondary / destructive rather than primary; the confirmation was readable and understandable on Android; no Restore / Reauthorize action appears after revocation; the Appointment remains visible;
  7. additional exploratory testing behaved correctly.
- Automated-only (not physically reproduced on device; covered by the automated suites): revoke vs Professional history read; revoke vs Draft update; revoke vs Amendment; revoke vs Clinic cancellation; revoke vs Guardian cancellation; revoke vs no-show; revoke vs expiry; simultaneous revocations; stale / foreign target confidentiality cases.

### Clinical Phase 1 core / P0 — DEVELOPMENT closure

**Clinical Phase 1 core / P0: CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT.** This closes the clinical P0/core, not all Clinical Phase 1 work. CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT:

- Minimal Clinical Record (Appointment → Encounter → Draft → Finalize → Terminar atención);
- immutable Finalized Entries;
- Guardian medical history;
- Clinical Amendments;
- Professional longitudinal history;
- cross-Clinic / cross-Organization history;
- Veterinarian provenance;
- Care Authorization access controls;
- Guardian Appointment medical-access revocation.

Clinical P1 / later (open, **not** promoted to P0): Attachments / professional attachment upload; Clinical Intake / walk-in client workflow; normal Care Authorization ending; broader clinical UX polish; future Guardian Veterinarian-name visibility.

Mandatory **pre-Production policy review** (D-CLOSE-4): a past unresolved `CONFIRMED` Appointment can currently leave pre-care longitudinal history access open without a time bound while its authorization is `ACTIVE`. This is in tension with the other access-intent rules (SPEC-ACC-001 BR-18/BR-25, SPEC-MED-005 BR-17) and must be decided before Production clinical use. Not fixed here; any resolution is a separate product decision.

Before-Production security / privacy hardening (open; not Clinical P0 blockers): `view_clinical_record_entry` (authenticated legacy path, internal ids); `get_professional_pet_active_care_authorizations`; `get_guardian_pet_active_care_authorizations`; the stale `CONFIRMED` policy above; the Production migration backlog and its preflights; REQ-NFR-016 account deletion / retention.

Small-clinic-first milestone: the clinical core supports a small Clinic with minimal operational setup — Appointment-based care; any currently authorized Veterinarian of the Clinic can start care; no Appointment Veterinarian Assignment and no Veterinarian Availability required; document care; finalize records; the Guardian sees the finalized history; corrections remain append-only; an authorized Veterinarian can review the longitudinal cross-Clinic history; the Guardian can withdraw an Appointment's medical access. MenizVet is not positioned as an enterprise workforce-management system.

**Production has not been deployed or touched.** `20261106090000_guardian_appointment_medical_access_revocation.sql` remains pending there, with the rest of the backlog. Clinical P0 being CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT does **not** mean Production clinical use is ready.

Next: **post-Clinical-P0 priority review** — a review, not an automatic implementation. Rank, small-clinic-first, across the whole app: new Appointment-request notifications; Opening Hours editor; Service Offerings editor; co-Guardian flow; Attachments; legacy RPC / privacy hardening; the stale `CONFIRMED` policy; NFR-016 deletion / retention; Production preparation.

### Known Appointment Schedule follow-ups

None of the following is part of the completed work above, and none is fixed:

- a `CANCELLED` row does not yet explicitly expose whether the Appointment had ever been confirmed;
- cancellation Communications for a never-confirmed Appointment may have separate schedule-time behavior that still needs its own audit;
- requester and Veterinarian display names are not part of the current schedule contract; no Veterinarian display-identity contract exists, so the professional Appointment detail shows no Veterinarian;
- pagination or date-bounded schedule retrieval remains a later concern for the schedule list itself. The professional Appointment detail and its sub-screens no longer read the whole schedule: in the working tree they read one Appointment through `get_clinic_appointment`, which exists in LOCAL only (see "Professional Appointment Calendar — Phase 1 contracts");
- ~~Clinical Encounter existence is not visible to professional scheduling UI~~ — resolved by Minimal Clinical Record (`has_clinical_encounter`, SPEC-APT-012 BR-48) and validated in DEVELOPMENT: the professional detail no longer offers Cancel, No-show, or a confirmed reschedule once an Encounter exists;
- the Guardian view does not know that an Encounter exists: a Guardian can still be offered Cancel before the confirmed start of an Appointment whose care started early; the backend rejects it calmly and writes nothing. Non-blocking follow-up for the next Appointment-action review — no Guardian read contract was invented for it;
- repair or reassignment of an assigned Veterinarian who is no longer valid or available has no mobile workflow; confirmation only maps the backend rejection to a calm message;
- Veterinarian assignment and reassignment still accept a `REQUESTED` Appointment whose requested start has passed until expiry is materialized (at most one scheduler interval) — a minor coherence follow-up to resolve before any assignment UI ships;
- `get_professional_pet_active_care_authorizations` (and its Guardian counterpart) can list a lapsed request's still-`ACTIVE` authorization until expiry is materialized; access is already denied by the authoritative predicate, and neither read has a current consumer — a cosmetic follow-up;

### Professional Appointment Calendar — Phase 1 contracts (SPEC-APT-012 §14/§15, BR-51–BR-59)

- Status: implemented and tested in **LOCAL only**. Not applied to DEVELOPMENT or Production.
- **Deployment order:** the migration must reach an environment before any app build that contains the phase-2 mobile change below; an older backend would make every professional Appointment detail fail to load.
- Migration `20261108090000_clinic_appointment_read_and_month_counts.sql` (additive; `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` unchanged):
  - `get_clinic_appointment(p_clinic_id uuid, p_appointment_id uuid)` — one Appointment, the schedule row's exact columns, `APPOINTMENT_VIEW_SCHEDULE` for the Clinic, one uniform `42501` `Clinic Appointment unavailable` otherwise (BR-52);
  - `get_clinic_appointment_month_counts(p_clinic_id uuid, p_year integer, p_month integer)` — `(day date, appointment_count integer)` for days with calendar Appointments (`REQUESTED`, `CONFIRMED`, `COMPLETED`, `NO_SHOW`; not `CANCELLED`/`EXPIRED`), dated by the latest Schedule Revision's start in its own establishment timezone; `22023` for an invalid month after authorization (BR-53, BR-54);
  - internal, client-inaccessible helpers `project_clinic_appointments(uuid, uuid[])` (the one row projection) and `clinic_calendar_appointments(uuid, date, date)` (the one calendar-day definition, shared with the day read);
  - index `appointment_schedule_revisions_start_idx` on `scheduled_start_at`: a one-month count over 20 000 Appointments of one Clinic (110 000 overall) fell from ~25–50 ms to ~3–6 ms in a rolled-back local measurement.
- Verification: `supabase/tests/clinic_appointment_calendar_reads_spec_apt_012.sql` (VN 62–68), plus the full local SQL battery.
- Phase 2 (mobile, working tree only): the professional Appointment detail, Clinic reschedule proposal, Clinic alternative time, and the clinical record screens (record, entry, amendment, through `useAppointmentClinicalRecord`) read their Appointment with `getClinicAppointment` (`data/clinic-appointment-schedule.ts`, same strict row parser). The schedule list is the only remaining consumer of `get_clinic_appointment_schedule`. The single read's uniform `42501` is shown as an unavailable Appointment.
- Phase 3B (backend, **LOCAL only**, not applied to DEVELOPMENT or Production; no mobile consumer yet). Migration `20261109090000_clinic_appointment_day_pending_history.sql` (additive; every earlier read unchanged):
  - `get_clinic_appointment_day(p_clinic_id uuid, p_day date)` — the calendar Appointments of one Clinic-local date as BR-51 rows, `(start, id)` order, no pagination; same membership definition as the month counts (BR-56);
  - `get_clinic_appointment_pending(p_clinic_id, p_after_rank, p_after_start_at, p_after_id, p_limit default 50, max 100)` — BR-51 rows plus `pending_reason` / `pending_category` / `pending_rank` and the whole classification's `action_required_count` / `awaiting_guardian_count` / `informational_count` on every row (an empty later page carries none; the summary does), classified for the acting User's real capabilities (and the `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT` Entitlement where the operation needs it) **before** the keyset cursor and limit are applied; proposal actionability and feasibility are evaluated only where they can change that actor's result; only the page is projected (BR-57);
  - `get_clinic_appointment_pending_summary(p_clinic_id)` — `action_required_count` (main), `awaiting_guardian_count`, `informational_count`, from the same classification (BR-58);
  - `get_clinic_appointment_history(p_clinic_id, p_before_start_at, p_before_id, p_limit default 30, max 50)` — terminal Appointments, keyset on `(start, id)` descending (BR-59);
  - internal `clinic_pending_appointments(uuid, uuid)` (the one classification) and partial index `appointments_active_clinic_idx` on `(clinic_id)` for `REQUESTED`/`CONFIRMED`.
  - Rolled-back local measurements (Clinic with 100 000 terminal + 3 000 open Appointments, 233 000 overall): day read ~1.6 ms; History ~11 ms first page, ~2 ms deep pages; Pending page or summary ~90 ms for a Clinic manager with 100 actionable proposals, ~30 ms for a 300-open Clinic. Because the first page carries the totals, opening Pending is one classification instead of two (~175 → ~90 ms). A Veterinarian, for whom proposals never matter, stays at ~15–20 ms whatever the number of proposals. For managers, Pending cost still grows by ~0.5 ms per actionable PENDING proposal (the existing proposer-authority helper) — ~570 ms per call in a stress case with 1 000 open proposals in one Clinic; not addressed.
  - Verification: `supabase/tests/clinic_appointment_day_pending_history_spec_apt_012.sql` (VN 69–79, including an equivalence oracle against the previous classification), plus the full local SQL battery.
  - Mobile integration requirement — the detail must not suggest Confirm, Propose a new time or Propose another time without `APPOINTMENT_MANAGEMENT` — addressed by phase 4C below.
- Calendar context (BR-60): migration `20261110090000_clinic_calendar_context.sql` (`get_clinic_calendar_context`), verified by `supabase/tests/clinic_calendar_context_spec_apt_012.sql` (VN 80–82); applied to DEVELOPMENT together with `20261108090000` and `20261109090000`.
- Phase 4A (mobile, committed after Android visual review): the Clinic's "Agenda de citas" now opens `professional/clinics/[clinicId]/calendar` — a Monday-first month grid (`react-native-calendars` 1.1314.0, JS-only, no native rebuild; only its `Calendar` grid is imported, with our own header, weekday row and day cells) showing each date's count (BR-54), the selected date's Appointments as compact rows (BR-56, the schedule's strict row parser), and the Clinic's today from BR-60 (none, and nothing auto-selected, when the Clinic has no valid timezone). One read per visible month and per selected day, cached per focus, sequenced against late answers; the context, visible month and selected day are re-read on focus and on returning to the foreground. It never reads the full schedule.
  - The Appointment detail's back fallback targets the calendar.
- Phase 4B (mobile, working tree only, pending Android visual review): the agenda has three tabs under its header — Calendario (initial), Pendientes, Historial — each with its own reads and state, kept across tab switches.
  - Pending reads `get_clinic_appointment_pending` (pages of 50) and History `get_clinic_appointment_history` (pages of 30), only once their tab is first shown, with "Cargar más" on the complete keyset cursor sent back exactly as returned; one read at a time, no repeated Appointment, a new generation on every restart so late answers are ignored. A focus refreshes only the tab on screen from its first page; the other tabs refresh when shown again. A failed refresh or "load more" keeps the rows; 42501 from any tab replaces the agenda.
  - Pending groups rows under the backend's categories, in its order, with the whole classification's totals from the rows (an empty first page = zero; an empty later page keeps the previous totals). `get_clinic_appointment_pending_summary` is not read: no counter outside Pending exists yet. Reasons are worded as situations, never as operations.
  - Removed: the transitional `schedule.tsx` and its card, `partitionClinicSchedule`, and the client function for `get_clinic_appointment_schedule` (the RPC itself is unchanged for installed versions). No mobile code reads the full schedule any more.
- Phase 4C (mobile, working tree only, pending Android functional review): SPEC-APT-012 BR-61. The professional Appointment detail, `reschedule.tsx` and `alternative-time.tsx` read `get_my_professional_context()` in parallel with `get_my_professional_clinics()` on every focus and relate the Clinic to its Organization by `organizationId` (`lib/appointment-management.ts`: available / unavailable / unknown). Confirm (including an accepted alternative), Propose a new time and Propose another time are offered only when eligible by their existing `offers*` rule AND the Entitlement is available (`managedActionGate`); one short note explains a held-back action — "not available" when reported missing, "could not be checked" with a retry when unknown (never claimed missing) — and nothing is said when the action is not eligible anyway. A proposal screen reached directly shows that state instead of its form and reads no candidates. Cancel, Complete, No-show and Withdraw are unchanged; Pending's classification is unchanged. No SQL or backend change.

### Guardian "My appointments" with tabs and pagination — Phase 1 (SPEC-APT-011 §13/§14, Punto 9)

- Backend: **applied to DEVELOPMENT** (`eqvlydfflfvltprkwcxx`) after the user's approval and verified there (remote history at `20261117090000`; both reads authenticated-only, the projection executable by no client role; per-User results equal to the Appointments of their currently guarded Pets; Appointments, Pets, Guardianships, Care Authorizations, schedule revisions, proposals, lifecycle events and every other public function unchanged). ### Professional Appointment management — current mobile coverage

The basic professional Appointment surface now includes:

- schedule viewing, grouped into Upcoming / Needs resolution / History, with navigation-only cards;
- a professional Appointment detail screen, the single action surface;
- stale-safe confirmation of future `REQUESTED` Appointments;
- stale-safe Clinic cancellation of future `REQUESTED` Appointments and of `CONFIRMED` Appointments before or after their start (after the start as a less prominent action beside Complete and No-show), with the recorded cancellation reason shown on the professional detail (SPEC-APT-012 BR-47);
- the Guardian cancellation cutoff at the confirmed start (SPEC-APT-003 BR-31) with the unresolved-past Guardian presentation (SPEC-APT-011 BR-34) — migrations applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user and the full Android flow physically validated; Production untouched (see its section);
- Guardian request notes shown on the professional detail when present, and no "not assigned yet" Veterinarian copy (Veterinarian Assignment stays optional);
- stale-safe NO_SHOW resolution of eligible past `CONFIRMED` Appointments;
- stale-safe Clinic proposal of a new time for a future `CONFIRMED` Appointment, Clinic withdrawal of that proposal, and Guardian acceptance (SPEC-APT-005, SPEC-APT-006, SPEC-APT-016, SPEC-APT-017) — migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user and the core Android workflow physically validated; some edge cases remain physically unverified (see its section). It now runs on the normalized Reschedule Proposal model, re-validated physically in DEVELOPMENT after that migration;
- stale-safe Clinic proposal of another time for a future `REQUESTED` Appointment, Clinic withdrawal of it, Guardian acceptance, and subsequent Clinic confirmation of the accepted time (SPEC-APT-018, SPEC-APT-019, SPEC-APT-020) — `communications-dispatch` deployed and the migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user, and the core Android workflow physically validated; push delivery remains physically unverified (see its section);
- stale-safe lightweight completion of eligible past `CONFIRMED` Appointments without Veterinarian Assignment, Clinical Encounter, or clinical report (SPEC-APT-009 Migration 1) — migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user and the core Android workflow physically validated; two-session staleness and Encounter-existing-before-Complete remain automated-only (see its section);
- Clinic Appointment hours — standard duration, weekly Appointment availability, and one-day exceptions (SPEC-APT-013 BR-72–BR-75) — migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user and the core Android workflow physically validated; weekly saves are now stale-protected, also physically validated on Android in DEVELOPMENT; Production untouched (see their sections);
- the unified Standard Appointment Duration rule across `REQUESTED` and `CONFIRMED` proposals and candidates (SPEC-APT-013 BR-68) — migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user and the core `CONFIRMED` reschedule Android flow physically validated; Production untouched (see its section);
- derived current feasibility of a pending proposal on the professional detail (generic "no longer available" note, Withdraw kept) and Guardian current acceptability deciding the Guardian's Accept (SPEC-APT-005 BR-34/BR-35) — migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user and the REQUESTED and CONFIRMED core flows physically validated on Android; Production untouched (see its section).

Several approved Appointment-management workflows still lack their professional mobile workflows. These are follow-ups, not bugs:

- Guardian-originated proposal UI and its Clinic acceptance UI (the backend supports both; mobile shows a Guardian proposal read-only);
- assign Veterinarian to Appointment (SPEC-APT-007);
- reassign Veterinarian (SPEC-APT-008);
- Clinical Encounter mobile workflow (SPEC-MED-001) — delivered by Minimal Clinical Record (start, Entries, finalize, mark care as finished) and CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT; Production untouched;
- Veterinarian display identity;
- Clinical Encounter visibility in professional scheduling UI — delivered (`has_clinical_encounter`) and validated in DEVELOPMENT; Production untouched;
- a single-Appointment backend read and schedule pagination.

The assignment/reassignment coherence follow-up after logical `REQUESTED` expiry, listed above, still applies before any Veterinarian assignment UI ships.

Deliberately deferred from the confirmed-reschedule slice: Guardian-originated proposal UI and Clinic acceptance UI; Guardian rejection; Guardian withdrawal; counterproposals; history of never-accepted proposals; withdrawal Communications and withdrawal reasons; Veterinarian reassignment suggestions; Clinic-side notifications; a professional business scheduling horizon (by design there is none; candidates are paged per 60-date request); a multi-session concurrency harness (races are covered by serialized single-session cases, as in earlier slices). The historical-duration diagnostic, normalization question, and SPEC-APT-017 §4 historical-duration limitation listed here earlier are superseded by the unified Standard Appointment Duration rule (see its section).

### Known unresolved policy

A `CONFIRMED` Appointment that is never explicitly resolved remains an active commitment, and its Appointment-linked Care Authorization can remain active indefinitely. This is not solved. NO_SHOW is never automatic: it is recorded only by an explicit, authorized professional action, and no lateness threshold exists. Approved classification (D-CLOSE-4): not a DEVELOPMENT closure blocker; a **mandatory policy review before Production clinical use**, because pre-care professional longitudinal history (SPEC-MED-005 BR-40) stays available while such an Appointment's authorization is `ACTIVE`. A Guardian can end that access through **Retirar acceso médico** (CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT), and the Clinic can resolve the Appointment.


## Immediate next step

Slices 1–16 are closed. `docs/RELATIONAL_MODEL.md` §11 enumerates conceptual migration dependencies only through Slice 15 and does not yet describe Slice 16; no further slice is defined there or anywhere else in the approved repository. There is currently no approved next migration slice.

The Professional Context and Appointment streams are in DEVELOPMENT only. DEVELOPMENT now supports:

- Guardian Appointment request → professional visibility → stale-safe confirmation → Guardian sees `CONFIRMED`;
- a `REQUESTED` Appointment not confirmed before its time → logically expires → is materialized as `EXPIRED` by the scheduler → appears as historical → with no expiry Communication;
- rejection of a second future pending request for the same Pet at the same Clinic;
- a past `CONFIRMED` Appointment → appears under "Por resolver / Needs resolution" → stale-safe professional NO_SHOW → `NO_SHOW` in History;
- navigation-only schedule cards → professional Appointment detail, which offers Confirm and Cancel for a future `REQUESTED` Appointment, Cancel for a future `CONFIRMED` one, and Mark as completed and Mark as no-show for a past `CONFIRMED` one → a real Clinic-side cancellation of a future Appointment from the detail → `CANCELLED` and read-only, in History, and `CANCELLED` in the Guardian view, where the Guardian Appointment detail shows the Clinic-entered cancellation reason separately from the reason for visit;
- with the confirmed-reschedule migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: a Clinic proposes a new time for a future `CONFIRMED` Appointment → the confirmed time stays active and both sides see the pending proposal → the Guardian accepts and the same Appointment stays `CONFIRMED` at the new time, or the Clinic withdraws it and may propose again. The core Android workflow is physically validated; push navigation, two-session staleness, real lost-response timing, and an Encounter during a pending proposal are not physically verified;
- with `communications-dispatch` deployed and the REQUESTED alternative-time migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: a Clinic proposes another time for a future `REQUESTED` Appointment → the request stays `REQUESTED` at the Guardian's time and both sides see the pending alternative → the Guardian accepts and the request stays `REQUESTED` at the accepted time, awaiting Clinic confirmation → the Clinic confirms it as `CONFIRMED`; or the Clinic withdraws it and may propose again; or a Guardian reschedule or a Clinic confirmation discards it. The core Android workflow and the email are physically validated; push delivery and navigation are not physically verified;
- with the lightweight completion migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: a past `CONFIRMED` Appointment → "Marcar como completada / Mark as completed" (beside No-show) → `COMPLETED` with no Veterinarian Assignment, Clinical Encounter, or clinical report → History for the Professional, `COMPLETED` for the Guardian, and the `APPOINTMENT_COMPLETED` Communication. The core Android workflow is physically validated; two-session staleness and Encounter-existing-before-Complete are covered by automated tests only;
- with the Clinic Appointment Hours migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: an eligible professional or Receptionist opens "Horario de citas / Appointment hours" → edits the standard duration, the weekly hours, and one-day exceptions (OPEN, CLOSED, custom hours, removal) without SQL → the authoritative configuration is re-read and Guardian candidate times reflect it, while existing confirmed intervals are unchanged. The core Android workflow, including Back/navigation without Fabric errors, is physically validated;
- with the unified Standard Appointment Duration migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: a Clinic changes the standard duration → new `CONFIRMED` reschedule candidates and proposals use it → a pending proposal keeps its frozen interval after a later change → Guardian acceptance establishes that frozen interval while earlier history is unchanged. The core Android workflow is physically validated; the no-standard-duration case is covered by automated tests only;
- with the current-feasibility migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: a pending Clinic proposal (Alternative Time or confirmed reschedule) whose time becomes unavailable stays visible and `PENDING` → the Guardian sees no Accept and a generic "can't be accepted right now" message, the Clinic sees a "no longer available" note and keeps Withdraw → when the time becomes available again, Accept returns and the same proposal can be accepted. The REQUESTED and CONFIRMED core Android flows are physically validated; Entitlement-driven acceptability, standard-duration independence, withdrawal while infeasible, and notification behaviour are covered by automated tests;
- with the weekly-revision migration applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: a weekly Appointment hours save prepared from an outdated week is rejected with nothing written → the editor loads the latest week and says so → a new edit from it saves. Physically validated on Android.
- with `20261020090000`, `20261021090000`, and `20261022090000` applied to DEVELOPMENT by the user: a Guardian can cancel a `CONFIRMED` Appointment only before its confirmed start (backend time authoritative) → after the start the Appointment stays `CONFIRMED` under "Activas / Active" with the unresolved-past note and no Cancel, and an already-open panel submits to the backend and reconciles after the cutoff rejection → the Clinic resolves it as Complete, No-show, or a reasoned Clinic cancellation whose reason both sides can see. Client roles can no longer establish Organizations. The full Android flow is physically validated; the checkpoint is CLOSED.

No part of these streams has been applied to Production, and they still need a deliberate Production rollout, including the pending-duplicate diagnostic before the expiry scheduler is enabled there.

Its **post-Phase-1 UX/context polish** (selected-Organization Professional Clinics, timezone selected-state feedback, Clinic settings visual hierarchy) is also CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT after the user's Android validation. The project can continue to the next prioritized product area; further visual refinement can be revisited as the overall product grows. **Staff access lifecycle Phase 1 is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (Slices A, B1, B2 and C; see its section). **Clinic creation self-service Phase 1 — Add Clinic** (SPEC-CLI-001 §10), the last routine SQL-only step for an existing Organization, is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT (see its section). Organization establishment remains trusted Commercial Onboarding (ADR-009). **Subscription Operational Safety — Phase 1** is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT (see its section). **Trusted Organization Establishment Hardening** (the atomic, idempotent ADR-009 Organization + Owner + initial Subscription operation; not commercial onboarding UI or billing) is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT (see its section): an operator establishes a customer with one trusted, retry-safe call and the Owner continues setup in the app.

**Staff Responsibility Editing + Veterinarian Enablement — Phase 1 is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (see its section): Responsibilities are managed without SQL, `VETERINARIAN` can be invited and enabled, a Veterinarian creates their own Professional Profile, and an Administrator assigns Clinics. Production has not been deployed or touched.

**Minimal Clinical Record — Phase 1 is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (see its section): the migration is applied there and the key Android workflow is physically validated — a Veterinarian starts the Appointment's Encounter, documents it in several Entries, finalizes them so the Guardian sees them, and marks care as finished; Production has not been deployed or touched. **Clinical Phase 1 remains OPEN**: the clinical-completeness work after it was **Amendments UI** (since CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT, see below) and **professional longitudinal/cross-Clinic history** (since CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT, see below). The scope originally planned for the minimal slice was:

- a clinical state/action in the Professional Appointment detail;
- Start Encounter;
- create/edit a Draft clinical entry: category + text + optional event time;
- Finalize;
- Conclude Encounter;
- expose Encounter existence where needed so incompatible Appointment actions are not offered;
- calm mobile error handling;
- the resulting finalized history visible to the Guardian through the already-existing history read/UI.

Excluded from the first Minimal Clinical Record slice unless a new audit proves otherwise: Appointment Veterinarian Assignment; Veterinarian Availability; clinical attachments; Profile editing; credential verification; full EMR / specialized structured diagnosis/treatment models. Amendments and professional longitudinal history remain subsequent Phase-1 clinical completeness work unless the minimal slice's Specifications already require them.

**Clinical Amendments — Phase 1 is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (see its section): `20261104090000_clinical_amendments.sql` is applied there and the key Amendment workflow is physically validated on Android; Production has not been deployed or touched. Clinical Phase 1 remains OPEN; next: **Professional longitudinal / cross-Clinic history + Veterinarian provenance identity**; attachments remain P1/deferred.

**Professional Longitudinal / Cross-Clinic Medical History + Veterinarian Provenance — Phase 1 is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (see its section): `20261105090000_professional_longitudinal_history.sql` is applied there, its ACLs were physically checked, and the key Professional history workflow is physically validated on Android; Production has not been deployed or touched. **Clinical Phase 1 remains OPEN pending the final P0 scope/closure review** (to decide: Clinical Intake / walk-in scope, Guardian Care Authorization revocation UI, normal Care Authorization ending / unresolved past `CONFIRMED` Appointments, any other P0, and the overall Phase-1 closure criteria). Attachments remain P1/deferred; Guardian Veterinarian-name exposure remains separate/deferred.

**Clinical Phase 1 P0 closure review — done** (decisions D-CLOSE-1…5 recorded in their section). Its only remaining P0 slice, **Guardian Appointment Medical Access Revocation — Phase 1**, is **CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (migration applied, ACLs checked, Android workflow and UX polish physically validated), and **Clinical Phase 1 core / P0 is CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT** (see its section). Clinical P1 / later items (attachments upload, Clinical Intake client workflow, normal Care Authorization ending, clinical UX polish, Guardian Veterinarian identity) and the D-CLOSE-4 pre-Production policy review stay open. Production has not been deployed or touched.

Roadmap: CLOSED — Staff access lifecycle; Staff Responsibility Editing + Veterinarian Enablement; Minimal Clinical Record (in DEVELOPMENT); Clinical Amendments (in DEVELOPMENT); Professional Longitudinal / Cross-Clinic Medical History + Veterinarian Provenance (in DEVELOPMENT); Clinical Phase 1 P0 closure review; Guardian Appointment Medical Access Revocation (in DEVELOPMENT); **Clinical Phase 1 core / P0 (in DEVELOPMENT)**. NEXT — **post-Clinical-P0 priority review** (a review, not an automatic implementation; candidates listed in the Clinical core / P0 closure section). Commercial onboarding is not reopened. Recorded follow-ups also include Opening Hours and Service Offerings editors. Production has not been deployed or touched.

Separately, the remaining major approved professional Appointment workflows for the detail architecture are:

- Veterinarian assignment and reassignment (SPEC-APT-007, SPEC-APT-008), after the logical-expiry coherence follow-up above;
- Clinical Encounter mobile workflow — delivered by Minimal Clinical Record and CLOSED in DEVELOPMENT. Post-completion Encounter establishment (SPEC-MED-001 BR-34, Migration 2) is validated in DEVELOPMENT as a backend checkpoint, and its device validation (retrospective documentation of a COMPLETED Appointment) is part of the physically validated Minimal Clinical Record workflow; Production untouched. Lightweight Complete (SPEC-APT-009 Migration 1) is closed: implemented, applied to DEVELOPMENT, and physically validated for its core Android workflow; Production untouched.

They need not all be implemented now, and no implementation order is committed to yet. The professional Appointment detail screen is the intended action container: future Appointment operations should be added there rather than re-introducing action buttons into schedule cards.


## Validation policy

The migration-validation flow is:

```text
physical design review
→ SQL implementation
→ static audit
→ deploy to development Supabase
→ deterministic runtime validation
→ update operational checkpoint
→ commit
→ close Slice
```

Runtime validation should prefer deterministic SQL and rollback-only synthetic
fixtures; those validation paths must verify that retained synthetic state is
zero. Removable linked setup state must also be cleaned. A live-provider test
that necessarily commits history protected by an approved append-only model is
not subject to a blanket zero-retained-state criterion; the applicable
domain-specific operational procedure must instead minimize, isolate, identify,
terminalize or allow finite expiry of, and account for that evidence. Harness or
fixture errors are not production migration defects; approved migration SQL
should be reopened only for a concrete reproducible defect.

Deferred does not mean verified. Genuine multi-session concurrency scenarios may be deferred to a future integration-test harness when a reasonably sized deterministic harness cannot verify them, provided deterministic final-state integrity and lock ordering have been validated.

Deferred integration-test coverage currently includes:

### Slice 9

- Subscription transition versus paid mutation;
- Clinic Assignment ending versus Appointment Assignment establishment;
- simultaneous cross-Clinic confirmed scheduling for one Professional Profile;
- Guardianship ending versus Guardian mutation.

### Slice 10

- Appointment request versus Guardianship ending;
- Appointment request versus Entitlement transition;
- concurrent Guardian/Clinic Appointment cancellation;
- Care Authorization revocation versus Appointment cancellation;
- Clinic responsibility/capability loss versus Clinic cancellation.

Slice 10 also did not repeat the confirmed-professional conflict-release scenario as a live runtime fixture because doing so required disproportionate Veterinarian-assignment
setup. The underlying conflict model was runtime-validated in Slice 9, and Slice 10 confirmed statically that CANCELLED Appointments no longer satisfy the CONFIRMED-commitment predicate.

### Slice 12

- Care Authorization revocation versus Clinical Encounter creation;
- Appointment cancellation versus Clinical Encounter creation;
- Appointment no-show versus Clinical Encounter creation;
- professional longitudinal-history read versus Care Authorization revocation;
- Guardian longitudinal-history read versus Guardianship ending;
- Draft update versus professional-authority loss;
- concurrent Amendment submissions using the same idempotency key with different payloads.

### Slice 13

- simultaneous fifth/sixth Attachment initiation;
- professional authority ending versus Attachment initiation;
- Care Authorization revocation versus Attachment initiation;
- Attachment validation versus Draft removal across Edge requests;
- signed upload-token issuance versus immediate Attachment removal;
- cleanup versus delayed upload completion.

## Source-of-truth and handoff rule

Repository Requirements, Specifications, ADRs, the current relational model, and migrations are authoritative. This file records operational progress. Agent prompts, responses, and chat history are not authoritative; a fresh session must inspect the repository rather than reconstruct architecture from memory.
